[[sep-dv-functional-coverage]]
== SEP OCAH Open-Source Functional Coverage Plan

=== Overview

This plan covers **basic legal operating modes** of every SEP IP the OSS suite
already walks. It is the functional-coverage contract for the cocotb flow.

Verilator does not compile SystemVerilog `covergroup`. Phase 1 bins are VCS
covergroups in `hw/sys/sep/dv/cov/sv/sep_fcov.sv` (`ifndef VERILATOR`),
sampled by the single passive `u_sep_fcov` instance in `tb/tb_top.sv`. There
is no Verilator cover-property layer.

Feature checkers stay on the VPLAN traceability matrix. The FCOV number is the URG
Group report on `sep_uvm_top.u_sep_fcov`, not the DUT line/cond/toggle/FSM
SCORE.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Field |Value
|DUT |`sep_wrapper`
|Reference specification |`hw/sys/sep/doc/`
|Reference VPLAN |`SEP_VPLAN.adoc`
|Open-source framework |PyUVM on cocotb
|Coverage policy |`cov/config/vcs/coverage_policy.toml` (the URG exclusion lists) and `cov/config/vcs/sep_cov_scope.hier` (the VCS compile-time scope)
|Open-source simulator |Verilator: checker evidence only; it compiles no covergroup
|Commercial simulator |VCS: the Phase 1 covergroups and the graded code coverage
|===

=== Coverage Goals

[cols=",,",options="header"]
|===
|Metric |Contract |Collection path
|Phase 1 functional coverage |Every Phase 1 bin below |URG Group report on `sep_uvm_top.u_sep_fcov`, from a `--cov --tool vcs` run
|Phase 2 functional coverage |Every executable bin of <<sep-fcov-planned>>: the coverage of the Phase 3 tests of <<sep-dv-verification-plan,SEP_VPLAN>> |The Phase 2 covergroups of `cov/sv/sep_fcov.sv`, from a `--cov --tool vcs` run; credit rule in <<sep-fcov-planned>>
|Line / expression coverage (Verilator) |Collected; not a functional-coverage contract |`[coverage.verilator]` (`--coverage-line`, `--coverage-expr`)
|Line / condition / toggle / FSM / branch / assertion |Code coverage, no target (<<sep-fcov-closure>>) |`--cov --tool vcs` (`[coverage.vcs]`)
|===

The VCS code-coverage number -- line, condition, toggle, FSM and branch over
the scope of `cov/config/vcs/sep_cov_scope.hier` -- has no agreed score target,
and no policy file sets a floor on it: `cov/config/vcs/coverage_policy.toml`
carries no `pass:[[[thresholds]]]` row. The runner reports the raw
and the effective figure of each family, and <<sep-fcov-closure>> states how a
hole closes, who reviews an exclusion and what reopens it.

Do not mix line coverage with the Phase 1 bins: the line population on this
DUT would mask them.

==== Two-phase scope

[cols="1,3",options="header"]
|===
|Phase |Scope
|**Phase 1** |One basic-mode covergroup per SEP IP. Bins are the
legal cells a named `all`-group test already walks.
|**Phase 2** |The coverage of the Phase 3 tests of <<sep-dv-verification-plan,SEP_VPLAN>>: dense enumerations, crosses, window edges, error and fault paths, illegal encodings. Named in <<sep-fcov-planned>>, with its credit rule.
|===

=== Coverage Summary

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|IP / path |Phase 1 covergroup
|AXI fabric decode |`sep_axi_decode_cg`
|SMN inbound port |`sep_smn_inbound_cg`
|SRAM |`sep_sram_access_cg`
|Boot ROM |`sep_boot_rom_cg`
|CPU boot |`sep_cpu_boot_cg`
|AES |`sep_aes_mode_cg`
|HMAC |`sep_hmac_mode_cg`
|KMAC |`sep_kmac_mode_cg`
|OTBN |`sep_otbn_execute_cg`
|Adams Bridge |`sep_abr_keygen_cg`, `sep_abr_sign_cg`, `sep_abr_mlkem_cg`
|ESRC / DRBG / EDN |`sep_esrc_edn_flow_cg`
|Key Manager |`sep_km_generate_cg`, `sep_km_command_sideload_cg`, `sep_km_host_cmd_cg`, `sep_km_wipe_cg`, `sep_km_sw_reset_cg`
|Crypto isolate / reset |`sep_crypto_isolate_cg`
|Secure DMA |`sep_dma_copy_hash_cg`
|SPI host |`sep_spi_flash_cg`
|Inbound filter |`sep_inbound_filter_allow_cg`
|eFuse |`sep_efuse_sense_cg`
|eFuse fail-closed |`sep_efuse_fail_closed_cg`
|Lifecycle |`sep_lc_state_cg`, `sep_feat_ctrl_cg`, `sep_sec_dis_boot_cg`
|Mailbox / PIC |`sep_mailbox_pic_cg`, `sep_mailbox_channel_cg`
|WDT |`sep_wdt_bark_cg`
|Warm / cold scratch |`sep_scratch_reset_cg`
|eFuse program / lock |`sep_efuse_program_lock_cg`
|Lifecycle transition |`sep_lc_transition_cg`
|Lifecycle demotion |`sep_lc_demote_cg`
|Secure DMA completion route |`sep_dma_completion_route_cg`
|Seed-dependent stimulus values |`sep_spi_host_rand_cg`, `sep_mbox_rand_cg`, `sep_dma_rand_cg` (see <<sep-fcov-rand>>). Phase 2: the coverpoints marked seed-dependent in their area sections, graded on a merge of regressions and not on one regression (see <<sep-fcov-rand-phase2>>)
|===

The deterministic Phase 1 covergroups and the seed-dependent `*_rand_cg`
groups are compiled and sampled for VCS only (`ifndef VERILATOR`). Several IPs carry
more than one group, because their Phase 1 contract has independent halves that different tests reach: the Key
Manager splits generate from consume; lifecycle splits the sensed state, the
transition taken, the `FEAT_CTRL` profile, demotion, and the SEC_DIS window
while sensing is still open; eFuse splits sense from
the program/lock matrix; and the DMA splits the transfer from which completion
route reported it. <<sep-fcov-planned>> lists the Phase 2 names.

=== Coverage Definitions

Each row is one coverpoint of the named covergroup in
`cov/sv/sep_fcov.sv`. A row states what the sampler must SEE, which is not
always the whole feature contract: where the contract needs a golden, the row
says which checker keeps it. Do not read a hit as the checker's claim.

==== `sep_axi_decode_cg`

*Description*: The LSU bus decodes a live CSR and returns OKAY.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_reset_value` |An OKAY read of `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR` returns its generated reset value
|`cp_wr_rdback` |A whole-word CSR write, then an OKAY read of that address returning the written word
|`cp_resp_wr` / `cp_resp_rd` |An OKAY write response; an OKAY read response (separate coverpoints, so a write-only or read-only run cannot fill both)
|`cp_block` |An OKAY read in each LSU-reachable register block of `sep_reg.svh`: `SEP_CPU_CTRL`, `SECURE_DMA`, `WDT_TIMER`, the cold and warm scratch banks, `SEP_RESET_CTRL`, OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, CSRNG, EDN, `ENTROPY_SOURCE`, `SEP_LIFECYCLE_CTRL`, the KM mailbox, the eFuse shadow map, `EFUSE_INTERFACE_CTRL`, `EFUSE_MMR`, ABR, the entropy pool, the AXI-Lite mailboxes, and the alias, AP remap, STEE remap, outbound filter and inbound filter banks, and the SPI host. SRAM and the boot ROM have their own groups. The PIC is inside the VeeR core and `SEP_EXTERNAL` is the outbound port, so neither has a cell. TRNG has no cell because no `all` leaf makes an OKAY read of it
|`cp_aw_w_order` |Which VALID the master raised first for a write that then completed OKAY: `aw_first`, `w_first`, `same_cycle`. Taken at the first cycle either VALID is high with no write outstanding
|`cp_ar_during_write` |A read address accepted while a write has its address accepted and no data beat yet
|`cp_wstrb` |Byte lanes of a completed write: 1, 2, 4, 8
|`cp_rd_depth` |Reads in flight when a read is accepted, this one included: 1, 2-4, 5 or more
|`cp_arsize` |`ARSIZE` of a completed read: 1, 2, 4, 8 bytes
|===

`cp_block` records decode, not a value: the reset-value and RW checks stay on
`sep_address_map_test` and `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`.

==== `sep_smn_inbound_cg`

*Description*: Reads in flight on the SMN-inbound port (`m_axi_*`).

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_rd_depth` |Reads in flight when an `m_axi` read is accepted, this one included: 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5 to 16
|===

The count restarts when `sep_reset_n` asserts (<<sep-fcov-collection>>). The
read-data and ID-ordering claims stay on `sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test`.

==== `sep_sram_access_cg`

*Description*: The 64-bit SRAM port accepts a write and returns the same data.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_write` |An OKAY write inside the SRAM aperture
|`cp_match` |An OKAY read of that address returning the full 64-bit word written
|===

==== `sep_boot_rom_cg`

*Description*: The CPU fetches from the Boot ROM, and the ROM answers a data read.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_ifu` |A retired PC inside the Boot ROM aperture
|`cp_lsu` |An OKAY data read of the Boot ROM aperture
|===

The word-matches-the-loaded-image claim stays on the VPLAN checker
(`sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test`); the sampler sees the access, not the image.
`sep_axi_map_refuse_test` also reads the ROM as the OKAY control for its
refused rows. That is a real ROM data read, so it counts.

==== `sep_cpu_boot_cg`

*Description*: VeeR EL2 boots firmware and reports PASS.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_console` |A firmware console-byte strobe
|`cp_pass` |Firmware completion with PASS
|`cp_pc` |A retired PC that is non-zero
|===

`cp_pass` is sampled in the time step the outbound-mailbox model raises
`fw_done` with PASS, not on a clock. The cocotb boot loop reads `fw_done` on the
next rising edge and ends the run there, so a clock-sampled strobe races the end
of the simulation and would be lost on almost every firmware leaf. VCS does not
allow an explicit `sample()` on a group with a clocking event, so this group is
sampled by function: every clock for `cp_console` / `cp_pc`, and once at the
PASS edge.

The expected banner text and the distinct-PC count stay on the boot
scoreboard. A covergroup counts hits, not distinct values.

==== `sep_aes_mode_cg`

*Description*: AES legal mode x key-size x operation. The suite walks 15 cells.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_mode` |ECB, CBC, CTR
|`cp_key` |128, 192, 256
|`cp_op` |ENC, DEC
|`cp_sideload` |SW key (`KEY_SHARE` CSRs) and KM sideload key (`CTRL_SHADOWED.sideload`)
|`x_mode_key_op` |15 cells: the 9 ENC cells plus the ECB/CBC decrypt legs of the round-trip. CTR x DECRYPT is excluded, see below. A cell is sampled when that configuration reached `STATUS.output_valid` and its `DATA_OUT_0` word was read
|===

CTR x DECRYPT is an `ignore_bins` in the cross. CTR is a stream mode: the
engine runs the forward cipher whichever way `OPERATION` is programmed, so a
test that programmed DECRYPT there would pass with the field disconnected.
No cipher contract stands behind that cell. The scoreable neighbour is
`OPERATION`'s shadowed-register behaviour, which is mode-independent and
belongs on a CSR vehicle. `cp_op` and the decrypt cells are owned by
`sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test` CHK-CELL (the engine round trip);
`cp_sideload.km_key` by `sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test` and
`sep_km_command_set_rand_test`. The ciphertext-equals-golden and
round-trip-equals-plaintext claims stay on those tests. The GCM phases are
Phase 2.

==== `sep_hmac_mode_cg`

*Description*: HMAC SHA-2 variants the suite walks. A cell is sampled when
`INTR_STATE.hmac_done` is observed after a `CMD.hash_process` on that CFG.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_digest` |SHA2-256, SHA2-384, SHA2-512
|`cp_key_length` |Keyed only: 128, 256, 384, 512, 1024
|`cp_plain` |Plain SHA (`hmac_en` = 0): the three digest sizes
|`x_digest_key` |The 14 legal keyed cells. SHA-256 with a 1024-bit key is illegal in `hmac.sv` and is an `ignore_bins`, not an empty cell
|===

The digest-equals-golden claim stays on `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`.

==== `sep_kmac_mode_cg`

*Description*: KMAC-family modes the suite walks. A cell is sampled when
`INTR_STATE.kmac_done` is observed, or the first `STATE` share is read, after a
`CMD` process on that CFG. The `keyed_mac` path that the KM sideload KAT takes
polls `STATUS` and reads the shares, and never reads `INTR_STATE`, so an
`INTR_STATE`-only anchor could not sample a sideloaded operation.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_sha3` |L224, L256, L384, L512
|`cp_shake` |L128, L256
|`cp_cshake` |L128, L256
|`cp_kmac` |L128, L256. Samples `CFG_SHADOWED.mode` = cSHAKE with `kmac_en` = 1 after a completed process. That is the programmed configuration, not an SP800-185 digest proof. `SepKmacCfg.cfg_word()` and `keyed_mac` both write that word. `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test` walks five keyed cells (L128 / L256 × key 128 / 192 / 256 / 384 / 512) against `kmac_family_words`, an SP800-185 golden that self-tests at import. The KM sideload KAT is an engine cross-check and has no digest golden.
|`cp_sideload` |Keyed KMAC only: SW key (`KEY_SHARE` CSRs) and KM sideload key (`CFG_SHADOWED.sideload`)
|`cp_kmac_key_length` |Key 128, 192, 256, 384, 512 from the `KEY_LEN` register. Both drivers write `KEY_LEN` before `CFG_SHADOWED` (`CFG_REGWEN` locks at Start) and the register holds its value across a CFG write, so the sampler does not invalidate it there
|===

The digest-equals-golden claim stays on `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`.
A `keyed_mac` hit fills `cp_kmac` as configuration only.

==== `sep_otbn_execute_cg`

*Description*: OTBN runs a compute program to IDLE.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_cmd` |An `EXECUTE` write to `CMD`
|`cp_done` |ONE bin: the same post-`EXECUTE` window saw `STATUS` back at IDLE and then `ERR_BITS` == 0
|`cp_imem_rdback` / `cp_dmem_rdback` |The last whole-word write to IMEM (or DMEM), then an OKAY read of that address returning it
|===

`cp_done` is one bin. Two independent strobes would let a stale
`ERR_BITS` zero score alongside a program that never ran. Secure wipe and
`LOCKED` are Phase 2.

==== `sep_abr_keygen_cg`

*Description*: Adams Bridge ML-DSA keygen completes.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_op` |`MLDSA_CTRL.CTRL` = KEYGEN. The command is compared on the `CTRL` field mask from `sep_reg.svh` only, so a write that also sets `ZEROIZE` (bit 3) still scores
|`cp_done` |`MLDSA_STATUS.VALID` after that KEYGEN -- the bit the KAT polls, not "STATUS non-zero". It arms only on an observed `VALID == 0` read after the KEYGEN write, the same as the sign and verify bins, so a dropped KEYGEN cannot take the previous operation's sticky VALID
|===

The public-key-equals-KAT claim stays on `sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test`.

==== `sep_abr_sign_cg`

*Description*: Adams Bridge ML-DSA signing and verification complete.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_op` |`MLDSA_CTRL.CTRL` = SIGNING, and `MLDSA_CTRL.CTRL` = VERIFYING, on the `CTRL` field mask only
|`cp_sign_done` / `cp_verify_done` |`MLDSA_STATUS.VALID` after that command.
Separate coverpoints, not one concatenated cell: the pending flags are
independent, so a command that never completes would otherwise drop a later
command's genuine completion into no bin. Each arms only on an observed
`VALID == 0` read after its `MLDSA_CTRL` write, so the sticky VALID of a
previous operation cannot be credited to a command the engine dropped
|===

KEYGEN, SIGNING and VERIFYING are separate `MLDSA_CTRL.CTRL` encodings with
separate owning leaves, so they are separate groups: one group would let the
keygen leaf report a sign cell. The
signature-equals-ACVP and verify-accept-vs-reject claims stay on
`sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test`. KEYGEN_SIGN (0x4) is not covered.

==== `sep_abr_mlkem_cg`

*Description*: Adams Bridge ML-KEM-1024 commands complete.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_op` |`MLKEM_CTRL` = KEYGEN, ENCAPS, and DECAPS
|`cp_keygen_done` / `cp_encaps_done` / `cp_decaps_done` |`MLKEM_STATUS.VALID`
after that command, one coverpoint each for the same reason as the ML-DSA pair,
and each armed on an observed `VALID == 0` read after its `MLKEM_CTRL` write.
`MLKEM_CTRL` is writable only while `STATUS.READY` is set, so a command issued to
a busy engine is dropped silently and the arm is what stops the stale VALID being
read as its completion
|===

ML-KEM is a separate register block from ML-DSA in the same aperture, with its
own `MLKEM_CTRL` and `MLKEM_STATUS`, so this is a separate group rather than
more bins on `sep_abr_sign_cg`: no ML-DSA command can reach an ML-KEM cell and
the owning leaves stay distinct. The value claims -- ek/dk, ciphertext and
shared key against the ACVP vectors, and the implicit-rejection key for a
modified ciphertext -- stay on `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`. `KEYGEN+DECAPS` (0x4)
is not covered.

==== `sep_esrc_edn_flow_cg`

*Description*: Entropy leaves ESRC, passes DRBG, and a sink consumes it.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_seed` |Seed presented to CSRNG
|`cp_gen` |A CTR_DRBG genbits-valid beat
|`cp_crypto` |A crypto-leg sink handshake (`tvalid && tready`)
|`cp_km` |A KM sink handshake
|`cp_edn_aes` / `cp_edn_kmac` / `cp_edn_otbn_rnd` / `cp_edn_otbn_urnd` |Which `drbg_axis_edn_adapter` client took the post-adapter grant: `aes`, `kmac`, `otbn_rnd`, `otbn_urnd`. `cp_crypto` above samples the shared AXIS stream ahead of the fan-out, so it is hit by any sink and cannot show that a given client was served.
|`cp_pool_pop` |An OKAY read of the entropy-pool `DATA` register
|`cp_ht_sel` |An `HT_WATERMARK_NUM` write of each supported `WATERMARK_TEST` encoding (REPCNT_HI, APT_HI, APT_LO, MARKOV_HI, MARKOV_LO), and of an unsupported value (5-15, one bin)
|===

`cp_ht_sel` records the selector walk. The armed-value-per-mode claim stays on
`sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test`. Which unsupported value is used is seeded,
so the range is one bin. FIPS lock and alerts are Phase 2.

==== `sep_km_generate_cg`

*Description*: The mailbox generates a key.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_generate` |A decoded frame whose header is `RESP_CMD`, echoing `CMD_KEY_GENERATE` with rc 0 and a non-null handle
|===

The frame is decoded from the mailbox `SEP_READ_DATA` word sequence, so the
bin needs the KM's own answer. Silence cannot score it.

==== `sep_km_command_sideload_cg`

*Description*: A generated key is transferred to each consumer.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_dest` |HMAC, KMAC, AES, OTBN, ABR ML-DSA seed, ABR ML-KEM seed D, ABR ML-KEM seed Z, ABR ML-KEM message -- one cell each for all eight destinations the KM firmware decodes, taken from `RETURN_ARG` dest_engine of a successful `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER`
|===

The consumer's use of the sideloaded key (the KAT digest) stays on the four
`sep_km_*_sideload_kat_test` checkers for HMAC/KMAC/AES/OTBN, and on
`sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test` for the ABR ML-DSA seed and
`sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test` for the three ML-KEM cells. Those leaves are
the MUST-fill owners for consume. `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` also writes one
seed-selected classic dest and a directed ABR dest every seed. The sampler
scores `RETURN_ARG` dest_engine when `RESP_CMD` echoes `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER`
with rc 0. A refused dest cannot fill a cell. A raw `WRITE_DATA` fill
cannot. One seed of that leaf fills one classic cell; the four sideload
KATs are the MUST-fill owners.

==== `sep_km_host_cmd_cg`

*Description*: SEP-visible mailbox command IDs that are not generate or transfer.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_cmd` |`CMD_HW_VER`, `CMD_ROM_VER`, `CMD_SRAM_VER`, `CMD_RECOV_ACK`, `CMD_EXEC_ROM`, `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC`, `CMD_SRAM_EXEC`, `CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD`
|===

Sampled on the `RESP_CMD` echoed command ID when the latched return code is 0.
`CMD_SRAM_VER` scores its defined failure code. A refused command cannot fill
a success bin. A raw inbound `WRITE_DATA` fill cannot score.

The command-frame tracker stays in step with the KM ROM. After a
`CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` success the SEP streams `FW_WORDS` raw image words and a
CRC-32C trailer outside the framing
(`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC`), so the tracker
counts those words off instead of parsing them as headers. A `SW_RESET_N`
write that holds the KM in reset restarts the tracker, because the ROM
restarts its own frame state there.

==== `sep_km_wipe_cg`

*Description*: A write of `KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state` with the byte strobe that
carries bit 0.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_wipe` |`wipe_state`: an OKAY write with bit 0 set
|===

==== `sep_km_sw_reset_cg`

*Description*: A write of `SW_RESET_N` that releases the KM bit, with the byte
strobe that carries bit 0.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_rel` |`km_released`: `KM_SW_RST_N` goes 0 to 1. A write that sets the bit while the KM is already released does not score
|===

The sampler tracks `KM_SW_RST_N` from `SW_RESET_N` writes and reads, from its
reset value. `SW_RESET_N` resets with `sep_reset_n`, which the sampler follows
only for the inbound counts, so after a warm reset the tracker can be stale at
1 and miss one release. It cannot invent one.

[[sep-fcov-isolate]]
==== `sep_crypto_isolate_cg`

*Description*: The per-IP isolate-then-reset sequencing for the HMAC and Adams Bridge domains.
An accelerator reset waits on both the SEP host path and the Key Manager path,
so the ordering bin reads both completion bits.

Every bin is an EDGE, not a state. Both isolate bits stay high for the whole
reset window, so a state bin would fill on any run that parks HMAC at all --
including one where the reset preceded the drain, which is the thing the
ordering bin exists to catch.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_km_iso` |The cycle the `km_hmac` path reports isolated while the HMAC isolate request is high. The host-side isolation entries never present a transaction on this path; that a KM master path reaches isolation under the HMAC request is what this bin records.
|`cp_rst_ordered` |The cycle the HMAC gated reset falls under the HMAC isolate request, with both the host and Key Manager isolate bits high and the KM bit raised by that request (`cp_km_iso`). A reset that fell before either bit does not fill this bin.
|`cp_abr_rst_ordered` |The cycle the Adams Bridge gated reset falls under the ABR isolate request, with both isolate bits high and the host bit (a full-AXI isolate) raised by that request. The leaves that reset ABR hold the KM in reset, so `km_abr` is already high and only the host term is ordered; see below.
|`cp_reopen` |The cycle `km_hmac` reports un-isolated again after a `cp_km_iso` edge, so the path was released rather than left isolated for the rest of the run.
|===

The KM-path bits are also raised by a Key Manager reset: `sep_reset_ctrl.sv`
ORs the KM isolate request into `km_hmac` and `km_abr`. A leaf that holds the KM
in reset therefore sees them high from time 0, and a KM hold or release on
`SW_RESET_N` moves them with no HMAC or ABR isolate at all. So every KM-path
edge counts only while the domain's own isolate request is high, and an
ordered reset counts only when the KM bit rose under that request: a KM bit
that was already high cannot show that the sequencer waited for it. The
request also excludes a warm reset, because `sep_reset_n` forces the gated
resets without the sequencer and leaves the request low.

The ABR Key Manager term (`km_abr` raised by an ABR request with the KM
released) has no leaf: `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test` holds the KM
at its reset default and grades the host term only (`CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ORDER`). No
bin records it. A bin needs a leaf that resets ABR with the KM released.

==== `sep_dma_copy_hash_cg`

*Description*: DMA copies memory and computes a hash.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_copy` |`CONTROL.GO` with opcode COPY
|`cp_hash` |`CONTROL.GO` with opcode SHA256
|`cp_hash_opcode` |Which inline-hash opcode was commanded on a `CONTROL.GO`: `sha256`, `sha384`. `cp_hash` above is SHA-256 only, so it does not distinguish a SHA-384 transfer. SHA-512 is a legal opcode (`OpcSha512`) that no leaf commands, so it has no bin rather than a permanent hole.
|`cp_copy_done` |Completion after a COPY GO
|`cp_hash_done` |Completion after a SHA256 GO
|`cp_chunk` |`STATUS.chunk_done` observed while a GO'd transfer is outstanding
|`cp_rego` |A GO with `INITIAL_TRANSFER` = 0, i.e. a continuation chunk
|===

`cp_chunk` and `cp_rego` record the DMA's normal one-chunk-per-GO protocol:
the engine raises `CHUNK_DONE` and drops BUSY, and firmware W1Cs it and re-GOes
until the final chunk reports DONE (`fw/drivers/sep_dma.h`,
`dma_basic_test.c`). That loop is why one transfer produces many GO writes, and
without these two bins it hides inside `cp_copy`. They do not claim the Phase 2
`sep_dma_transfer_cg` enumeration -- chunk sizes, endpoints and range configs
stay there.

Completion is `STATUS.done` OR the DMA-done interrupt
(`sep_internal_interrupts[8]`, driven by `intr_dma_done` in `sep.sv`), because the suite's firmware uses
both routes: `dma_basic_test` polls `STATUS`, while `dma_hash_test` enables
`INTR_ENABLE.DMA_DONE`, WFIs, and its handler clears `STATUS.done` before
software reads it -- so on that path no `STATUS` read with the DONE bit ever
reaches the bus. A `STATUS`-only bin therefore cannot fill `cp_hash_done`.
The GO'd opcode still labels the cell, so an interrupt cannot score the
wrong one.

The destination-matches-source and digest-matches-golden claims stay in the
DMA firmware. These cells are driven only by the firmware tests
(`sep_dma_basic_test`, `sep_dma_hash_test`, `sep_dma_cpu_contention_test`, and
the SPI DMA leaves `sep_spi_ot_dma_rx_test` / `_tx_test`, which GO a COPY),
which is why the sampler reads the internal LSU bus: on the flat TB ports they
could not fire at all. Opcode and range errors are Phase 2.

==== `sep_spi_flash_cg`

*Description*: The OpenTitan SPI host talks to a flash model.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_csr` |A `COMMAND` write that the host acted on: chip select asserts after it
|`cp_frame` |Chip select asserted on the SPI pads
|`cp_sck` |A shift-clock rising edge inside an asserted frame
|`cp_hs` |`CONTROL.GO` with the DMA hardware handshake enabled
|`cp_opcode` |The first byte of a frame on `sd[0]`, MSB first, on the SCK rising edges: JEDEC ID `0x9F`, WREN `0x06`, WRDI `0x04`, RDSR `0x05`, RDSR2 `0x35`, PAGE PROGRAM `0x02`, READ `0x03`, FAST READ `0x0B`, SECTOR ERASE `0x20`
|===

`cp_csr` is not any access in the SPI window: register walks
(`sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`, `sep_axi_order_sweep_test` and the unmapped and
dead-space walks) reach that window too, and they do not start a segment.

The JEDEC ID value, the SRAM pattern, and the SPI error status stay on the SPI
tests. `cp_frame` and `cp_sck` are on the pads: CSR traffic alone
cannot prove a frame left the host.

==== `sep_inbound_filter_allow_cg`

*Description*: One legal inbound transaction is allowed.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_allow_wr` |An `m_axi` write OKAY whose address is inside the programmed enabled window
|`cp_allow_rd` |An `m_axi` read OKAY inside that window
|===

The window is decoded from the `FILTER_CONFIG` / `START_ADDR` / `END_ADDR`
writes on the CSR bus. `START_ADDR` and `END_ADDR` hold a 56-bit address in two
32-bit halves, and the compare uses all 56 bits of the `m_axi` address. A write
OKAY counts only when the entry's `FILTER_CONFIG.WRITE_ALLOWED` is set, and a
read OKAY only with `READ_ALLOWED`: an OKAY in a direction the entry refuses
came from another entry or the default policy. The compare is against the
PROGRAMMED range, not its 4 KB page:
`traffic_filter.sv` compares `addr[.:12]` only in its allow_burst arm, and
`addr[.:DATA_BUS_WIDTH_LOG2]` otherwise, so a page compare is a superset of the
real grant. Comparing the range can only miss a page-widened grant, never
invent one. Both bins are also gated on exactly one outstanding inbound
transaction, like the CSR-side bins -- `sep_axi_order_sweep_m_axi_test` runs
several at once, and a last-write latch would pair one access's response with
another's address.

The window is committed as ONE entry: each half of START and END is latched
with its entry index, and they are accepted only at the enabling
`FILTER_CONFIG` write of that same entry. Independent last-writes would otherwise assemble a window out
of one entry's START, another's END and a third's ENABLE -- a window the DUT
never held. The entry count comes from
`sep_pkg::InboundFilterNumFilters`, not a literal.

The full rule matrix, the deny path, and the winner walk over all 48 entries
(inbound `InboundFilterNumFilters` = 16 plus outbound
`OutboundFilterNumFilters` = 32) are Phase 2.

==== `sep_efuse_sense_cg`

*Description*: Real fuse sense completes.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_done` |A RISING edge of `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`, on a run that did NOT pass `+skip_fuse_sense`
|`cp_episode` |First sense after cold-reset release, and a later re-sense
|===

The episode split separates a re-sense after programming a fuse from the
bring-up sense.
The producers are `sep_efuse_otp_program_seq` and `sep_lcc_demote_matrix_seq`;
the scratch leaf re-senses but passes `+skip_fuse_sense`, which disqualifies
the sense bin, so it cannot fill this one.

Both qualifiers are load-bearing. `+skip_fuse_sense` makes
`efuse_shadow_regs.sv` force `fuse_sense_done` to 1 one cycle after reset with
no sense FSM and no macro read, so an ungated bin scores the simulation
bypass on every test that skips sense.
The edge matters because the held level would score one hit per clock for the
rest of the run.

The sensed-words-match-the-staged-image claim stays on the shadow checker
(`write_efuse_image` and the stitch tests). Program × lock and set-only walks
are Phase 2.

==== `sep_efuse_program_lock_cg`

*Description*: A spare eFuse field is programmed while unlocked, and refused
once its write-lock is set.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_field` |SPARE0..SPARE8
|`cp_lock` |The target's write-lock state when the program was issued: unlocked, locked. A spare counts as locked once a program of its lock bit completed with `PROGRAM_DONE` and a clear `PROGRAM_STATUS`; a refused lock program leaves it unlocked
|`x_field_lock` |18 cells
|`cp_outcome` |Sampled on the first `PROGRAM_CTRL` read with `PROGRAM_DONE` set after the GO: programmed (`PROGRAM_STATUS` clear) and refused (`PROGRAM_STATUS` set)
|===

`PROGRAM_STATUS` is the outcome only on a read that also shows `PROGRAM_DONE`.
GO clears DONE but not STATUS, and both update together when the program ends
(`efuse_program_interface.sv`), so a STATUS read while the program is busy
still holds the previous program's outcome.

Programming a write-locked field is a legal software action with a specified
outcome, not a fault injection -- the same class as writing a read-only
register -- so it is Phase 1 and not one of the Phase 2 error groups.

All 18 cross cells are filled by a SINGLE seed:
`sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test` walks unlocked-program, then lock, then
reject on each of SPARE0..SPARE8 in order, and only the bit offset inside a
spare is seed-selected. Walking every spare is what makes the slot numbering
falsifiable -- a lock bit wired to the wrong slot would otherwise be caught only
on the seed that happened to draw that spare.

The outcome is a separate coverpoint rather than a third cross dimension: a
`field x lock x outcome` cross would declare 18 cells that only a broken DUT
could fill, and the program-succeeds / lock-refuses contract belongs to the
test's checker.

Everything is decoded frontdoor from `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL` -- `EFUSE_ADDR` plus
`PROGRAM_GO` on the write, `PROGRAM_DONE` / `PROGRAM_STATUS` on the readback --
and the spare index comes from the generated `SEP_EFUSE_MAP_SPARE*` offsets, not
a literal. The spare count comes from the generated `LOCKS_SPARE` reserved-field
shift.

==== `sep_efuse_fail_closed_cg`

*Description*: An eFuse read or program FSM recovers from an illegal state
encoding.

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_illegal` |`2'b00`, `2'b11` -- the encodings `efuse_read_interface` and `efuse_program_interface` declare unreachable. The legal `2'b01` / `2'b10` land in no bin, so ordinary traffic scores nothing
|`cp_fsm` |`read_if`, `program_if` (`program` is a SystemVerilog keyword)
|`x_fsm_illegal` |Four cells. `sep_efuse_illegal_state_fail_closed_test` walks the product in one seed
|===

The two illegal encodings are separate cells: they fail the legal-set
test for different reasons, so a recovery written as a comparison against one
value would fill one cell and leave the other dead. The cells record that the
injection matrix was walked, not that the recovery was correct -- that stays on
`CHK-READ-FAILCLOSED` and `CHK-PROGRAM-FAILCLOSED`. Because the DUT cannot reach
these encodings, a hit records testbench injection by construction.

==== `sep_lc_state_cg`

*Description*: A legal lifecycle state is sensed.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_lc` |TEST_DEV, PROD, RMA_SIP_0, RMA_SIP_1, RMA_CHIP_0, RMA_CHIP_1, PROD_END
|===

A W1S write with a token match moves PROD straight to `RMA_SIP_1` /
`RMA_CHIP_1` (`sep_lcc_golden.lc_state_next`), so a `_0` code is read frontdoor
only when a leaf is staged in it. `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_rma_sip_test`
(`+lc_start=2`) and `_rma_chiplet_test` (`+lc_start=6`) stage RMA_SIP_0 and
RMA_CHIP_0 and read `LC_STATE` frontdoor in their `CHK-START-SENSED` cell, so
every cell is filled by a single seed. `sep_base_test.select_efuse_image()`
also stages a seeded legal code for a leaf that does not pin `lc_raw`; that
adds hits, not cells.

The bin is sampled from an OKAY read of the `LC_STATE` shadow word only when
the word carries the differential `{~raw, raw}` encoding, so a stuck-at or
half-sensed word lands in no bin instead of scoring one.

==== `sep_lc_transition_cg`

*Description*: A documented lifecycle transition is taken.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_transition` |Two bins over the packed `{from, to}` pair: PROD to RMA_SIP_1, RMA_SIP_1 to RMA_CHIP_1
|===

`sep_lc_state_cg` records which states were SEEN; this group records which were
TAKEN. A pair is recovered from two `LC_STATE` reads in the same leaf --
`sep_lc_shadow_write_seq` reads ONCE per cell, after its write, so the pair is
two consecutive cells of one leaf, not a before/after pair around one write.
The contributors are `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_test`, `_prod_demote_test`
and `_rma_sip_test`.

One packed coverpoint over `{from, to}`. A `cross` of two 7-bin coverpoints
auto-generates the full product; named bins inside the cross do not suppress
those cells.

Only two bins are declared. A pair needs two `LC_STATE` reads inside ONE leaf,
and the `+lc_start` leaves are sensed at PROD, RMA_SIP_0, RMA_CHIP_0 or
PROD_END -- none at TEST_DEV -- so TEST_DEV to PROD has no producer; the
PROD_END/transient leaf advances `0x8` to `0xA`, which is not a named pair, so
it produces nothing here. TEST_DEV to PROD_END and TEST_DEV to RMA_SIP have no
walk either, and PROD to PROD_END is impossible under write-1-to-set at all:
`0x1 | 0x8` is `0x9`. The transition-legality claim stays on
`sep_lcc_golden.lc_state_next`.

==== `sep_dma_completion_route_cg`

*Description*: Which route reported a DMA completion, and in which transfer mode.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_route` |`STATUS.done` poll, DMA-done interrupt
|`cp_mode` |Normal, hardware handshake
|`x_route_mode` |3 cells; interrupt x handshake is an `ignore_bins` because the SPI DMA firmware that uses handshake mode does not complete through the ISR
|===

The DMA reports completion two ways and the suite walks both: `dma_basic_test`
polls `STATUS`, while `dma_hash_test` takes the `DMA_DONE` interrupt whose
handler clears `STATUS.done` before software reads it. `sep_dma_copy_hash_cg`
accepts either route so its cells can fill; this group keeps the distinction
that would otherwise be hidden. Handshake mode comes from
`sep_spi_ot_dma_rx_test` / `_tx_test`.

==== `sep_feat_ctrl_cg`

*Description*: The decoded debug profile `FEAT_CTRL` produced, per sensed
lifecycle state and per override.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_state` |The seven legal raw encodings, taken from the SENSED shadow value
|`cp_debug` |none / partial / full over the five defined debug bits: `DBG_1` `sep_debug`, `chiplet_dbg`, `sep_fuse_dbg`, `smc_fuse_dbg` and `DBG_2` `sip_debug`
|`cp_sec_dis` / `x_sec_dis_debug` |SEC_DIS off/on crossed with the profile; `on` can only pair with `full`, so the other two products are `ignore_bins`
|`cp_secure_tm` |The latched TEST_EN strap, both polarities
|===

Sampled when the second 32-bit half of `FEAT_CTRL` is read, so the profile is a
complete 64-bit value, and only after a real fuse sense completed: the shadow
reads 0x0, which is the TEST_DEV encoding, until sensing is done, and a
`+skip_fuse_sense` run never senses at all. `sep_lc_demote_cg` takes the same
sample.

There is no state x debug cross: the decode fixes one legal profile per
lifecycle state, plus the SEC_DIS all-ones override, so most of the 21 products
are illegal and would be permanent holes. Naming the legal set means restating
`sep_lcc_golden.feat_ctrl_expected`, which belongs to the checker.

`SECURE_TM` is a standalone coverpoint and NOT crossed with the profile: it does
not qualify feature control (`env/sep_lcc_golden.py`). The `FEAT_CTRL` layout is
`DBG_1[23:0]`, `DBG_2[47:24]`, `Function[63:48]`, with no test group to cross
against.

The state comes from the sensed shadow probe rather than a frontdoor
`LC_STATE` read, because the probe is the state the decode used at the time of
the `FEAT_CTRL` read. A frontdoor read of `LC_STATE` is a separate access that
can come before or after it.

The decode-matches-golden claim stays on `sep_lcc_golden`. Token faults and the
`sigint` fail-closed matrix are Phase 2.

==== `sep_sec_dis_boot_cg`

*Description*: SEC_DIS while fuse sensing is still open. A match leaves
`sep_reset_n` held. The `sep_reset_n` override releases it, and clearing the
override holds it again. The `LC_STATE` shadow-map read in that window is
recorded by its AXI response.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_hold` |SEC_DIS rises while sensing is still open, `sep_cpu_reset_n` held, override clear
|`cp_release` |Override select and value set, sensing still open, `sep_cpu_reset_n` rises
|`cp_rehold` |Override clear after that release, sensing still open, `sep_cpu_reset_n` falls
|`cp_alone` |That release while SEC_DIS is still off
|`cp_map_resp` |`okay` on the `LC_STATE` shadow read while sensing is still open and SEC_DIS is on. A refused read is an error path (Phase 2), so it has no cell
|`cp_reach` |OKAY read of `SW_RESET_N` while sensing is still open and `sep_cpu_reset_n` is released
|===

`sep_sec_dis_override_test` owns `cp_hold`, the match while the reset stays
held, and `cp_map_resp`, the `LC_STATE` response in that window. `okay` fills
when that read returns AXI OKAY. The leaf also reads that word after a
`rst_ni` pulse with the token kept, while SEC_DIS is still active. The same leaf walks `cp_release`, `cp_rehold`,
and `cp_alone` from the override pins. `sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test` walks
those three from the IC_RESET TDR and fills `cp_reach`. Both probes of the
reset pair stay on the checker; this group samples `sep_cpu_reset_n` only.

==== `sep_lc_demote_cg`

*Description*: Volatile PROD debug demotion.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_state` |TEST_DEV, PROD, PROD_END, and the RMA states as one bin
|`cp_demote_1` / `cp_demote_2` |clear, set (from the differential `{~demote, demote}` probes)
|`x_demote` |4 cells
|===

Only the demote pair is crossed. Crossing all seven states would declare 28
cells for a walk that exercises the PROD rows only
(`sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test`).

==== `sep_mailbox_pic_cg`

*Description*: A mailbox interrupt is raised and cleared, per source domain.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_raise` / `cp_clear` |Rising and falling edge of any SEP AXI mailbox source, `sep_internal_interrupts[7:0]`, driven from `inbound_interrupt_o` in `sep.sv`
|`cp_km_raise` / `cp_km_clear` |Rising and falling edge of the KM mailbox, `sep_internal_interrupts[14]`, after software wrote a non-zero `SEP_IRQ_ENABLE`. A leaf that never enables a KM mailbox interrupt does not exercise this path, so its edges do not count. `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test` writes `SEP_IRQ_ENABLE` = 0x15 and then 0 in its storage walk; the inbound-space status is set at idle, so the line really falls, and on some seeds (the walk order is seeded) that leaf fills `cp_km_clear` too
|`cp_peer_fill` |A completed external-master write to the inbound mailbox `WRITE_DATA`. The SEP CPU can raise the same inbound source itself, so an IRQ edge alone does not show an external master drove the receive direction. Both 4-byte halves alias to the register, so the address compare masks bit 2.
|===

The two domains are separate coverpoints because they are separate sources with
separate owners; one shared pair would let a KM-mailbox edge stand in for the
SEP mailbox.

The EL2 PIC claim stays on the CPU test. `cp_raise` / `cp_clear` are one edge
of the OR of the eight sources, so a channel stuck at 0 still fills them;
`sep_mailbox_channel_cg` names the channel. The 42-source exclusivity map is
Phase 2.

==== `sep_mailbox_channel_cg`

*Description*: Each SEP AXI mailbox interrupt is raised and cleared on its own
line.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_ch` |Channel 0-7, the bit of `sep_internal_interrupts[7:0]`
|`cp_edge` |raise, clear
|`x_ch_edge` |16 cells
|===

`sep_mailbox_plic_test` runs one inbound channel at a time and claims it as PIC
source `ch + 1`, so it fills all 16 cells in one seed. Sampled once per edge
per channel, so two channels that move in the same cycle each score.

==== `sep_wdt_bark_cg`

*Description*: Watchdog bark reaches NMI.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_thold` |A `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` write that the watchdog acted on: the bark interrupt rises after it
|`cp_bark` |An OKAY read of the WDT `INTR_STATE` with `wdog_timer_bark` set
|`cp_nmi` |A retired exception flagged as an interrupt while the bark interrupt, which is the CPU NMI input, is high
|===

`cp_nmi` reads the bark interrupt itself (`intr_wdog_timer_bark`, the net
`sep.sv` connects to the CPU `nmi_int_i`), so it does not depend on when
firmware reads `INTR_STATE`. The firmware reads it inside the NMI handler,
after the trap, so a bin that required the read first could never see the NMI
that the bark caused. An unrelated interrupt with the bark low does not score.
`cp_thold` needs a bark after the write, because a register walk writes the
threshold with the watchdog disabled. A run whose WDT never barks leaves these
cells empty. Bite, wakeup cause, and
`REGWEN` lock are Phase 2.

==== `sep_scratch_reset_cg`

*Description*: Warm scratch clears on warm reset; cold scratch retains.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins
|`cp_warm_wr` |An OKAY write in the warm bank, scored at the warm reset that follows it
|`cp_cold_wr` |An OKAY whole-word write in the cold bank, scored at the warm reset that follows it
|`cp_warm_rst` |A falling edge of `sep_cpu_reset_n`, after the reset release has settled and after a scratch value was staged
|`cp_warm_rd` |A warm-bank read returning 0 AFTER that reset, with a warm write before it
|`cp_cold_rd` |A cold-bank read returning the value written before that reset
|===

The two write bins are scored at the warm reset, not at the write: many bus
leaves use scratch as plain storage and never reset it, and those writes say
nothing about retention. The two read bins are sampled after the reset edge, not on it: a bin ANDed
with the falling edge can never fire, because no read completes in that cycle.

`cp_warm_rst` carries two guards for the same reason. `sep_cpu_reset_n`
(`sep_reset_n & wdt_rst_ni`) also falls during cold bring-up, so a bare edge
term scores in any test and, worse, arms the
two retention bins off an edge the scenario never produced. Requiring a
settled release and a staged scratch value makes the bin the scenario's warm
reset.

[[sep-fcov-rand]]
==== Seed-dependent groups

These groups record a value that a RANDCFG leaf draws from its seed, so one
regression can leave a cell empty, and a merge of the VDBs of later regressions
closes it. Their names end in `_rand_cg`, so the report separates them from the
deterministic groups. A deterministic group must be full in one VCS `all
--cov`; a `_rand_cg` group is graded on a merge of regressions. Each Phase 1
`_rand_cg` group below has two bins, so one regression (three seeds of the owner) leaves a bin
empty with a probability of about 4 %. The two-bin figure and the 4 % estimate
apply to these Phase 1 groups only. `sep_mbox_rand_cg` and
`sep_spi_host_rand_cg` are the exceptions: each owner programs one value from
each bin on every seed, so one run fills both.

[cols="1,1,2",options="header"]
|===
|Covergroup |Owner |Coverpoint and bins
|`sep_spi_host_rand_cg` |`sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test` |`cp_tx_wm`: `CONTROL.TX_WATERMARK` as written, `low` 2-4 and `high` 5-8. `SepSpiHostCfg` draws one watermark from each half on every seed, so one run fills both bins. `sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test` writes a fixed watermark in `low`
|`sep_mbox_rand_cg` |`sep_axil_mailbox_iface_rand_test` |`cp_wirqt`: a `WIRQT` value, scored at the next `WRITE_DATA` push to the same mailbox, `low` 1-3 and `high` 4-7. `SepMboxCfg.per_half` draws one threshold from each half on every seed, so one run fills both bins. A register walk that writes `WIRQT` and never pushes after it does not score
|`sep_dma_rand_cg` |`sep_dma_basic_test` |`cp_len`: `TOTAL_DATA_SIZE`, scored at the COPY `CONTROL.GO` that uses it, 16 and 32 (the copy length the test draws). A register walk writes the size but never issues GO. `sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test` also copies 32 bytes. Phase 2 adds the seed-dependent `cp_len` bins `b64` to `b4096` and `cp_irq_x_enable` (<<sep-fcov-p2-spi-dma>>)
|===

[[sep-fcov-rand-phase2]]
===== Seed-dependent coverpoints of the Phase 2 groups

The Phase 3 tests draw their costly cells from the seed (<<sep-vplan-phase3-random,SEP_VPLAN>>), so the
same rule applies to the Phase 2 groups (<<sep-fcov-planned>>). A coverpoint
that its area section marks *seed-dependent* (in its status cell, or in the
status of its group) is graded on a merge of regressions and not on one
regression. This holds also when the coverpoint is in a group that is
otherwise deterministic, and also for the Phase 2 groups whose names end in
`_rand_cg`. Where the mark names some bins only, only those bins are graded on
a merge. Every other bin and coverpoint of a Phase 2 group must fill in one VCS
`all --cov` regression. Where a seed-dependent coverpoint has bins that fill on
every seed, the area section names them. No claim says that one regression fills a
seed-dependent bin. The SPI and DMA area writes the mark as the status
`executable, seed-dependent` or `parked: <item>, seed-dependent` (<<sep-fcov-spi-dma-status>>), and the eFuse and Key Manager areas end the class cell in `; seed-dependent`.

[cols="2,6",options="header"]
|===
|Area |Coverpoints
|<<sep-fcov-p2-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>> |`cp_detune_lane`, `cp_window_class`, `x_gen_latch`, `cp_alert_threshold`, `cp_active_sinks`, `x_op_protocol` (the re-arm and single-step cells), `cp_verify_outcome` (the reason-class bins)
|<<sep-fcov-p2-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>> |`cp_smc_cfg_class_dir`, `sil_out_on_smc_route`, `sil_smc_outside_aperture` (the above-top bin)
|<<sep-fcov-p2-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>> |`cp_winner`, `cp_nmi_target` (the trampoline-copy bins), `x_case_taken` (cases b to d), `cp_vec_unchanged` (cases b and c), `cp_level_val` (the encodings 2 to 13), `x_level_class_order` (the interior cells), `cp_thresh_val` (the values 1, 6, 9 and other), `x_op_offset`
|<<sep-fcov-p2-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>> |`cp_len` (bins `b64` to `b4096`), `cp_irq_x_enable`, `cp_len_x_kind`, `cp_csaat_x_dir`, `cp_clkdiv_x_speed`, `cp_mode8`, `cp_mode_x_dir`, `cp_clkdiv_class`, `cp_csn_class`, `cp_cpha_edge`, `cp_stall_x_speed`, `cp_clear_form`, `cp_mode_x_width`, `cp_mode_x_chunkcount`, `cp_wrap_independence`, `cp_abort_phase`, `cp_inrange_pos_x_side`, `cp_msg_len_class`
|<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>> |`cp_rst_release_sync`, `cp_aon_release_sync`, `cp_otf_reset_x_path`, `cp_independence_pair`
|<<sep-fcov-p2-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>> |Every coverpoint of `sep_efuse_field_value_cg`, `sep_lc_feat_ctrl_profile_cg`, `sep_lc_feature_bit_semantics_cg`, `sep_dtp_gating_ladder_cg`, `sep_lc_sigint_failclosed_cg`, `sep_lc_illegal_transition_cg`, `sep_efuse_lock_policy_cg`, `sep_lc_demotion_cg` and `sep_lc_demote_lock_cg`; `x_ch_kind_copy` and `x_ch_retry` of `sep_token_fault_cg`; `cp_match_phase`, `x_verdict_out` (the Match and Mismatch cells) and `x_state_demote_forced` of `sep_sec_dis_activation_cg`; `cp_stops_sense` (the two phase bins) and `x_state_strap_feat` of `sep_sec_dis_robustness_cg`; `cp_state`, `x_state_class_op` and `cp_gate_independent_of_feat` of `sep_efuse_jtag_policy_cg`
|<<sep-fcov-p2-key-manager,Key Manager>> |`cp_csr_isr_winner`, `cp_if_parity`, `cp_otp_read_through`, `cp_km_size_vs_width`, `cp_ex_mode_x_target`, `cp_smp_count_good`, `cp_fr_unrec_code`, `cp_fr_isr_disposition`, `cp_transfer_integrity`
|===

===== Random factors and their bins

Every randomized stimulus factor of an `all` leaf, with the bin that records
it or the reason it has none. A value that only changes data or an address
inside a fixed contract has no bin: the checker reads it back, and a bin would
count values, not behaviour.

[cols="2,2,3",options="header"]
|===
|Leaf |Factor the seed picks |Bin, or why none
|`sep_km_command_set_rand_test` |Extra transfer destination |`sep_km_command_sideload_cg.cp_dest`
|`sep_km_command_set_rand_test` |Refused destination, two undefined command IDs |None: refusals are error paths (Phase 2 `sep_km_cmd_reject_cg`)
|`sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test` |One TX watermark in 2-4 and one in 5-8, their order, fill depths |`sep_spi_host_rand_cg.cp_tx_wm`, both bins on every seed; the fill depth only has to pass the watermark, which the checker grades
|`sep_axil_mailbox_iface_rand_test` |One WIRQT in 1-3 and one in 4-7, their order, first fill lengths |`sep_mbox_rand_cg.cp_wirqt`, both bins on every seed; the fill length only has to cross WIRQT, which the checker grades
|`sep_dma_basic_test` |Copy length 16 or 32 |`sep_dma_rand_cg.cp_len`
|`sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test` |Unsupported selector 5-15 |`sep_esrc_edn_flow_cg.cp_ht_sel.unsupported` records that one was written. One draw per regression, so a bin per value would stay mostly empty
|`sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test` |AP or STEE bank, region, outbound entry |None: `sep_fabric_outbound_remap_filter_walk_test` walks every region of both banks each seed, so a bank or index bin is deterministic and says nothing about this leaf
|`sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`, `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test` |Bank entry indices |None, for the same reason: the three entry-walk leaves write every entry each seed
|`sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test` |Order of the eight extra sources |None: every catalog source is delivered and graded on every seed, so a source bin would be full by construction. The 42-source map is Phase 2
|`sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test` |Message length 1-16 words |None: one draw per regression, and only 3 of the 16 values need a second SHA-256 padding block
|`sep_km_key_policy_vault_test` |KPV slot, SRAM lock region, seal pair |None: the ROM writes KPV `CTRL` and `SRAM_LOCK` on the KM CPU bus, which the sampler does not see, and it walks slot 0, the pick and slot 63 every seed
|`sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test` |Which DEMOTE register carries the lock proof |None: the lock proof is a refusal (Phase 2 `sep_lc_demote_lock_cg`)
|`sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test`, `sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test` |Locked and control spares |None: refusals and the interrupt-33 pulse are error paths (Phase 2 `sep_efuse_lock_policy_cg`)
|`sep_efuse_km_public_id_test` |Which OTP ID is read-locked |None: the KM-side copy has no SEP-visible decode (Phase 2 `sep_km_otp_readout_cg`)
|`sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test` |PAGE PROGRAM length, sector, page, data |None: one length per seed, and the opcode sequence (`cp_opcode`) is fixed
|`sep_axi_order_sweep_test`, `_m_axi_test` |AW/W skew 1-4, BREADY/RREADY delay 0-2 |None: the three orderings are in `cp_aw_w_order` on every seed; the skew depth is a timing value
|`sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test` |Depth 3-6 per bus, AWID, operation mix |None: depth is in `cp_rd_depth` and `sep_smn_inbound_cg.cp_rd_depth`, which deterministic leaves fill
|`sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test` |Thresholds, prescaler |None: values inside a fixed divided-rate contract
|`sep_efuse_image_test` |Lifecycle state of the staged image, SIP_DIS / SYS_DIS vectors |`sep_lc_state_cg.cp_lc`, `sep_feat_ctrl_cg.cp_state` and `cp_debug`; every cell also has a deterministic owner
|`sep_cpu_trace_diag_test` |The docstring names a seeded clock-timing factor |None exists: `env/sep_env_cfg.py` fixes every clock period
|Other RANDCFG leaves |Key, data, operand, token and address values; sweep order; bit offsets inside a walked field |None: values inside a fixed contract that the checker reads back
|`sep_aes_reseed_trigger_rand_test` |One multi-hot invalid reseed-rate code of four |`sep_crypto_invalid_encoding_cg.x_aes_invalid_map` (multi-hot class), on every seed. No bin per code
|`sep_abr_command_matrix_rand_test` |Single-step cell, re-arm of each trial, predecessor of each trial, reason class and row of the ACVP ML-DSA reject case, the two ACVP ML-KEM modified-ciphertext rows |`sep_abr_completion_cg.x_op_protocol` (the re-arm cells of the combined operations and the single-step cells) and `cp_verify_outcome` (the reason-class bins), seed-dependent. The blocked cells and `cp_combined_cmd` fill on every seed. The predecessor, the row inside a reason class and the ML-KEM rows have no bin
|`sep_esrc_health_pattern_rand_test` |Lane and window of each pattern trial |`sep_esrc_fault_alert_cg.x_gen_latch` (lane bins) and `sep_esrc_startup_health_cg.cp_window_class` (above 2048), seed-dependent
|`sep_esrc_persistent_alert_rand_test` |Third threshold, 4, 5 or 7 |`sep_esrc_fault_alert_cg.cp_alert_threshold` (bins 5 and 7), seed-dependent
|`sep_esrc_autotune_detune_test` |Detuned lane |`sep_esrc_noise_gen_cg.cp_detune_lane`, seed-dependent
|`sep_drbg_sink_mix_rand_test` |Sink set |`sep_entropy_delivery_cg.cp_active_sinks`, seed-dependent (the Key Manager bin fills on every seed)
|`sep_fabric_filter_match_priority_rand_test` |Destination class of each leg 1 cell, one more source ID, two more wildcard AxUSER values |None: `cp_src_id_class` bins the classes, and no bin crosses attribute and destination class
|`sep_fabric_smc_route_test` |SMC aperture configuration A or B |`cp_smc_cfg_class_dir`, `sil_out_on_smc_route` and the above-top bin of `sil_smc_outside_aperture`, seed-dependent
|`sep_mailbox_fifo_model_rand_test` |Two thresholds per FIFO of each mailbox, flush kind of each mailbox |`sep_mbox_fifo_model_cg.cp_thresh_val` (1, 6, 9, other), seed-dependent. The flush kinds fill `x_flush` on every seed
|`sep_mailbox_reset_clear_test` |Reset cell (operation x offset class) |`sep_mbox_reset_cg.x_op_offset`, seed-dependent
|`sep_pic_priority_gate_rand_test` |Set size and drawn priorities of each round |`sep_pic_enable_priority_cg.cp_winner`, `cp_level_val` (the encodings 2 to 13) and `x_level_class_order` (the interior cells), seed-dependent
|`sep_nmi_vector_bark_rand_test` |Trampoline copy, the second case of (b) to (d) |`sep_nmi_vector_cfg_cg.cp_nmi_target` (copy bins), `sep_nmi_concurrency_cg.x_case_taken` and `cp_vec_unchanged`, seed-dependent
|SPI and DMA Phase 3 tests |See <<sep-fcov-p2-spi-dma>>, Random factors and their bins |The coverpoints of that area whose status ends in `seed-dependent`
|`sep_boot_release_terms_test` |Second arrival epoch of `ext_boot_seq_done_i` |None: in each drawn arrival the sensing or a short delay is the last term, so the epoch is a control of CHK-REL-GATE. `cp_release_order` fills from the H = yes epoch of every leaf
|`sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` |Partition of leg A into step subsets |`cp_independence_pair`, seed-dependent
|`sep_cold_reset_phase_test` |`clk_i` third of the graded pass |`cp_rst_release_sync`, `cp_aon_release_sync`, seed-dependent
|`sep_sram_axi_shape_matrix_test` |Offset of the reset in flight |`cp_otf_reset_x_path` (the SRAM offset thirds), seed-dependent
|`sep_efuse_field_layout_rand_test`, `sep_efuse_lock_slot_matrix_rand_test` |Class and value of each narrow field; leaf kind and lock layout |`sep_efuse_field_value_cg`, `sep_efuse_lock_policy_cg`, seed-dependent
|`sep_lcc_feat_ctrl_state_matrix_rand_test` |Leaf plan: follow-on leg, raw state code, strap, reset vector, walk positions, leg E order |`sep_lc_feat_ctrl_profile_cg`, `sep_lc_feature_bit_semantics_cg`, `sep_dtp_gating_ladder_cg`, `sep_lc_sigint_failclosed_cg.x_raw16_sigint`, `sep_lc_demotion_cg`, `sep_lc_demote_lock_cg`, `sep_sec_dis_activation_cg` (the after-sense bin of `cp_match_phase`, the Match and Mismatch cells of `x_verdict_out`, `x_state_demote_forced`), seed-dependent. The 32 leg E combinations have no bin (<<sep-fcov-p2-efuse-lifecycle-notdeclared>>)
|`sep_lcc_state_codeword_rand_test` |Nibble of the state code word |`sep_lc_sigint_failclosed_cg.x_sigint_state_secdis_out` and the integrity-error cells of `sep_efuse_jtag_policy_cg`, seed-dependent
|`sep_efuse_transient_rma_rand_test` |Enable and start state |`sep_lc_illegal_transition_cg.x_transient`, seed-dependent
|`sep_efuse_token_fault_matrix_test` |Fault cell of each channel |`sep_token_fault_cg.x_ch_kind_copy`, `x_ch_retry`, seed-dependent
|`sep_efuse_secure_tm_matrix_rand_test`, `sep_efuse_jtag_access_rand_test` |Lifecycle state of the image |`sep_efuse_jtag_policy_cg.cp_state`, `x_state_class_op`, `cp_gate_independent_of_feat`, seed-dependent; no bin of `sep_efuse_secure_tm_cg` depends on the state
|`sep_sec_dis_timing_rand_test` |Class B or C |`sep_sec_dis_activation_cg.cp_match_phase` and `sep_sec_dis_robustness_cg.cp_stops_sense` phase bins, seed-dependent
|`sep_efuse_shim_response_fault_test` |Word class of the sense status |None: `cp_sense_status_word` has one bin for every word of 10 to 254, and CHK-SENSE-STATUS grades the drawn word
|`sep_km_key_transfer_rand_test` |Size class of each non-witness engine |`sep_km_sideload_delivery_cg.cp_km_size_vs_width`, seed-dependent
|`sep_km_boot_reset_rand_test` |Two warm-reset points of (i) to (iii) |None: no bin records the reset point
|`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test` |Single-source points, multi-source winners, parity points, key CRC word class |`cp_csr_isr_winner`, `cp_if_parity`, `cp_fr_unrec_code`, `cp_fr_isr_disposition`, `cp_transfer_integrity`, seed-dependent
|`sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test` |Enable of the C5 and C6 boots |`sep_km_exec_protection_cg.cp_ex_mode_x_target`, seed-dependent
|`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |Whether the leaf runs the good-side saturation loop |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_cg.cp_smp_count_good`, seed-dependent
|`sep_km_otp_interface_rand_test` |Lifecycle state of the image |`sep_km_otp_readout_cg.cp_otp_read_through`, seed-dependent
|===

[[sep-fcov-owners]]
=== Owning tests

A hole is not gradable without knowing which test was supposed to fill it, so
the owner is named here rather than left to the VPLAN. `owner` is the `all`
member that MUST fill the group; a group its owner leaves empty is a dead bin
or a stimulus that does not do what the test says, and either way it needs a
human. Other members may fill a group in passing.

[cols="1,2",options="header"]
|===
|Covergroup |Owning `all` test(s)
|`sep_axi_decode_cg` |`sep_axi_smoke_test`; `cp_block` `sep_address_map_test` (one read in every block it walks; the STEE remap and outbound filter banks are read back by `sep_fabric_outbound_remap_filter_walk_test`, `EFUSE_INTERFACE_CTRL` by the eFuse program leaves); `cp_aw_w_order` `sep_axi_order_sweep_test`; `cp_ar_during_write` `sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test` and `_edn_aw_then_ar_test`; `cp_wstrb` `sep_axi_strobe_window_test`; `cp_rd_depth` `sep_axi_id_routing_test`; `cp_arsize` `sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test`
|`sep_smn_inbound_cg` |`sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test` (depths 1, 2, 3, 4 and 8 on `m_axi`)
|`sep_sram_access_cg` |`sep_sram_smoke_test`, `sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test`
|`sep_boot_rom_cg` |`sep_boot_rom_smoke_test` (IFU), `sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test` (LSU)
|`sep_cpu_boot_cg` |`sep_hello_world_test`, and every firmware leaf that ends on the PASS magic
|`sep_aes_mode_cg` |`sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test`
|`sep_hmac_mode_cg` |`sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`
|`sep_kmac_mode_cg` |`sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test` owns the digest-equals-golden walk. The KM sideload cell needs `sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test`, whose completion is a STATE-share read rather than an `INTR_STATE` read and whose `cp_kmac` hit is configuration only
|`sep_otbn_execute_cg` |`sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`; `cp_imem_rdback` / `cp_dmem_rdback` `sep_otbn_mem_smoke_test`
|`sep_abr_keygen_cg` |`sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test`
|`sep_abr_sign_cg` |`sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test`
|`sep_abr_mlkem_cg` |`sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`
|`sep_esrc_edn_flow_cg` |`sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`; KM sink `sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test`; the four `cp_edn_*` client coverpoints `sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test` (it is the only leaf that drives every adapter client in one run); `cp_pool_pop` `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test`; `cp_ht_sel` `sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test` (it walks every supported selector and one unsupported value each seed)
|`sep_km_generate_cg` |`sep_km_command_set_rand_test`
|`sep_km_command_sideload_cg` |the four `sep_km_{aes,hmac,kmac,otbn}_sideload_kat_test` (MUST-fill for HMAC/KMAC/AES/OTBN). `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` writes one seed-selected classic dest plus a directed ABR ML-DSA seed dest every seed. `sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test` and `sep_km_abr_mldsa_kat_test` also write the ML-DSA seed dest. The three ML-KEM cells -- `abr_mlkem_seed_d`, `abr_mlkem_seed_z`, `abr_mlkem_msg` -- are MUST-fill for `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`, the only leaf that transfers to them. One seed of the command-set leaf does not fill all four classic cells.
|`sep_km_host_cmd_cg` |`sep_km_command_set_rand_test` (`CMD_HW_VER` / `CMD_ROM_VER` / `CMD_SRAM_VER` / `CMD_RECOV_ACK` / `CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD`); `sep_km_handover_test` (`CMD_EXEC_ROM` / `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` / `CMD_SRAM_EXEC`)
|`sep_km_wipe_cg` |`sep_km_wipe_reset_test`
|`sep_km_sw_reset_cg` |`sep_km_wipe_reset_test`, `sep_km_handover_test`
|`sep_crypto_isolate_cg` |`sep_km_isolate_termination_test` (the three HMAC/KM bins: it parks HMAC with the KM released, so the KM path isolates under the HMAC request, and then reopens it). `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` also parks HMAC at bring-up with the KM released and fills `cp_km_iso` / `cp_rst_ordered`. `cp_abr_rst_ordered` `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test` (`CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ORDER`, the host term); `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` (its firmware pulses each crypto `SW_RESET_N` bit) and `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test` also fill it. Those leaves hold the KM in reset, so their KM path bits are high from time 0 and cannot fill the three HMAC/KM bins.
|`sep_dma_copy_hash_cg` |`sep_dma_basic_test` (copy), `sep_dma_hash_test` (hash; both `cp_hash_opcode` bins -- it is the only leaf that commands an inline hash, and it walks SHA-256 then SHA-384)
|`sep_spi_flash_cg` |`sep_spi_flash_jedec_smoke_test`, `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test`; DMA handshake `sep_spi_ot_dma_rx_test` / `sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test`; `cp_opcode` `sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test` (every opcode is in its fixed BFM sequence)
|`sep_inbound_filter_allow_cg` |`sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`
|`sep_efuse_sense_cg` |`sep_efuse_sense_test`, `sep_efuse_image_test` (any leaf passing `+skip_fuse_sense` cannot fill it)
|`sep_efuse_program_lock_cg` |`sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test`
|`sep_efuse_fail_closed_cg` |`sep_efuse_illegal_state_fail_closed_test`
|`sep_lc_state_cg` |`sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test` and the seven `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_*` leaves
|`sep_lc_transition_cg` |`sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_test` and `_prod_demote_test` (PROD to RMA_SIP_1), `_rma_sip_test` (RMA_SIP_1 to RMA_CHIP_1)
|`sep_dma_completion_route_cg` |`sep_dma_basic_test` (STATUS poll), `sep_dma_hash_test` (interrupt), `sep_spi_ot_dma_rx_test` / `_tx_test` (handshake mode)
|`sep_feat_ctrl_cg` |`sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test` (also both `SECURE_TM` polarities), the seven W1S leaves, `sep_sec_dis_override_test` (SEC_DIS)
|`sep_sec_dis_boot_cg` |`sep_sec_dis_override_test` (`cp_hold`, `cp_release`, `cp_rehold`, `cp_alone`, `cp_map_resp`). `cp_map_resp.okay` fills when the pre-sense `LC_STATE` read returns AXI OKAY. `sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test` fills `cp_release`, `cp_rehold`, `cp_alone`, and `cp_reach`
|`sep_lc_demote_cg` |`sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test`
|`sep_mailbox_pic_cg` |SEP mailbox `sep_mailbox_plic_test`, `sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test`; KM mailbox `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`; `cp_peer_fill` `sep_mailbox_peer_rx_rirqt_test` (the only leaf that drives the peer aperture). `sep_axil_mailbox_iface_rand_test` does not fill `cp_raise` / `cp_clear`: it is transmit side, and `sep_internal_interrupts[7:0]` carries the inbound interrupt
|`sep_mailbox_channel_cg` |`sep_mailbox_plic_test` (it runs one channel at a time, all eight)
|`sep_wdt_bark_cg` |`sep_nmi_sanity_test`, `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` (threshold, bark and NMI). `sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test` writes the threshold but never lets the watchdog bark, so it does not fill `cp_thold`
|`sep_scratch_reset_cg` |`sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test`
|===

Per-test detail is in <<sep-dv-verification-plan,SEP_VPLAN>>.

==== Tests whose own contract has no Phase 1 bin

Every other `all` test is an owner above or fills a group in passing. The
tests below do not have a bin for the behaviour they exist to check. Each has
the reason.

[cols="2,3",options="header"]
|===
|Test |Reason
|`sep_clock_uvm_wdt_rst_input_reset_path_test`, `sep_cpu_dbg_reset_independence_test` |No bin: no group samples a CPU-only reset (`sep_cpu_reset_n` falls while `sep_reset_n` stays high). Both nets are sampler inputs. These two leaves feed no group
|`sep_cpu_trace_diag_test` |No bin: no group samples an exception-not-interrupt retirement on the trace port. Fills `sep_cpu_boot_cg` in passing
|`sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test` |No bin: no group samples a retired PC in the `SEP_LOCAL` alias window (Phase 2 `sep_remap_alias_cg`)
|`sep_km_mem_smoke_test` |No bin: KM ROM fetch and scrambled KM SRAM write bins need the `tb_top` KM memory monitors as sampler inputs (Phase 2 `sep_km_memory_boot_cg`)
|`sep_efuse_jtag_window_allow_test`, `sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test` |No bin: the JTAG AXI-Lite port is not a sampler input. The deny leg is an error path (Phase 2)
|`sep_efuse_km_axil_cpu_mux_coexist_test` |No bin: same-cycle requests at the KM/EL2 eFuse mux need a mux probe
|`sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test` |No bin: no group samples an inbound OKAY with the filter skipped (`FEAT_CTRL.sep_debug` = 1)
|`sep_trng_reset_recovery_test` |No bin: no group samples a new seed after a TRNG domain reset (Phase 2 `sep_crypto_reset_cg`)
|`sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test` |No bin: the leaf issues one `glen`, so a length coverpoint would be half empty by construction (Phase 2 `sep_csrng_cmd_cg`)
|`sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test` |No bin: token match and mismatch per channel need the token-status decode (Phase 2 `sep_token_channel_cg`)
|`sep_km_ic_reset_release_test` |No bin: the `km_jtag_rst_n` override edges need a probe
|`sep_hmac_kmac_cpu_crypto_smoke_test` |Fills the AES, HMAC and KMAC mode groups in passing. No cell records a CPU source
|`sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`, `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test`, `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`, `sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test`, `sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test`, `sep_efuse_set_only_monotonicity_test`, `sep_efuse_digest_latch_fault_test`, `sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test`, `sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test`, `sep_efuse_km_public_id_test` |Phase 2: FIPS lock, alerts, the 42-source map, token faults, set-only walks, lock refusals and the KM OTP readout are named Phase 2 groups
|`sep_periph_bus_err_misaligned_test`, `sep_km_rom_write_err_test`, `sep_km_otp_shim_unreachable_test`, `sep_km_vrom_decerr_test`, `sep_axi_map_refuse_test`, `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test`, `sep_unmapped_access_policy_test` |Error paths (Phase 2 `sep_aperture_reserved_access_cg` and the KM error groups)
|`sep_sram_burst_type_test` |FIXED and WRAP bursts: the data bins score single-beat transactions only, by the pairing rule in <<sep-fcov-collection>>, and no Phase 2 group names burst type
|`sep_fabric_outbound_remap_filter_walk_test`, `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_window_walk_test`, `sep_fabric_local_alias_offset_walk_test`, `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`, `sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`, `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test` |Fill the bank cells of `cp_block`. The per-entry winner walk is Phase 2 (`sep_filter_match_cg`, `sep_remap_alias_cg`, `sep_remap_output_offset_cg`)
|`sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`, `sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test`, `sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test` |The behaviour is on the KM CPU bus, which the sampler does not see (Phase 2 `sep_km_kpv_slot_cg`)
|`sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`, `sep_clock_gate_control_test` |Not meaningful: per-register bins restate the RDL that the sweep checks, and the RTL does not consume `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL`. Both fill `sep_axi_decode_cg` in passing
|`sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test` |Not meaningful: it drives a TB-owned adapter instance outside the DUT
|===

[[sep-fcov-closure]]
=== Closure Policy

A Phase 1 bin takes no waiver. A bin its owner leaves empty is a dead bin or a
stimulus that does not do what the test says (<<sep-fcov-owners>>), and a cell
that no `all` test walks belongs in <<sep-fcov-planned>>.

The rules below bind the VCS code-coverage number and its policy file,
`cov/config/vcs/coverage_policy.toml`. The runner's schema and grading
behaviour are in `tools/dv/doc/run-dv.adoc`. A code-coverage point that the
`all` regression leaves uncovered stays in the denominator until a test reaches
it or an RTL fact removes it.

[[sep-fcov-evidence-paths]]
==== Evidence paths

* *Functional coverage.* The URG Group report on `sep_uvm_top.u_sep_fcov` from
  a `--cov --tool vcs` run (<<sep-fcov-collection>>). Verilator does not
  compile these groups, so a Verilator class regression is not Phase 1 fill
  evidence.
* *Code coverage.* The VCS run of the `all` group:
+
[source,bash]
----
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut sep --items all --regress --cov --tool vcs \
  --target default --sim-jobs 32 --build-jobs 32
----
+
`--cov` compiles and simulates with `-cm line+cond+tgl+fsm+branch+assert`,
writes one database per leaf (`coverage/simv.vdb`), and the `cov_merge` and
`cov_report` stages merge them with `urg` into `<run_dir>/cov/merged.vdb`.
`--target default` compiles the full CPU once: no_cpu leaves force-splice the
LSU VIP onto the post-remap request, and cpu leaves run as firmware. The run
has every `all` leaf except `sep_abr_absent_decerr_test`, which runs only on
Verilator (`tools = ["verilator"]`, target `lsu_stub_abr_absent`), so its code
coverage is not part of the graded number. After an edit to
`sep_cov_scope.hier`, rebuild with `--rebuild`.
* *Run claims.* A collection or elaboration claim names the tool and the
  `run_dv.py` run ID and quotes the launcher `result status=` line. Local paths
  under `build/runs/` are not durable.
* No hosted CI tier collects coverage (`.github/workflows/regress.yml`).
  `--cov --tool verilator` is not the graded number, because only
  `[coverage.vcs]` applies the compile-time scope.

What the code-coverage number is not:

* *Not functional coverage.* These are code metrics only. Functional coverage
  is the URG Group report on `sep_uvm_top.u_sep_fcov`. A covergroup bin records
  that an interface event happened, never that it was correct; the checkers in
  <<sep-dv-verification-plan,SEP_VPLAN>> grade correctness.
* *Not the whole DUT.* It grades Tenstorrent-owned SEP integration RTL in the
  scope of <<sep-fcov-scope>>. Quote that scope with the percentage. Never call
  it bare "SEP DUT coverage".
* *Not a read on assertions.* Assertion coverage counts elaborated assertions
  only, and SEP gates those through the `prim_assert` shim. Confirm that
  assertions are live before you read that column.
* *One seed per leaf.* `--regress` takes a fresh seed per leaf, so a randomized
  test gives one sample. Pin the seeds for a number that you cite.

===== Boot ROM firmware C coverage

Boot ROM C source coverage is separate from simulator-native RTL coverage. Run
the `rom_fw` group with `--plusarg +sep_rom_fw_coverage`. The CPU trace monitor
then writes a Renode-format retired-PC trace into each simulation leaf.
Generate the report from that run directory:

[source,bash]
----
uv run --locked python3 tools/dv/fw_coverage/gen_sep_rom_coverage.py \
  --run-dir "$RUN_DIR"
----

The generator accepts only passing, complete traces and uses the leaf-local
`boot_rom.elf` that the firmware profile stages. It reports `boot_rom` and
`boot_rom_pio` separately, because one ELF cannot interpret the PCs of both.
`tools/dv/fw_coverage/README.md` has the complete command and the tool
prerequisites.

==== Hole dispositions

URG hands the runner family totals and no per-point list, so the policy
carries no `pass:[[[holes]]]` and a hole has no recorded disposition entry. An
uncovered code-coverage point closes in one of these ways:

[cols="1,3,2",options="header"]
|===
|Disposition |Meaning |Where it is recorded
|Cover |The point is reachable. A new or extended scenario reaches it |The test and its VPLAN entry
|Fix design |A design defect blocks the point |The design change
|Scope |The point is outside the graded ownership scope |`sep_cov_scope.hier` (<<sep-fcov-scope>>)
|Exclude |An RTL tie, parameter, unconnected port or generate condition makes the point unreachable |One of the eight `-elfile` lists (<<sep-fcov-exclusion-lists>>)
|===

A stimulus gap stays in the denominator. It takes no exclusion.

==== Waiver fields

The policy has no waiver entry: it sets no `pass:[[[holes]]]` and names no
`role = "waive"` file. An exclusion-list entry carries a URG `ANNOTATION` that
states the RTL fact that holds the points constant, and a `CHECKSUM` from
`urg -dump full_exclusions` on the merged VCS database, never written by hand.

==== Run status

A regression passes or fails on its tests; its coverage figures do not move its
status. `cov/config/vcs/coverage_policy.toml` sets no `pass:[[[thresholds]]]`
row and `sep_sim_cfg.toml` sets no `fail_under`, so the runner grades coverage
against 0 percent unless the command passes `--fail-under`. `cov_report` fails
when the report tool fails or the runner refuses the policy file, not on a
figure.

==== Reviewers

The reviewer of a change to a coverage input is not its author. The review
class follows the kind of fact that the input rests on.

[cols="2,3,2",options="header"]
|===
|Input |Fact it rests on |Review
|`sep_cov_scope.hier` |An ownership decision: which modules the number grades. The testbench, the VeeR EL2 core complex, library-class cells, DV models and complete third-party IP leave the scope |Coverage-flow review
|The eight `-elfile` lists |A design fact: an RTL tie, an elaborated parameter, an unconnected port or a generate condition, which <<sep-fcov-exclusion-lists>> states for each list |Design review
|A `pass:[[[native_files]]]` entry of the policy |Which file the report applies, with which arguments |Coverage-flow review
|A Phase 1 bin or its owner |The cell that an `all` test walks (<<sep-fcov-owners>>) |DV review
|===

A change to an exclusion list is graded by a re-render of a named finished
run, `python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut sep --run-dir <run> --stage cov_report`,
and the report must complete with no missing-object warning. A report run
with `-excl_strict` must show the same counts as a run without it, which shows
that no excluded object has a hit. The report does not reject an entry with
a stale checksum, so the review of an entry checks it against a fresh
`urg -dump full_exclusions` of its scope (<<sep-fcov-reopen>>). A
change to `sep_cov_scope.hier` takes a new compile (`--rebuild`) and a new
coverage regression, because the scope applies at compile time.

[[sep-fcov-reopen]]
==== Reopen triggers

An accepted exclusion stays accepted until the policy or the design changes;
the calendar does not reopen it. It reopens when:

* an RTL change touches a module or instance that an entry names. The review
  re-dumps that scope with `urg -dump full_exclusions` and checks the entry
  against the dump, because the report applies an entry with a stale checksum
  and does not reject it;
* a design change removes the tie, parameter, unconnected port or generate
  condition that the entry rests on;
* a leaf covers a point that an entry excludes, which shows that the fact the
  entry states is false;
* the policy's `scope_epoch` changes, or `sep_cov_scope.hier` changes which
  points enter the database;
* the `-cm` metric set or the report `-metric` list of `[coverage.vcs]` in
  `sep_sim_cfg.toml` changes;
* a coverage run builds a target other than `--target default`. The lists are
  written for that elaboration, and several of them state that a `rom_boot`
  target run is not merged against them.

[[sep-fcov-scope]]
==== Compile-time scope

The VCS code-coverage result grades Tenstorrent-owned SEP integration RTL. The
compile-time `cov/config/vcs/sep_cov_scope.hier` removes the testbench, the VeeR
EL2 core complex inside `sep_cpu`, library-class cells, DV models and complete
third-party IP modules and interfaces, across code and assertion coverage
(`-cm_hier` with `-cm_common_hier`). The Tenstorrent CPU wrapper `sep_cpu`
(window remaps, IFU/LSU demux decode) stays graded. `urg -hier` at report time
prunes report pages and still grades the whole database, so the scope is this
compile-time file.
Those whole-module entries are ownership decisions, not claims that the logic
is unreachable.

Report-time `.el` files apply only to objects that remain in the compile-time
scope. An exclusion needs an RTL tie, parameter, unconnected port or generate
condition that makes the object unreachable. A stimulus gap stays in the
denominator.

[[sep-fcov-exclusion-lists]]
==== Unreachability exclusion lists

The VCS code-coverage report applies eight `-elfile` lists, which
`cov/config/vcs/coverage_policy.toml` names, on top of the compile-time scope
(<<sep-fcov-scope>>). Report-time lists apply only to objects that remain in
the compile-time scope. The checksums in each list come from
`urg -dump full_exclusions` on the merged VCS database. A report run must
complete without a missing-object warning, and a report run with
`-excl_strict` must show the same counts as a run without it.

All eight lists obey the same rules:

* An entry rests only on a literal RTL constant, an elaborated parameter, an
  unconnected DUT port or a generate-disabled path of SEP design logic. Its
  `ANNOTATION` cites that fact as file and line and names the fields that stay
  graded.
* A constant that the reference integration (`hw/top/sep_ip_integration.sv`,
  `hw/top/sep_wrapper.sv`) drives into a SEP input port is not an exclusion
  reason. A literal assign inside a module that the reference integration
  instantiates is an RTL tie of that module.
* A field that a master, the inbound SoC port, JTAG, an integrator port, a
  software-writable CSR, an error response or a fault can move stays graded as
  a stimulus gap.
* A logic argument without a constant signal (an implication, a complement, a
  case default, an FSM default arm, a value set or reset timing) is not an
  exclusion reason.
