[[sep-fcov-p2-mem-aperture]]
===== `sep_mem_aperture_cg`

*Class*: executable (<<sep-fcov-parked>>).

*Description*: The memory apertures, the access shapes the DUT accepts, the
masters that reach each memory, and a reset that falls with a request in
flight. Tool: VCS.

[cols="2,6,4,3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins and crosses |Illegal bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Notes
|`cp_mem_x_class` |memory {Boot ROM 64 KiB at 0x1004_0000, ICCM 256 KiB at 0xC000_0000, DCCM 128 KiB at 0xC004_0000} x address class {first word, last word}. The ROM cells are crossed with the load size {byte, half} = 4 cells, and the ICCM and DCCM cells are 4 cells, so 8 bins. The 32-bit ROM loads of the first and last word are graded by `sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test` (open) and are controls of the owning test, so they are not bins. The SRAM first and last word are graded by `sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test` (open), and `sep_sram_axi_shape_matrix_test` reaches them only in its control `CTL-SRAM-REACH`, so they are not bins. The address is taken at the AXI side: ROM at the LSU bus, ICCM and DCCM at `cpu_tcm_axi_req`. The classes "one word past the end" and "reserved window" belong to the reserved-access groups. Spec: `regs/memory_map.adoc` (rows `sep_sram`, `sep_boot_rom`, `sep_iccm`, `sep_dccm`); `doc/cpu.adoc`, Local Memory |None |LSU bus, `SEP_CORE.cpu_tcm_axi_req` |`sep_boot_rom_aperture_sweep_test`, `sep_tcm_dma_aperture_ecc_test` |executable
|`cp_access_size` |memory x access size {byte, half, word, double}, taken from AxSIZE of a read and from the strobe width of a write on the LSU bus. Cells: ROM x {byte, half} (2). ROM x word is graded by `sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test` (open) and is a control of the owning test, so it is not a bin. ROM x double and the TCM sizes have no owning test. The SRAM sizes are graded by `sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test` and `sep_sram_smoke_test` (open), and `sep_sram_axi_shape_matrix_test` guarantees every size only in its control `CTL-SRAM-REACH`, so they are not bins. The spec gives the 64-bit word path and no size limit. Spec: `doc/rom.adoc`, ROM Architecture |None |LSU bus address, AxSIZE and write strobes |`sep_boot_rom_aperture_sweep_test` |executable
|`cp_macro_if` |pair {ROM, SRAM, TCM} x phase {request issued, response consumed}. Request cell: `req` = 1 on `sep_boot_rom_req` or `sep_sram_req`, or any `clken` on `sep_cpu_tcm_req`. Response cell: `rvalid` = 1 on `sep_boot_rom_rsp` or `sep_sram_rsp`. = 5 cells. CHK-ROM-PORT and CHK-ROM-OTF fill the ROM cells, CHK-SRAM-OTF-REPLAY fills the SRAM cells and CHK-TCM-APERT fills the TCM request cell. The TCM response phase is not a bin (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). Spec: `doc/port_table.adoc`, port list (memory macro interfaces) |None |`u_dut.sep_boot_rom_req`, `u_dut.sep_boot_rom_rsp`, `u_dut.sep_sram_req`, `u_dut.sep_sram_rsp`, `u_dut.sep_cpu_tcm_req` |`sep_boot_rom_aperture_sweep_test` (the ROM cells), `sep_sram_axi_shape_matrix_test` (the SRAM cells), `sep_tcm_dma_aperture_ecc_test` (the TCM request cell: its DMA copies drive the TCM macro) |executable. The SRAM traffic of the ROM owner (its firmware runs from SRAM) and of the TCM owner (its staging words) fills no SRAM cell
|`cp_requesting_master` |master {CPU LSU on `s_axi`, inbound master on `m_axi`} x single-beat burst type at the SRAM aperture {FIXED, WRAP with AxLEN = 0}, each with an OKAY response = 4 cells. The sample is the B beat of a write and the R beat of a read. A plain reach cell (SRAM x master, any burst) is not a bin: `sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test`, `sep_sram_smoke_test` and `sep_sram_inbound_burst_attr_test` (open) grade the reach of both masters, and the owner reaches the SRAM from both masters only in its control `CTL-SRAM-REACH` (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). The ROM x CPU LSU and ROM x CPU IFU cells are bins of the Phase 1 `sep_boot_rom_cg` (`cp_lsu`, `cp_ifu`), the ICCM and DCCM x DMA cells are bins of `cp_tcm_dma_dir_range` of the fabric groups, and a CPU request on a TCM or ROM write port is graded by `cp_core_internal_path`, `cp_tcm_refused_initiator` and `cp_rom_write_master`. Spec: `doc/cpu.adoc`, CPU; `doc/fabric.adoc`, Top Level AXI4 (single-beat bursts of every type) |A single-beat FIXED or WRAP access at the SRAM aperture with a response other than OKAY |LSU bus and `m_axi_*` AxLEN, AxBURST and response |`sep_sram_axi_shape_matrix_test` |executable. The single-beat FIXED step and the single-beat WRAP step of the owner (CHK-SRAM-SINGLE) fill the four cells in every leaf. `CTL-SRAM-REACH` and the control writes of the refusal step run with the graded window closed
|`cp_sram_burst` |burst type {FIXED, WRAP} x length class {2 or more beats} x response {SLVERR}, on the inbound master (`m_axi`) at the SRAM aperture = 2 cells. The sample window is the B beat of a write and every R beat of a read. The single-beat FIXED and WRAP cells are bins of `cp_requesting_master`. The INCR cells are not bins: `sep_sram_inbound_burst_attr_test` (open) grades the INCR lengths, the owner reaches the SRAM with single-beat INCR accesses only in `CTL-SRAM-REACH` and in the set-up writes of its steps, and CHK-SRAM-OUTSTANDING grades its outstanding INCR bursts on the ID and data order, which a burst-shape cell does not record (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). The multi-beat FIXED and WRAP cells of the CPU-LSU master are graded by `sep_sram_burst_type_test` (open), and the owner drives no multi-beat burst on the CPU LSU. Spec: `doc/fabric.adoc`, Top Level AXI4; `doc/memory_map.adoc`, Single-Beat Register Access |FIXED or WRAP x 2 or more beats x OKAY. INCR x any x SLVERR |LSU bus and `m_axi_*` AxLEN, AxBURST and response |`sep_sram_axi_shape_matrix_test` |executable. The sampler takes AxLEN and AxBURST inputs from the approved `s_axi_*` and `m_axi_*` TB inputs. The refusal step of the owner (CHK-SRAM-REFUSE) fills both cells in every leaf
|`cp_otf_reset_x_path` |path {SRAM read, SRAM write} x offset class of the `rst_ni` fall inside the response window {first third, middle third, last third}, and one ROM read cell = 7 cells. The ROM read cell has no offset-third axis: the ROM response window of the owner (from the `sep_boot_rom_req` handshake to the `sep_boot_rom_rsp` beat) is one `clk_i` cycle, so the offset is 0 in every leaf. The window starts at the request handshake. Recovery cell: the first `rvalid` or B beat after the release belongs to a request issued after the release. Spec: `doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller; `doc/port_table.adoc`, port list (memory macro interfaces) |An R or B beat after the release with no request after the release |`rst_ni`, LSU bus handshakes (SRAM cells), `u_dut.sep_boot_rom_req`, `u_dut.sep_boot_rom_rsp` (ROM cells) |`sep_sram_axi_shape_matrix_test` (the SRAM cells), `sep_boot_rom_aperture_sweep_test` (the ROM cells) |executable. Seed-dependent: the SRAM owner runs one reset in flight at a drawn offset for each SRAM path, so the SRAM offset-third bins fill on a merge of regressions (<<sep-fcov-rand>>). The ROM cell fills in every leaf of its owner. The coverpoint uses an ungated block
|`cp_tcm_ecc` |memory {ICCM, DCCM} x error kind {single, double} x position class. Single: {data bit, ECC bit}. Double: {2 data, 1 data and 1 ECC, 2 ECC}. Cells: 4 single, 6 double = 10. The no-error cells are not bins: the clean reads of the owner are the control of the error cells (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). The kind and the position class come from comparing the read data and ECC on `sep_cpu_tcm_rsp` with the last write on `sep_cpu_tcm_req` (`*_wr_data`, `*_wr_ecc`) for the same bank row. The response class comes from `cpu_tcm_axi_resp.r.resp` {not OKAY for double}. Spec: `doc/interrupts.adoc`, Memory ECC Errors (Correctable (single-bit) errors, Uncorrectable (double-bit) errors, Debug bus mirror) |Double x response OKAY |`u_dut.sep_cpu_tcm_req`, `u_dut.sep_cpu_tcm_rsp`, `SEP_CORE.cpu_tcm_axi_resp` |`sep_tcm_dma_aperture_ecc_test` |executable. CHK-TCM-SBE fills the single cells and CHK-TCM-DBE the double cells in every leaf: every bit position runs in each memory, and the owning test draws at least 4 pairs of each class (data-data, data-ECC, ECC-ECC) in each memory. The TCM bit-flip hook is an added hook (error inject)
|`cp_demux_target` |IFU target {error responder} and LSU target {ROM port, crossbar port} = 3 bins. The IFU ROM-port and SRAM-port cells are not bins: the open boot tests grade the ROM fetch, and the owner runs the ROM and SRAM fetches only as the control of CHK-IFU-DECERR (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). The LSU cells are the loads that CHK-LSU-ROUTE grades. Address classes: ROM aperture, SRAM aperture, other. Spec: `doc/cpu.adoc`, CPU; `doc/fabric.adoc`, Top Level AXI4 |An IFU fetch to an address outside ROM and SRAM with a response other than DECERR. An IFU request on the ROM or SRAM port for an address of the other class. An LSU ROM-address request on the crossbar port. An LSU SRAM-address request on the ROM port |`SEP_CORE.u_sep_cpu.ifu_axi_req`, `SEP_CORE.u_sep_cpu.ifu_axi_resp`, `SEP_CORE.u_sep_cpu.ifu_demux_resp`, `SEP_CORE.ifu_rom_axi_req`, `SEP_CORE.ifu_sram_axi_req`, `SEP_CORE.lsu_rom_axi_req`, `SEP_CORE.lsu_xbar_axi_req` |`sep_cpu_demux_unmapped_fetch_test` |executable
|`cp_core_internal_path` |ICCM fetch by the IFU, and DCCM load or store by the LSU: no request on `cpu_tcm_axi_req` during the access = 2 bins. The DMA copy to DCCM that shows `cpu_tcm_axi_req` activity is the control of CHK-CORE-PATH-XBAR and is not a bin. Spec: `doc/cpu.adoc`, CPU |None |`SEP_CORE.cpu_tcm_axi_req`, `u_dut.sep_cpu_tcm_req` |`sep_cpu_demux_unmapped_fetch_test` |executable
|===

[[sep-fcov-p2-reset-sw-ctrl]]
===== `sep_reset_sw_ctrl_cg`

*Class*: executable.

*Description*: The `SW_RESET_N` fields and the per-IP resets they drive. Tool:
VCS.

[cols="2,6,4,3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins and crosses |Illegal bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Notes
|`cp_field_transition` |field {km, otbn, aes, hmac, kmac, trng, abr} x transition {assert 1 to 0, release 0 to 1} = 14 cells. A cell is the `ip_rst_n[i]` fall (or rise) with `sep_reset_n` = 1, the override off and the `SW_RESET_N` write data at the new value. Spec: `regs/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc`, `SW_RESET_N`; `doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Software Reset Register |None |`ip_rst_n[6:1]` (the gated-reset probes), `SEP_CORE.sep_crypto_gated_rst_n` bit 0 (added tap), the LSU write data of `SW_RESET_N`, `sep_reset_n` |`sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` |executable. The leg A steps with \|S\| = 1 are the control `CTL-SUBSET` and run with the graded window closed
|`cp_multi_field_write` |one write that asserts two or more fields; one write that releases two or more fields = 2 bins. Spec: as `cp_field_transition` |None |the LSU write data of `SW_RESET_N`, `ip_rst_n[6:1]` |`sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` |executable. The subset draw has at least one step with two or more domains
|`cp_power_up_value` |the `hmac` field reads its reset value 1 at the first read after a cold reset that lands in the middle of a DMA copy (the graded cold-reset leg of the owner), with the field at 0 before the reset = 1 cell; the aggregate `SW_RESET_N` = 0x7E at the same read = 1 cell; 2 bins. The owner holds the other six fields at their reset value before the reset, so their per-field cells are not bins (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). The value after a power-on reset is graded by open tests (`sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test`, `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`), and the first read of `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` is a control, so neither fills the cell. Spec: `regs/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc`, `SW_RESET_N`; `doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Software Reset Register |None |LSU read data |`sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` |executable

[[sep-fcov-p2-reserved-access]]
===== `sep_aperture_reserved_access_cg`

*Class*: executable (<<sep-fcov-parked>>).

*Description*: Writes to reserved register bits. The refused-access response
codes of this group are in <<sep-fcov-planned>>. Tool: VCS.

[cols="2,6,4,3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins and crosses |Illegal bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Notes
|`cp_reserved_field_write` |register {`SW_RESET_N` bits 63:7, cold `SCRATCH[n]` bits 63:32, warm `SCRATCH[n]` bits 63:32} x write shape {64-bit lane, high 32-bit half only, low 32-bit half with data bits above the field} = 7 cells: `SW_RESET_N` x all three shapes, and each scratch bank x {64-bit lane, high 32-bit half}. The low-half shape with data bits above the field exists for `SW_RESET_N` only, because the scratch field is bits 31:0. The bin hits when the next read of the register returns the field bits that the leaf wrote (bits 6:0 of `SW_RESET_N`; bits 31:0 of `SCRATCH[n]`, which a high-half-only write leaves at their value before the write). The read value of the reserved bits is not stated and is not a bin condition (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). Spec: `regs/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc`, `SW_RESET_N`; `regs/blocks/sep_scratch.adoc`, `SCRATCH` |None |LSU bus write and read data and strobes |`sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` (the `SW_RESET_N` cells), `sep_cpu_ctrl_reg_shape_test` (the scratch cells) |executable. The sampler needs the write-completion event

[[sep-fcov-p2-wdt-config-bark]]
===== `sep_wdt_config_bark_cg`

*Class*: executable.

*Description*: Watchdog enable, bark status, threshold lock, bus adaptation and
threshold relations. Tool: VCS.

[cols="2,6,4,3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins and crosses |Illegal bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Notes
|`cp_enable_outcome` |`WDOG_CTRL.enable` {0, 1} x outcome {no count, count advances, bark threshold crossed} = 6 cells: 1 bin, 2 control cells that are not bins and 3 illegal cells. "No count": two `WDOG_COUNT` reads 64 `clk_wdt_i` ticks apart (a choice of the environment) are equal. "Advances": the second read is larger. "Bark threshold crossed": a `WDOG_COUNT` read is at or above `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` after reads below it. A bark status forced by `INTR_TEST` is not a crossing. Bin: enable 1 x crossed = 1 bin. The cells enable 0 x no count and enable 1 x advances are filled only by the control `CTL-WDT-ENABLE` (the open `sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test` grades the count), so they are not bins. Spec: `aon_timer.adoc`, `WDOG_CTRL` and `WDOG_COUNT`; `doc/watchdog.adoc`, Watchdog Timer (WDT) |Enable 0 with the count advancing or crossing the bark threshold. Enable 1 with the count not advancing |LSU read data, `clk_wdt_i` |`sep_timer_clock_contract_test` |executable
|`cp_bark_status` |`INTR_STATE.wdog_timer_bark` {set by threshold, cleared by a write of 1 after a set that `INTR_TEST` forced (CHK-WDT-INTRTEST, CHK-WDT-SHAPE), forced by `INTR_TEST` with enable 0, forced by `INTR_TEST` with enable 1 and a count far below the threshold} = 4 bins. Spec: `aon_timer.adoc`, `INTR_STATE` and `INTR_TEST` |`wdt_timer_rst_req_o` = 1 in any forced-bark window |LSU read data, `wdt_timer_rst_req_o`, `SEP_CORE.intr_wdog_timer_bark` |`sep_timer_clock_contract_test` |executable
|`cp_lock_x_reg` |(a) after `WDOG_REGWEN.regwen` clears, a write of 1 leaves it at 0 (CHK-REGWEN-STICKY); (b) after the cold reset of leg L, `regwen` reads 1 and a write to `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` lands (CHK-REGWEN-COLD) = 2 bins. The cells regwen 0 x ignored are not bins: `sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test` (open, CHK-REGWEN-LOCK) grades them, and the owner reads them as the control `CTL-REGWEN-LOCKED`. The cells regwen 1 x landed before the lock come only from set-up writes and the control `CTL-WDT-THOLD`, and are not bins (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). The `WDOG_CTRL` and `WDOG_COUNT` lock cells are not bins. Spec: `aon_timer.adoc`, `WDOG_REGWEN`; `doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture |regwen 0 x landed. regwen 1 x ignored |LSU bus write and read data |`sep_timer_clock_contract_test` |executable
|`cp_adapt_stage` |stage {`sep_wdt_tlul_axi_req` (after the 64-to-32 converter), `sep_wdt_tlul_axi_req_offset` (after the base subtraction), `axi_lite_req`, `tl_d_i`} x access class {32-bit low half, 32-bit high half} = 8 cells. A cell hits when the stage shows a handshake for that class on a 32-bit access whose CHK-WDT-SHAPE readback passes. CHK-WDT-SHAPE fills the cell. Spec: `doc/watchdog.adoc`, Bus Interface; `regs/memory_map.adoc` (row Always-On Timer) |None |`SEP_CORE.u_sep_wdt_wrap.sep_wdt_tlul_axi_req`, `SEP_CORE.u_sep_wdt_wrap.sep_wdt_tlul_axi_req_offset`, `SEP_CORE.u_sep_wdt_wrap.axi_lite_req`, `SEP_CORE.u_sep_wdt_wrap.tl_d_i` |`sep_timer_clock_contract_test` |executable. The four stage nets of `u_sep_wdt_wrap` are added taps
|`cp_access_x_register` |read while the counter advances x register {`WDOG_CTRL`, `WDOG_COUNT`} = 2 bins: the `WDOG_CTRL` reads between two count reads and the count reads of the coherent-read step of the owner (CHK-WDT-COHERENT). The threshold cells (a write or a read of `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` or `WDOG_BITE_THOLD` while the counter advances) are the control `CTL-WDT-THOLD`, and a write of `WDOG_CTRL` while the counter advances occurs only in set-up steps and in the control `CTL-WDT-ENABLE`, so they are not bins (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). The cell (write while the counter advances, `WDOG_COUNT`) is not a bin: no owning test writes the count while the timer counts. "Advances" is enable 1 and two successive `WDOG_COUNT` reads that differ. The `WDOG_COUNT` cell samples a count read that directly follows another `WDOG_COUNT` read or a `WDOG_CTRL` read, as in the coherent-read step. The count reads of a watchdog run follow an `INTR_STATE` read and fill no cell. A write in the same tick as a threshold crossing is not a bin. Spec: `aon_timer.adoc`, `WDOG_CTRL`, `WDOG_BARK_THOLD`, `WDOG_BITE_THOLD`, `WDOG_COUNT` |None |LSU bus write and read data |`sep_timer_clock_contract_test` |executable
|`cp_thold_order` |(a) `intr_wdog_timer_bark` rises with `wdt_timer_rst_req_o` = 0. (b) a larger bark threshold gives a later bark (second run). (c) thresholds near 2^32 with a preloaded count reach bark and bite (the threshold values are drawn). The rise of `wdt_timer_rst_req_o` after an unanswered bark is graded by `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` (open) and is not a bin. Spec: `aon_timer.adoc`, `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` and `WDOG_BITE_THOLD`; `doc/watchdog.adoc`, Watchdog Timer (WDT) and Bark Interrupt and Bite Reset |`wdt_timer_rst_req_o` rises while the bark status is 0 |`wdt_timer_rst_req_o`, `SEP_CORE.intr_wdog_timer_bark`, LSU read data |`sep_timer_clock_contract_test` |executable
|===

[[sep-fcov-p2-wdt-wakeup-timer]]
===== `sep_wdt_wakeup_timer_cg`

*Class*: executable.

*Description*: The wakeup timer prescaler and the 64-bit threshold. Tool: VCS.

[cols="2,6,4,3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins and crosses |Illegal bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Notes
|`cp_wkup_thold_x_presc` |with `WKUP_CTRL.enable` = 1: prescaler class {0, 1, mid-range 2 to 4094} x threshold class crossing the 32-bit boundary (high half non-zero, count preload 0xFFFF_FFF0) = 3 cells. The small threshold class (high half 0) is filled only by the control `CTL-WKUP-SMALL`, so its three cells are not bins. Extra bins, each with the crossing class: split write order {`WKUP_THOLD_HI` first, `WKUP_THOLD_LO` first} of the two threshold writes before a `WKUP_CTRL` write that sets `enable`, with the `WKUP_THOLD_HI` readback non-zero; `INTR_STATE.wkup_timer_expired` reads 1 after a `WKUP_CTRL` write that sets `enable` and before the next `WKUP_CTRL` write, with the `WKUP_THOLD_HI` readback non-zero (CHK-WKUP-WIDE). The threshold writes of legs S and L (the timer is disabled), the writes and the expiry of the small class, and a status that `INTR_TEST` forces with the timer disabled fill no extra bin (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). Prescaler 4095 and threshold 0 or maximum are not bins. Spec: `aon_timer.adoc`, `WKUP_CTRL`, `WKUP_THOLD_HI`, `WKUP_THOLD_LO`, `WKUP_COUNT_HI`, `WKUP_COUNT_LO` and `INTR_STATE` |None |LSU bus write and read data |`sep_timer_clock_contract_test` |executable. Every leaf of the owning test runs prescaler 0, 1 and a drawn value of 2 to 15, each with the crossing class, and both half orders. The control run `CTL-WKUP-SMALL` runs with the graded window closed
|===

|`cp_refcnt` |(a) after the `rst_ni` pulse of leg R, which follows a readback at or above 2^40, the first read is below 2^40 and the later reads increase (CHK-REFCNT-RESET). The owning test reloads once with each class in every leaf. The cell (low word below 0xFFFF_FFF0, written + 1) is not a bin: the leaf reads within a few `clk_ref_i` edges of the write, which cannot carry from a low word below 0xFFFF_FFF0, and wrap is not drawn. The read before any write is the control of the owning test, and `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test` grades its value, so it is not a bin. [[sep-fcov-p2-reset-scope-retention]]
===== `sep_reset_scope_retention_cg`

*Class*: executable (<<sep-fcov-parked>>).

*Description*: What each reset class clears and what it keeps. Tool: VCS.

[cols="2,6,4,3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins and crosses |Illegal bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Notes
|`cp_reset_class` |class {power-on (`rst_ni`), watchdog reset (`wdt_rst_ni` low, `sep_reset_n` high), per-IP software reset, `dbg_rstb_i`, JTAG TAP reset, JTAG `sep_reset_n` override} = 6 bins. The scan reset is not a bin. A class is seen at its event: `rst_ni` fall; `wdt_rst_ni` fall; `ip_rst_n[i]` fall (i from 1 to 6) with `sep_reset_n` = 1 and the override off; `dbg_rstb_i` fall; `jtag_trst_ni` fall or Test-Logic-Reset; `jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i` = 1 with value 0. A class samples only an event that a graded check of its owner evaluates, as the Owning tests cell lists it. The per-IP software reset class of `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` samples only the `ip_rst_n[D]` fall (the domain D) of an overlap run, so the HMAC hold of its arming step fills no cell. The reset events of calibration, set-up, control and logged-only steps run with the graded window closed and fill no cell (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). Spec: `doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture |None |`rst_ni`, `wdt_rst_ni`, `dbg_rstb_i`, `jtag_trst_ni` (added hook), `jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i`, `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i`, `ip_rst_n[6:1]` |Power-on: `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` (its cold-reset leg, CHK-DMA-COLDRST), `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` (the cold resets of legs R and L, CHK-REFCNT-RESET and CHK-REGWEN-COLD). Watchdog reset: `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` (the WDT_PULSE events of its WDT sequence, overlap leg and DMA leg), `sep_ip_reset_override_matrix_test` (its pulse leg, CHK-OVR-PULSE), `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` (leg L, CHK-REGWEN-HOLD). Per-IP software reset: `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` (the `ip_rst_n[D]` fall of its overlap runs, CHK-OVL), `sep_ip_reset_override_matrix_test` (its priority leg, CHK-OVR-PRIO, and the `SW_RESET_N` = 0x00 write of pass 2, CHK-OVR-IP), `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` (leg L, CHK-REGWEN-HOLD). `dbg_rstb_i`: `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test`, `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` (leg L). JTAG TAP reset: `sep_cpu_reset_entry_test`. JTAG `sep_reset_n` override: `sep_ip_reset_override_matrix_test` (its override leg), `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` (leg L, CHK-REGWEN-HOLD) |executable. The power-on, watchdog reset, per-IP software reset, `dbg_rstb_i` and JTAG `sep_reset_n` override classes have approved inputs. The JTAG TAP reset class uses the TAP pin drive, an added hook (CHK-TRST-SCOPE)
|`cp_bank_class_outcome` |bank {cold, warm} x class {power-on, watchdog reset, per-IP software reset, `dbg_rstb_i`, TAP reset} x outcome {cleared, retained}. Required cells (the outcome the spec allows): software reset and TAP reset retained (both banks) = 4 cells. The watchdog-reset cells (warm cleared, cold retained) are not bins here: the Phase 1 `sep_scratch_reset_cg` scores them. The power-on cleared cells and the `dbg_rstb_i` retained cells are not bins (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). The outcome is the first bank read after the event against the non-zero value written before it. Spec: `doc/cpu.adoc`, Dual Scratch Register Banks; `doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture |Ten cells: cold cleared by watchdog reset, software reset, `dbg_rstb_i` or TAP reset (4); warm cleared by software reset, `dbg_rstb_i` or TAP reset (3); power-on retained (both banks, 2); warm retained after the watchdog reset (1) |LSU bus write and read data, `rst_ni`, `wdt_rst_ni`, `dbg_rstb_i`, `jtag_trst_ni`, `ip_rst_n[6:1]` |`sep_cpu_reset_entry_test` (TAP reset), `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` (per-IP software reset) |executable. CHK-TRST-SCOPE fills the TAP reset cells through the TAP pin drive (added hook). The per-IP software reset cells have approved inputs
|`cp_km_reset_condition` |Key Manager reset {asserted with the Key Manager idle, released} = 2 bins. The in-flight bin is not a bin. Spec: `doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing and Resetting the Key Manager |None |`SEP_CORE.sep_crypto_gated_rst_n`, `SEP_CORE.sep_crypto_isolate_req` |`sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` |executable. `SEP_CORE.sep_crypto_gated_rst_n` bit 0 and the Key Manager isolate vectors are added taps
|`cp_trng_iso_order` |TRNG release = 1 cell: the three isolate-complete bits fall within an environment allowance of the `ip_rst_n[5]` rise. The assert cell (`ip_rst_n[5]` falls only after all three isolate-complete bits are high) is not a bin (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). The specification gives no order among the three paths, so no bin names one. Spec: `doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Resetting the Key Manager and Resetting the TRNG |None |`ip_rst_n[5]`, `trng_axi_isolated_probe_o` |`sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` |executable
|`cp_no_sw_reset_units` |unit {SPI host, DMA engine, watchdog timer, inbound mailbox window, scratch banks} retained across a software engine reset = 5 cells. A cell hits when an armed value reads back unchanged after an engine reset. Four other units of the group (EL2 PIC, traffic filters, alias remap, `sep_cpu_ctrl`) have no owning test. The watchdog timer cell is filled by `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` only (its software engine reset event). Spec: `doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture; `doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing |None |LSU bus write and read data, `ip_rst_n[6:1]` |`sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test`, `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` |executable
|`cp_iso_order_per_domain` |domain {Key Manager, OTBN, AES, KMAC, TRNG} x event {`ip_rst_n[d]` falls with every acknowledge of the domain table set to 1 and the override off; the isolate-complete bits of the set fall within an environment allowance of the `ip_rst_n[d]` rise} = 10 cells; the fall cells of KMAC and TRNG are not bins (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>), so 8 bins. The acknowledge sets are: an engine = its host path and its Key Manager path; Key Manager = five Key Manager paths and the eFuse path; TRNG = three TRNG paths. HMAC and Adams Bridge are bins of the Phase 1 `sep_crypto_isolate_cg` and are not repeated here. Spec: `doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing |`ip_rst_n[d]` falls (override off) while an acknowledge of its set is 0 |`ip_rst_n[6:1]`, `SEP_CORE.sep_crypto_gated_rst_n` bit 0 (added tap), the isolate-complete probes of the KMAC and TRNG paths (approved), `SEP_CORE.sep_crypto_isolated` (the Key Manager, OTBN and AES bits, added tap) |`sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` |executable
|`cp_never_isolated_port` |port {external TRNG window (the read completes with any response except SLVERR with data 0x1501_A7ED), Key Manager mailbox (OKAY), lifecycle block (OKAY), entropy pool `STATUS` (OKAY)} x any approved isolate-complete bit high (HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge, TRNG paths) = 4 cells. Spec: `doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing; `regs/memory_map.adoc` (row External TRNG) |A refusal of the isolated kind (SLVERR with data 0x1501_A7ED) on these ports. An isolate-complete bit that belongs to none of the table sets |LSU bus response and read data, the approved isolate-complete probes |`sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` |executable
|`cp_pulse_vs_sequence` |pulse class {`wdt_rst_ni`, `dbg_rstb_i`} x position against the `SW_RESET_N` assert sequence of the domain D of an overlap run (`ip_rst_n[D]`, started by a write that clears the field of D; a pulse while the HMAC hold keeps `ip_rst_n[3]` low is not a cell) {falls shortly before the write (an environment-chosen band); falls between the write and the `ip_rst_n` fall; falls while `ip_rst_n` = 0 or shortly after its fall (the same band)} = 6 cells. The domain of the sequence is a bench draw from {OTBN, AES, KMAC, Adams Bridge} and is not a bin axis. A `dbg_rstb_i` cell records the stimulus position only; the VPLAN check CHK-OVL takes credit for a `dbg_rstb_i` run only when CHK-DBG-SYNC passes in the same leaf. Spec: `doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing |The sequence is lost: the `ip_rst_n` fall does not follow within an environment timeout |`wdt_rst_ni`, `dbg_rstb_i`, `ip_rst_n[6:1]`, the LSU write data of `SW_RESET_N`, the isolate-complete probes of the KMAC and Adams Bridge paths, the OTBN and AES bits of `SEP_CORE.sep_crypto_isolated` (added tap) |`sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` |executable
|`cp_ovrd_no_iso` |domain {OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, TRNG, Adams Bridge} x override with value 0 asserted while the software bit of the domain is 1: the isolate-complete bits of the domain stay 0 from the override edge to its release = 6 bins. The software assert of each domain (the control step of the owner) raises the isolate-complete bits. It is the control of CHK-OVR-SKIP-ISO and is not a bin. Spec: `doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing; `doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture |an isolate-complete bit rises under an override alone |the isolate-complete probes (HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge, TRNG approved; OTBN and AES added taps), `jtag_sep_reset_ctrl_drive.ovrd` (the `tb_top.sv` net after the pin-or-TDR mux), the LSU write data of `SW_RESET_N` |`sep_ip_reset_override_matrix_test` |executable
|===

[[sep-fcov-p2-crypto-reset]]
===== `sep_crypto_reset_cg` (reset coverpoints)

*Class*: executable (<<sep-fcov-parked>>).

*Description*: Reset independence and override of the crypto engines. The
remaining coverpoints of this group are in the crypto and entropy groups. Tool:
VCS.

[cols="2,6,4,3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins and crosses |Illegal bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Notes
|`cp_independence_pair` |aggressor x victim ordered pairs among {OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, TRNG} = 20 cells. A cell hits in a leg A step with \|S\| of 2 or more (CHK-SUBSET) when the aggressor is in S, the victim is outside S, and the victim's armed value is retained after the release. The single-victim passes of leg B read the other probes as the control `CTL-INDEP` and fill no cell: `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test` (open) grades that a neighbour survives a single-domain reset. The Key Manager and Adams Bridge pairs are not cells of this coverpoint. Spec: `doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture; `doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing |None |LSU bus write and read data, `ip_rst_n[5:1]` |`sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` |executable. Seed-dependent: the leaf draws the partition of leg A, so the cells fill on a merge of regressions (<<sep-fcov-rand>>)
|`cp_ip_ovrd_x_sw` |domain {OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, TRNG, Adams Bridge} x override enable E {0, 1} x override value V {0, 1} x software bit {0, 1}; with E = 0 the V axis is one cell. Per domain 6 cells (E0 x sw0, E0 x sw1, E1 x V0 x sw0, E1 x V0 x sw1, E1 x V1 x sw0, E1 x V1 x sw1) = 36 cells, of which 32 are bins. The four E = 1, V = 0 cells of Adams Bridge and TRNG are graded by `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test` and `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test` (open), and the owning test logs them as `CTL-OVR-IP`, so they are not bins. Expected `ip_rst_n[d]`: with E = 0 it follows the sequenced software value; with E = 1 it equals V. Spec: `doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing; `doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture |`ip_rst_n[d]` differs from the expected value for any cell once the two-flop synchronizer has settled (an environment-chosen settle time of at least three clocks) |`ip_rst_n[6:1]`, the LSU write data of `SW_RESET_N`, `jtag_sep_reset_ctrl_drive.ovrd` and `.val` (the `tb_top.sv` nets after the pin-or-TDR mux) |`sep_ip_reset_override_matrix_test` |executable. The IC_RESET TDR inputs `jtag_ic_reset_*` and the `SepIcResetTdr` driver are approved (SEP slice order in `doc/integrator/src/smu-composition.adoc`, SEP slice; bit pairs in `hw/ip/jtag/jtag_ptap/doc/architecture.adoc`, IC_RESET Support)
|`cp_ovrd_priority` |domain {OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC} x release order {software bit released first, override released first} = 8 cells. The bin hits when `ip_rst_n[d]` stays 0 after the first release and rises after the second. Spec: `doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing |`ip_rst_n[d]` rises after the first release |`ip_rst_n[6:1]`, the LSU write data of `SW_RESET_N`, `jtag_sep_reset_ctrl_drive.ovrd` (the `tb_top.sv` net after the pin-or-TDR mux) |`sep_ip_reset_override_matrix_test` |executable
|`cp_trng_nonmember` |a read of the entropy pool responder returns OKAY while the TRNG reset is active; a read of the external TRNG window completes (any response except the isolated-kind SLVERR) while the TRNG reset is active = 2 cells. Spec: `doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Resetting the TRNG |None |LSU bus response, `trng_gated_rst_n_probe_o`, `trng_axi_isolated_probe_o` |`sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` |executable
|===

[[sep-fcov-p2-debug-trace]]
===== `sep_debug_trace_cg`

*Class*: executable (<<sep-fcov-parked>>).

*Description*: The JTAG TAP, the debug module interface and the CPU trace port.
Tool: VCS.

[cols="2,6,4,3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins and crosses |Illegal bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Notes
|`cp_tap_instruction` |IR value shifted in {0x18 (reset-vector TDR)} = 1 bin. The undefined-opcode bin and the IDCODE opcode are not bins. Spec: `doc/cpu.adoc`, Reset Vector |None |`jtag_tck_i`, `jtag_tms_i`, `jtag_tdi_i` |`sep_cpu_reset_entry_test` |executable. The 0x18 shift of the redirect step of the owner (CHK-RSTVEC) fills the bin, through the TAP pin drive (added hook) and the TAP state tracker
|`cp_rstvec_tdr` |a DR shift while IR = 0x18, shifted through `jtag_tdi_i` to `jtag_tdo_o`; and `dmi_rst_vec` changes at Update-DR = 1 bin each. Spec: `doc/cpu.adoc`, Reset Vector |None |`jtag_tdi_i`, `jtag_tdo_o`, `SEP_CORE.u_sep_cpu.u_el2_veer_wrapper.dmi_rst_vec` |`sep_cpu_reset_entry_test` |executable. The redirect and rewrite shifts of the owner (CHK-RSTVEC) fill both bins, through the TAP pin drive (added hook)
|`cp_dmi_aperture` |`dmi_core_enable_i` {0, 1} x DMI address class {0x00 to 0x4F, 0x50 and above} = 4 cells, sampled at each DMI access (`dmi_en` before the mux). Cells (0, core) and (1, core) are the gate step. Cells (1, upper) and (0, upper) are the uncore step. Spec: `doc/port_table.adoc`, port list (`dmi_*`); `doc/cpu.adoc`, Debug |None |`dmi_core_enable_i`, `SEP_CORE.u_sep_cpu.u_el2_veer_wrapper.dmi_en`, `SEP_CORE.u_sep_cpu.u_el2_veer_wrapper.dmi_addr`, `SEP_CORE.u_sep_cpu.u_el2_veer_wrapper.dmi_wr_en` |`sep_debug_master_target_matrix_test` |executable. CHK-DMI-GATE fills (0, core) and (1, core), and CHK-DMI-UNCORE fills (1, upper) and (0, upper): the owning test runs the 0x50-and-above accesses once with each enable value. `dmi_core_enable_i` and the DTM on the TAP pins are added hooks (top-port drives)
|`cp_traced_event` |event {taken trap (exception = 1, interrupt = 0), interrupt (interrupt = 1, cause 0x1E), NMI (`tlu.mcause` = 0xF000_1000)} = 3 bins. The generic retired-instruction event is not a bin: base-format retirement is graded by `sep_rom_sanity_test` and `sep_boot_rom_smoke_test` (open), and the owning tests read it as a control. Spec: `doc/port_table.adoc`, port list (CPU trace); `doc/interrupts.adoc`, Non-PIC Interrupt Paths and Memory ECC Errors |None |`cpu_trace_valid_o`, `cpu_trace_addr_o`, `cpu_trace_insn_o`, `cpu_trace_ecause_o`, `cpu_trace_interrupt_o`, `dbg_cpu_trace_exc_o`, `SEP_CORE.u_sep_cpu.u_el2_veer_wrapper.veer.dec.tlu.mcause` |`sep_tcm_ecc_cpu_trap_test`, `sep_cpu_isa_pmp_csr_test` |executable. CHK-TCM-CE fills the interrupt bin and CHK-TCM-NMI the NMI bin, with the TCM bit-flip hook (added hook). The taken-trap bin has approved inputs. The trace `ecause` has 5 bits and cannot tell the NMI cause 0xF000_1000 from other causes
|`cp_instr_class` |class of a retired encoding {M, C, Zba, Zbb, Zbc} and class of a trapping encoding {Zbs} = 6 bins. RV32I is not a bin: base-format retirement is graded by `sep_rom_sanity_test` and `sep_boot_rom_smoke_test` (open) and is a control of the owning test. The decode uses the ratified RISC-V bit-manipulation encodings. The SEP spec names the extensions and gives no encodings. Spec: `doc/cpu.adoc`, Processor Core |None |`cpu_trace_valid_o`, `cpu_trace_insn_o`, `dbg_cpu_trace_exc_o` |`sep_cpu_isa_pmp_csr_test` |executable. Zbe is enabled, and Zbf, Zbp and Zbr are disabled. None of them is a bin: the spec gives no encodings and no test executes them. The owner builds its firmware for RV32I outside the table rows and the `bset` word, so no other code of the leaf retires an M, C, Zba, Zbb or Zbc encoding. Its control step after the `bset` trap runs with the graded window closed
|`cp_trace_activity` |a `sep_cpu_reset_n` low interval of a cold reset or a CPU reset with no `cpu_trace_valid_o` beat, followed by a trace beat after the `sep_cpu_reset_n` rise = 1 bin. Trace activity while the CPU runs is not a bin: `sep_boot_rom_smoke_test` and `sep_rom_sanity_test` (open) grade the ROM execution, and the owner uses it as the control of CHK-NO-EARLY-RETIRE (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). Spec: `doc/port_table.adoc`, port list (CPU trace); `doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller |A `cpu_trace_valid_o` beat while `sep_cpu_reset_n` = 0 |`cpu_trace_valid_o`, `sep_cpu_reset_n` |`sep_cpu_reset_entry_test` |executable. The cold resets of the run-request leg of the owner (CHK-NO-EARLY-RETIRE, CHK-RSTVEC-DEFAULT) fill the bin in every leaf
|===

[[sep-fcov-p2-documented-absence]]
===== `sep_documented_absence_cg`

*Class*: executable (<<sep-fcov-parked>>).

*Description*: Behaviour that the specification states as an absence: a ROM
that takes no write, a test-mode output that changes only at a sampling event,
and an uncore debug interface that stays disabled. Tool: VCS.

[cols="2,6,4,3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins and crosses |Illegal bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Notes
|`cp_rom_write_master` |master {CPU} x a store to the ROM aperture is issued: the cell hits when the ROM macro request after the ROM interface shim (`rom_macro_req` with `rom_macro_addr`) at the store word occurs only for the readback loads of that word in the store window of the owner. CHK-ROM-STORE-WE fills the cell. A store on `sep_boot_rom_req` with `wenable` = 1 is not illegal: the store filter is in the shim outside SEP. The DMA, debug and inbound cells have no owning test. Spec: `doc/rom.adoc`, ROM Architecture; `doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc`, Module Overview |A ROM macro request at the store word that is not a readback load |`u_dut.u_sep_ip_integration.rom_macro_req`, `u_dut.u_sep_ip_integration.rom_macro_addr`, `u_dut.sep_boot_rom_req`, `SEP_CORE.lsu_rom_axi_req`, LSU bus |`sep_boot_rom_aperture_sweep_test` |executable
|`cp_rom_write_response` |a write response (B beat) on the CPU LSU bus for a byte or halfword store (a write strobe of 1 or 2 bytes) to the ROM aperture 0x1004_0000 to 0x1004_FFFF, with any of the codes OKAY, SLVERR or DECERR = 1 bin. A store of another size or to another address does not sample. The specification states that a store receives a response and fixes no code, so no bin names a code (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). Spec: `doc/rom.adoc`, ROM Architecture |None |LSU bus AW address, W strobe and B response |`sep_boot_rom_aperture_sweep_test` |executable. The 16 byte and halfword stores of the store step of the owner (CHK-ROM-STORE) fill the bin in every leaf. The word store to the ROM is graded by `sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test` (open) and does not sample
|`cp_secure_tm_state` |the cold-reset clear: with `rst_ni` low and the strap at 1, `secure_tm_o` = 0 at the last `clk_i` rising edge before the release, after a value of 1 = 1 bin. The values of `secure_tm_o` after a sample are not bins: the capture of 1 at the early capture point is a bin of `cp_stm_event_x_strap`, the value after the fuse-sense completion sample is graded by `sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test` (open) and is a control of the owner, and a 0 after the early capture with strap 0 is also the value of the cold-reset clear (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). Spec: `doc/test_mode.adoc`, Entry and Exit |None |`secure_tm_o`, `rst_ni` |`sep_secure_tm_strap_sampling_test` |executable. Only leg A of the owner (CHK-STM-CLEAR) fills the bin, in every leaf. The cold boots that start legs C, D0 and E are set-up reset sequences and run with the graded window closed
|`cp_stm_event_x_strap` |sampling event early capture 1 to 2 clocks after the cold reset release with SEC_DIS asserted x strap 1 = 1 cell; non-sampling event {`wdt_rst_ni`, `dbg_rstb_i`, software engine reset, `sep_reset_n` override} x `secure_tm_o` unchanged = 4 cells; 5 bins. The early capture with strap 0 and the two fuse-sense completion cells are not bins (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). Spec: `doc/test_mode.adoc`, Entry and Exit; `doc/security_disable.adoc`, Reset Persistence |`secure_tm_o` changes outside a sampling event, on a cycle with `rst_ni` = 1. Cycles with `rst_ni` = 0 are not sampled: the cold-reset clear is its own condition of the sampling table (`doc/test_mode.adoc`, Entry and Exit) |`secure_tm_o`, `secure_tm_req_i`, `rst_ni`, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`, `wdt_rst_ni`, `dbg_rstb_i`, `ip_rst_n[3]`, `jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i`, `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i`, `sep_reset_n` (the `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` tap) |`sep_secure_tm_strap_sampling_test` |executable
|`cp_stm_strap_stability` |after a capture, the strap changes 1 to 0 or 0 to 1 and `secure_tm_o` is unchanged for the hold that follows = 1 bin. The cell with the strap unchanged is not a bin: every cycle after a capture fills it, and the retention across the non-sampling events is a bin of `cp_stm_event_x_strap` (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). Spec: `doc/test_mode.adoc`, Entry and Exit |`secure_tm_o` changes after the capture and before the next `rst_ni` fall, on a cycle with `rst_ni` = 1. Cycles with `rst_ni` = 0 are not sampled: the cold-reset clear is its own condition of the sampling table (`doc/test_mode.adoc`, Entry and Exit) |`secure_tm_o`, `secure_tm_req_i`, `rst_ni` |`sep_secure_tm_strap_sampling_test` |executable
|`cp_dmi_uncore` |`dmi_uncore_enable_i` at 0 (the tie-off) x DMI address class {0x50 and above, below 0x50} = 2 never cells. The read and the write at 0x50 with the enable at 1, where `dmi_uncore_en_o` rises (and `dmi_uncore_wr_en_o` for the write), are the control window of CHK-DMI-UNCORE and are not bins (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). The address and write-data outputs and the read-data consumption are not bins. Spec: `doc/port_table.adoc`, port list (`dmi_uncore_*`); `doc/cpu.adoc`, Debug |`dmi_uncore_en_o` = 1 or `dmi_uncore_wr_en_o` = 1 in any cycle with `dmi_uncore_enable_i` = 0 |`dmi_uncore_enable_i`, `dmi_uncore_en_o`, `dmi_uncore_wr_en_o`, `SEP_CORE.u_sep_cpu.u_el2_veer_wrapper.dmi_addr` |`sep_debug_master_target_matrix_test` |executable. CHK-DMI-UNCORE fills both cells. `dmi_uncore_enable_i` and the DTM on the TAP pins are added hooks (top-port drives)
|===

[[sep-fcov-p2-cpu-ecc-irq]]
===== `sep_cpu_ecc_irq_cg`

*Class*: executable (<<sep-fcov-parked>>).

*Description*: Memory ECC errors on the CPU path: the correctable-error counter
and its interrupt, the trap causes of uncorrectable errors, the NMI fast path,
and the debug-bus pulses. Tool: VCS.

[cols="2,6,4,3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins and crosses |Illegal bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Notes
|`cp_ce_counter` |memory {ICCM, DCCM} x count position against `2^t` {below, at or above} x `mie[30]` {0, 1}: DCCM x 2 x 2 = 4 cells, ICCM x 2 x {0} = 2 cells. The counter is `tlu.mdccmect[26:0]` or `tlu.miccmect[26:0]`, and t is bits [31:27]. The request is `tlu.ce_int`. Spec: `doc/interrupts.adoc`, Correctable (single-bit) errors |`ce_int` = 1 with the count below `2^t`. `ce_int` = 0 with the count at or above `2^t` |`SEP_CORE.u_sep_cpu.u_el2_veer_wrapper.veer.dec.tlu.{mdccmect, miccmect, ce_int, mie}` |`sep_tcm_ecc_cpu_trap_test` |executable. CHK-TCM-CE fills the cells: in the DCCM leg the samples below and at or above `2^t` with `mie[30]` clear, the armed sample at the count 0 and the arming at or above `2^t` with `mie[30]` set; in the ICCM leg the sample before the block (below `2^t`) and the quiescent sample after it (at or above `2^t`). It runs the ICCM leg and the DCCM leg in every leaf, so every cell fills in every leaf
|`cp_ce_clamp` |threshold exponent written {0 to 5 (one bin that the drawn t fills in every leaf), above 26 (written 31 reads 26)} = 2 bins. Spec: `doc/interrupts.adoc`, Correctable (single-bit) errors |None |`SEP_CORE.u_sep_cpu.u_el2_veer_wrapper.veer.dec.tlu.{mdccmect, miccmect}` |`sep_tcm_ecc_cpu_trap_test` |executable. CHK-TCM-CLAMP fills both bins and needs no flip
|`cp_mceip_delivery` |`tlu.mcause` = 0x8000_001E taken once per arming with `mie[30]` = 1 and `mstatus.MIE` = 1 (the handler clears `mie[30]` before `mret`, because `MCEIP` stays asserted while the count is at or above the threshold); no trap with `mie[30]` = 0 while `ce_int` = 1 = 2 bins. Spec: `doc/interrupts.adoc`, Correctable (single-bit) errors |None |`SEP_CORE.u_sep_cpu.u_el2_veer_wrapper.veer.dec.tlu.{mcause, ce_int, mie}`, `cpu_trace_valid_o` |`sep_tcm_ecc_cpu_trap_test` |executable. CHK-TCM-CE fills the delivery bin and CHK-TCM-SILENT the no-trap bin
|`cp_dma_ecc_response` |an externally initiated read {single-bit error: corrected data; double-bit error: not OKAY} = 2 bins. The response code of the single-bit read is not a bin (the spec does not state it for the DMA path). The same sample feeds `cp_tcm_ecc`. Spec: `doc/interrupts.adoc`, Correctable (single-bit) errors and Uncorrectable (double-bit) errors |None |`SEP_CORE.cpu_tcm_axi_resp` |`sep_tcm_dma_aperture_ecc_test` |executable. CHK-TCM-SBE fills the single-bit bin and CHK-TCM-DBE the double-bit bin
|`cp_tcm_trap_cause` |access {DCCM load, DCCM store} x `tlu.mcause` {5, 7} x `tlu.mscause` {1} = 2 cells. The ICCM fetch cell is blocked (see the VPLAN Blocked features). Spec: `doc/interrupts.adoc`, Uncorrectable (double-bit) errors |Either of these two traps with `mscause` other than 1 |`SEP_CORE.u_sep_cpu.u_el2_veer_wrapper.veer.dec.tlu.{mcause, mscause}` |`sep_tcm_ecc_cpu_trap_test` |executable. CHK-TCM-UE fills both cells
|`cp_nmi_fast_path` |`tlu.mcause` = 0xF000_1000 taken, and the next fetch address equals `SEP_CORE.nmi_vec` (the `SEP_NMI_VEC` value) = 1 bin. Spec: `doc/interrupts.adoc`, Uncorrectable (double-bit) errors |None |`SEP_CORE.u_sep_cpu.u_el2_veer_wrapper.veer.dec.tlu.mcause`, `SEP_CORE.nmi_vec`, `cpu_trace_addr_o` |`sep_tcm_ecc_cpu_trap_test` |executable. CHK-TCM-NMI fills the bin
|`cp_ecc_dbg_pulse` |`ext_debug_bus_o` bit {367 ICCM single, 365 DCCM single, 364 DCCM double} pulse seen on a CPU access = 3 bins. Bit 366 (ICCM double) is not a bin: no test drives an uncorrectable ICCM error on the CPU path. Pulses on a DMA-path access are not bins (the spec does not state that DMA-path errors drive the mirror). Spec: `doc/interrupts.adoc`, Debug bus mirror |A pulse wider than one `clk_i`. A double-bit event with a single-bit pulse. A single-bit event with a double-bit pulse |`ext_debug_bus_o[367:364]` |`sep_tcm_ecc_cpu_trap_test` |executable. CHK-TCM-DBG fills the three bins. `ext_debug_bus_o` is approved; it is 384 bits wide in the build and 512 bits in the port table, and the coverpoint indexes bits 367:364 only
|`cp_ecc_no_pic` |a corrected-error event (a pulse on bit 365 or 367) with `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` unchanged in the 4 clocks around the pulse (a window that is a choice of the environment) = 1 bin. Spec: `doc/interrupts.adoc`, Timer and Software Interrupts and Memory ECC Errors |Any bit of the vector changes because of the event |`ext_debug_bus_o[367:364]`, `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` |`sep_tcm_ecc_cpu_trap_test` |executable. The vector clause of CHK-TCM-NO-PIC fills the bin
|===

[[sep-fcov-p2-fabric-dedicated-port]]
===== `sep_fabric_dedicated_port_cg` (CPU path coverpoints)

*Class*: executable.

*Description*: Refused CPU requests at the TCM and the extension port. The
remaining coverpoints of this group are in the fabric groups. Tool: VCS.

[cols="2,6,4,3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins and crosses |Illegal bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Notes
|`cp_tcm_refused_initiator` |initiator x direction x range cells: CPU IFU read ICCM, CPU LSU read ICCM, CPU LSU write ICCM, CPU LSU read DCCM, CPU LSU write DCCM = 5 cells. CHK-CORE-PATH-XBAR fills every cell: the ICCM marker run and the DCCM accesses, and the LSU load and store at the ICCM word. The observation point is `cpu_tcm_axi_req` (the crossbar backdoor). It is not `sep_cpu_tcm_req_o`, the macro port that the core drives for its own accesses. Spec: `doc/cpu.adoc`, CPU; `doc/rom.adoc`, ROM Architecture |Any request on `cpu_tcm_axi_req` that these cells cause |`SEP_CORE.cpu_tcm_axi_req`, `SEP_CORE.ifu_rom_axi_req`, `SEP_CORE.lsu_rom_axi_req` |`sep_cpu_demux_unmapped_fetch_test` |executable
|`cp_ext_refused_ifu` |a CPU IFU fetch at an address U outside ROM and SRAM: no request with address U on `axi_extension_axi_req` or on `smn_outbound_axi_req_o` in the fetch window of the owner = 1 cell. CHK-IFU-NEVER fills it. The marker stores to 0x8000_0000 in the same window are not requests for U. The CPU LSU read at 0x2000_0100 that appears on `axi_extension_axi_req` is the control of CHK-IFU-NEVER and is not a bin. Spec: `doc/cpu.adoc`, CPU; `doc/fabric.adoc`, Fabric Topology |None |`u_dut.axi_extension_axi_req`, `smn_outbound_axi_req_o`, `SEP_CORE.u_sep_cpu.ifu_axi_req` |`sep_cpu_demux_unmapped_fetch_test` |executable
|===

[[sep-fcov-p2-periph-reset-state]]
===== `sep_periph_reset_state_cg`, `sep_dma_containment_cg` and `sep_fault_source_ack_cg` (reset-retention coverpoints)

*Class*: `sep_periph_reset_state_cg` executable; `sep_dma_containment_cg` executable in part; `sep_fault_source_ack_cg` executable (<<sep-fcov-parked>>).

*Description*: DMA register reset values, the DMA lock across resets, and the
bus-error latches across resets. The remaining coverpoints of these groups are
in the SPI and DMA and the mailbox, IRQ and PIC groups. Tool: VCS.

[cols="2,2,6,4,3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Group |Bins and crosses |Illegal bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Notes
|`cp_dma_reset_value` |`sep_periph_reset_state_cg` |one bin per DMA register that the owning test reads at a value other than its reset value before the cold reset, read after the `rst_ni` release and before any DMA configuration write: `SRC_ADDR_LO`, `DST_ADDR_LO`, `ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_BASE`, `ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_LIMIT`, `RANGE_VALID`, `RANGE_REGWEN`, `TOTAL_DATA_SIZE`, `CHUNK_DATA_SIZE` and `HANDSHAKE_INTR_ENABLE` = 9 bins. The other readable locations keep their reset value before the reset and are not bins (<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned>>). Spec: `secure_dma.adoc`, register list |None |LSU read data |`sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` |executable
|`cp_dma_nonzero_reset` |`sep_periph_reset_state_cg` |`RANGE_REGWEN` (0x6, from the cleared value 0x0) and `HANDSHAKE_INTR_ENABLE` (0x7FF, from the written 0) = 2 bins. `ADDR_SPACE_ID`, `CFG_REGWEN` and `TRANSFER_WIDTH` are not bins: the owner does not move them before the reset. Spec: `secure_dma.adoc`, reset column of `RANGE_REGWEN` and `HANDSHAKE_INTR_ENABLE` |None |LSU read data |`sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` |executable
|`cp_dma_failsecure` |`sep_periph_reset_state_cg` |`RANGE_VALID` = 0, `RANGE_REGWEN` = 0x6, handshake mask open = 3 bins, each from a value that the owner reads before the reset. `CONTROL.GO` = 0 is not a bin: the owner never reads `GO` at 1 before the reset. Spec: `secure_dma.adoc`, reset column of `RANGE_VALID`, `RANGE_REGWEN` and `HANDSHAKE_INTR_ENABLE` |None |LSU read data |`sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` |executable
|`cp_dma_lock_recovery` |`sep_dma_containment_cg` |`RANGE_REGWEN` cleared value 0x0 (a write of 0 to the RW0C field from 0x6): unchanged across `wdt_rst_ni` and `dbg_rstb_i` pulses (1 bin); restored to 0x6 after `rst_ni` (1 bin) = 2 bins. The bins grade the stored value only. The specification does not state that 0x0 applies the lock, so no bin claims a lock effect (VPLAN Blocked legs, DMA RANGE_REGWEN false value). Spec: `secure_dma.adoc`, `RANGE_REGWEN` (RW0C, reset 0x6); `regs/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` (a CPU-only reset does not reset the DMA) |None |LSU read data, `wdt_rst_ni`, `dbg_rstb_i`, `rst_ni` |`sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` |executable. The test clears `RANGE_REGWEN` to 0x0 before the pulses
|`cp_latch_x_reset_kind` |`sep_fault_source_ack_cg` |latch {`DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS.reg_path_err`, the `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS` bit of the drawn block L (`kmac` or `otbn`)} x reset kind {CPU-only (`wdt_rst_ni`), full SEP (`rst_ni`)} = 4 cells: a CPU-only reset keeps the latch and a full reset clears it. Spec: `regs/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` and `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS` |None |LSU read data, `wdt_rst_ni`, `rst_ni`, `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` |`sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` |executable
|`cp_boot_consequence` |`sep_fault_source_ack_cg` |source 41 or source 43 pending right after a CPU-only reset with no new fault = 2 cells (aggregator bits 40 and 42). Spec: `doc/interrupts.adoc`, SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map |None |`sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`, `wdt_rst_ni` |`sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` |executable
|===

[[sep-fcov-p2-cpu-ctrl-timeout-inert]]
===== `sep_cpu_ctrl_timeout_inert_cg` (write-inert coverpoint)

*Class*: executable (<<sep-fcov-parked>>).

*Description*: Registers that accept a write and have no effect. The remaining
coverpoints of this group are in the mailbox, IRQ and PIC groups. Tool: VCS.

[cols="2,6,4,3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins and crosses |Illegal bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Notes
|`cp_timeout_write_inert` |register {`TIMEOUT_CLEAR` (0x70), `TIMEOUT_MODE` (0x78)} x access {write} = 2 cells. The `TIMEOUT_INTERRUPT` read after both writes is a logged observation, not a bin. The other ten registers are bins of `x_reg_dir` in the mailbox, IRQ and PIC definition of this group, owned by `sep_cpu_ctrl_timeout_inert_rand_test`. Spec: `regs/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `TIMEOUT_CLEAR`, `TIMEOUT_MODE`, `TIMEOUT_INTERRUPT` |None |LSU bus write and read data |`sep_cpu_ctrl_reg_shape_test` |executable
|===

[[sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-unowned]]
===== Bins not graded

These bins belong to a group above. They are not in the group, for the reason
given. No stimulus reaches them, the specification does not define their
value, an open test grades them and the owning Phase 3 test runs them as a
control only, or only a control, a set-up step or unrelated traffic of the
owner reaches them.

[cols="2,4,4",options="header"]
|===
|Group |Bins not graded |Reason
|`sep_scratch_reg_access_cg`
|`cp_neighbour` (a neighbour register keeps its value across a write) and `cp_reset_value` (the first read of a bank after a reset that clears it)
|`sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test` (open) grades the distinct value of every register and the clear and retain across the resets. `sep_cpu_ctrl_reg_shape_test` reads both as controls (`CTL-SCR-NEIGHBOUR`, `CTL-SCR-SEQ`).
|`sep_reset_source_cg`
|`rst_ni` assertion width (shorter than one period, exactly one period), assertion with a stopped clock, `wdt_rst_ni` phase classes other than idle, `cp_rst_assert_async` (the `sep_reset_n` fall against the `clk_i` edges after the `rst_ni` fall)
|No owning test. The specification states no width rule. The `rst_ni` fall reaches `u_sep_reset_sync` through the eFuse wrapper synchronizer that makes `sep_intermediate_reset_n`, and the specification gives no assertion timing for that synchronizer, so `sep_cold_reset_phase_test` logs the assertion delay as `OBS-RST-ASSERT` and no bin grades it.
|`sep_clk_domain_cg`
|Primary clock state, ratio class, stopped-domain class, `entropy_rosc_sample_clk_i`, the `clk_wdt_i` phase quartile at the completion of a watchdog-window access
|No owning test. The `entropy_rosc_sample_clk_i` cell belongs to the crypto and entropy groups. A leaf sets only the issue time of a watchdog-window access, and the specification states no relation between the issue time and the `clk_i` edge that completes a `clk_wdt_i` crossing, so no stimulus places that edge in a chosen quartile.
|`sep_boot_milestone_cg`
|Outbound SMC access safety cells, BL0 strap read ordering, OTBN verifier load, BL1 transfer stage, the milestone list (BL0 firmware is out of scope), the `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` = 0 cell, the halt cells (`mpc_reset_run_req_i` = 0) of the run request, the `lc_state_o` value after sense, the stability of `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` after its rise, `cp_sense_surface` (the `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` port before and after sense, and `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` at 1)
|No owning test, or out of scope. The rise of the port and the first `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` read are graded by `sep_efuse_sense_test` and `sep_efuse_image_test` (open); `sep_boot_release_terms_test` reads them as controls and logs its status polls as `OBS-SENSE-POLL`. The specification does not state that the port stays high after the rise, so no bin grades it. The port table gives "run/halt selection after reset" and no value-to-action map, so the halt cells have no stated result. The port table gives `lc_state_o` as differentially encoded and states no layout.
|`sep_mem_aperture_cg`
|Classes "past the end" and "reserved window", ROM x double, the TCM access sizes, interface concurrency, the TCM response phase, the exclusivity of the ICCM and DCCM clock enables (`cp_tcm_req_exclusive`), the SRAM first and last word of `cp_mem_x_class`, the SRAM sizes of `cp_access_size`, the 32-bit ROM first and last word loads of `cp_mem_x_class`, ROM x word of `cp_access_size`
|No owning test. `sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test` (open) grades 32-bit ROM loads of the base and top words; `sep_boot_rom_aperture_sweep_test` runs them as controls. The TCM macro response type has no valid field, and the specification gives no latency, so the TCM response phase cannot be sampled. The specification states no rule that an access of one TCM leaves the other clock enable low. The SRAM first and last word and the SRAM sizes are graded by `sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test` and `sep_sram_smoke_test` (open); `sep_sram_axi_shape_matrix_test` reaches them only in its control `CTL-SRAM-REACH`.
|`sep_reset_sw_ctrl_cg`
|Key Manager access class; the km, otbn, aes, kmac, trng and abr cells of `cp_power_up_value`
|No owning test. `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` arms `SW_RESET_N` = 0x76 before its graded cold reset, so only the `hmac` field moves; the other six fields hold their reset value through the reset (`OBS-SWRST-COLD-UNMOVED`), and the power-on value of every field is graded by `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` and `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test` (open).
|`sep_reset_scope_retention_cg`
|The power-on cleared cells (both banks) and the `dbg_rstb_i` retained cells (both banks) of `cp_bank_class_outcome`
|`sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test` (open) grades the clear of both banks at a cold reset. `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` reads the banks after each event as the liveness control `CTL-BANK` and grades no bank outcome, so no checker grades the banks after a `dbg_rstb_i` pulse.
|`sep_reset_scope_retention_cg`
|The reset-fall cells of KMAC and TRNG of `cp_iso_order_per_domain`; the assert cell of `cp_trng_iso_order`
|`sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test` and `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test` (open) grade the order and the drain of the KMAC and TRNG domains. `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` logs the same measurement as the control `CTL-ISO-ORDER` and grades only the release (CHK-ISO-REL).
|`sep_periph_reset_state_cg`
|The DMA register locations of `cp_dma_reset_value` that the owner does not move before the cold reset; the `ADDR_SPACE_ID`, `CFG_REGWEN` and `TRANSFER_WIDTH` cells of `cp_dma_nonzero_reset`; the `CONTROL.GO` cell of `cp_dma_failsecure`
|`sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` holds these registers at their reset value before the cold reset (it logs them as `OBS-DMA-COLD-UNMOVED`), so a missing reset cannot show. `CFG_REGWEN` is managed by hardware, and the specification states no value of it or of `CONTROL.GO` during a transfer.
|`sep_wdt_config_bark_cg`
|Pet window, 64-bit and byte adaptation classes, write in the same tick as a threshold crossing, write to `WDOG_COUNT` while the counter advances, the count advance per `clk_wdt_i` period
|The register write that is a pet, and its effect after a bark, are not stated. The count unit of `WDOG_COUNT` is not stated by the frozen specification.
|`sep_wdt_wakeup_timer_cg`
|Threshold 0 and maximum, prescaler 4095, prescaler restart, split-read carry, clear and force of the expiry status
|The specification states no result, or no owning test drives it.
|`sep_wdt_escalation_fault_cg`
|Bark response, count position, bite duration, NMI vector state at bark; `cp_bite_request` (the bite request asserted after an unanswered bark, and not asserted with the watchdog disabled)
|No owning test. `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` (open, CHK-BITE) grades the bite request before enable and after an unanswered bark; `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` grades the threshold relation of bark and bite in `cp_thold_order`.
|`sep_cpu_ctrl_reg_cg`
|`RAS_BANK_INFO`, chiplet identity, NMI target class, NMI programming order, TRNG source select and lock, the cell (low word below 0xFFFF_FFF0, written + 1) of `cp_refcnt_write_class`
|No owning test, or the cell cannot be reached by the stimulus of the owning test.
|`sep_scratch_reg_access_cg`
|Data pattern (all zeros, all ones, walking one, walking zero)
|The owning test writes unique non-zero random values.
|`sep_cpu_ecc_irq_cg`
|The ICCM double-bit pulse (bit 366) of `cp_ecc_dbg_pulse`; mirror pulses on a DMA-path access
|The register text and the interrupt chapter differ for an uncorrectable ICCM error. The specification does not state that DMA-path errors drive the mirror.
|`sep_debug_trace_cg`
|Undefined TAP opcode, IDCODE opcode, DMI access path by lifecycle state, `dmi_active_o` (the specification states only "DMI active indicator"), the hazard classes (back-to-back dependency, load-use) and branch mispredict (`cp_hazard`), Zbe, Zbf, Zbp, Zbr
|The specification states no value, defines no hazard class, no observation point exists, or no encodings are given.
|`sep_documented_absence_cg`
|The response codes OKAY, SLVERR and DECERR of the ROM store as separate bins; the early capture with strap 0 and the two fuse-sense completion cells of `cp_stm_event_x_strap`
|The specification states that a store receives a response and fixes no code. After the early capture with strap 0, `secure_tm_o` = 0 is also the value of the cold-reset clear, so the cell cannot show a missing capture. The sampling at fuse-sense completion is graded by `sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test` (open) and is a control of `sep_secure_tm_strap_sampling_test`.
|`sep_static_tieoff_cg`
|The `core_id` tie-off cell (no SEP port), the `jtag_id_i` cells and the IDCODE readback (the IDCODE contract is on the blocked list)
|No SEP port, or the contract is not stated.
|`sep_reset_source_cg`
|The arrival bins of `cp_release_order` before the `rst_ni` rise, in the `clk_i` cycle of the `rst_ni` rise, after the `rst_ni` rise and before the `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` rise, and within K cycles after the `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` rise
|In these arrivals the sensing or a short delay is the last term, so a design that ignores `ext_boot_seq_done_i` releases at the same time as a compliant one. `sep_boot_release_terms_test` runs them in its drawn epoch as controls of CHK-REL-GATE, and `sep_sec_dis_override_test` and `sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test` (open) grade the sense-only release.
|`sep_mem_aperture_cg`
|The SRAM x master reach cells of `cp_requesting_master`; the INCR cells of `cp_sram_burst` and its multi-beat cells on the CPU-LSU master; the IFU ROM-port and SRAM-port cells of `cp_demux_target`; the DMA copy to DCCM of `cp_core_internal_path`; the no-error cells of `cp_tcm_ecc`
|`sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test`, `sep_sram_smoke_test` and `sep_sram_inbound_burst_attr_test` (open) grade the SRAM reach and the INCR lengths, and `sep_sram_burst_type_test` (open) grades the multi-beat FIXED and WRAP bursts of the CPU-LSU master. `sep_sram_axi_shape_matrix_test` reaches the SRAM with INCR accesses only in `CTL-SRAM-REACH` and in set-up writes. The open boot tests grade the ROM fetch, and `sep_cpu_demux_unmapped_fetch_test` runs the ROM and SRAM fetches as the control of CHK-IFU-DECERR and the DMA copy to DCCM as the control of CHK-CORE-PATH-XBAR. The clean TCM reads of `sep_tcm_dma_aperture_ecc_test` are the control of its error cells.
|`sep_fault_source_ack_cg`
|`cp_ack_discipline` and `cp_agg_can_fall` (the fall of aggregator bits 40 and 42 after a write to `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR.clr` and to the `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_CLEAR` bit of the latched block)
|`sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` (open) grades the clear of `reg_path_err` and of a peripheral block bit with the fall of bits 40 and 42, and `sep_cpu_bridge_fault_matrix_rand_test` grades the clear matrix of both registers. `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` uses the fall after its clear writes as the control of CHK-AGG-SURVIVE.
|`sep_wdt_config_bark_cg`
|The cells regwen 0 x ignored and the cells regwen 1 x landed before the lock, of `cp_lock_x_reg`; the write and read cells of `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` and `WDOG_BITE_THOLD` and the write cell of `WDOG_CTRL` while the counter advances, of `cp_access_x_register`
|`sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test` (open, CHK-REGWEN-LOCK) grades the ignored writes under the lock, and `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` reads them as the control `CTL-REGWEN-LOCKED`. The other cells come only from set-up writes and from the controls `CTL-WDT-THOLD` and `CTL-WDT-ENABLE` of the owner.
|`sep_documented_absence_cg`
|The values of `secure_tm_o` after a sample (`cp_secure_tm_state`); the strap-unchanged cell of `cp_stm_strap_stability`
|The capture of 1 at the early capture point is a bin of `cp_stm_event_x_strap`. `sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test` (open) grades the value after the fuse-sense completion sample, and `sep_secure_tm_strap_sampling_test` reads it as a control. A 0 after the early capture with strap 0 is also the value of the cold-reset clear. Every cycle after a capture fills the strap-unchanged cell, so the cell records no event.
|`sep_debug_trace_cg`
|Trace activity while the CPU runs (`cp_trace_activity`); the two accesses with `dmi_uncore_enable_i` at 1, of `cp_dmi_uncore`
|`sep_boot_rom_smoke_test` and `sep_rom_sanity_test` (open) grade the ROM execution, and `sep_cpu_reset_entry_test` uses it as the control of CHK-NO-EARLY-RETIRE. The accesses with the uncore enable at 1 are the control window of CHK-DMI-UNCORE.
|`sep_fabric_dedicated_port_cg`, `sep_reset_scope_retention_cg`
|The extension-port LSU read of `cp_ext_refused_ifu`; the software assert of `cp_ovrd_no_iso`
|Each is the control of the checker that grades the coverpoint (CHK-IFU-NEVER, CHK-OVR-SKIP-ISO).
|`sep_reset_source_cg`
|The `rst_ni` edges of the bring-up reset and of the control pass of `sep_cold_reset_phase_test` (`cp_source_edge`, `cp_rst_release_sync`)
|The control pass releases at a fixed phase after a `clk_i` edge and is the control of CHK-RST-SYNC. The bring-up reset falls before the leaf opens its graded window.
|`sep_reset_scope_retention_cg`
|The reset events of `cp_reset_class` that no check of the owner grades: the `SW_RESET_N` writes of the calibration and arming steps of `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test`; the control of step 4, the set-up write that clears the field of d1 in the pulse leg and the `rst_ni` control of `sep_ip_reset_override_matrix_test`; the recovery reset of a watchdog-run baseline (`OBS-WDT-BITE-HOLD`) of `sep_timer_clock_contract_test`
|Each event places stimulus, restores a state or is logged only. The owner runs it with the graded window closed.
|`sep_boot_milestone_cg`
|The (OKAY, 1) read of `cp_presense_window` for a word with no SLVERR answer before it in the same boot
|Every read of the shadow map after sensing gives OKAY, so the cell does not show the pre-sense window.
|`sep_wdt_config_bark_cg`
|The clear of a threshold bark by the baseline write of a watchdog run (`cp_bark_status`); the count reads of a watchdog run (`cp_access_x_register`)
|The baseline clear is a set-up step. CHK-WDT-ORDER grades the threshold relation of the watchdog-run reads, not the coherence of a count read.
|`sep_wdt_wakeup_timer_cg`
|The split write order and the expiry of the small threshold class; the threshold writes of legs S and L; the expiry status that `INTR_TEST` forces (`cp_wkup_thold_x_presc`)
|`CTL-WKUP-SMALL` is a control. Legs S and L write the thresholds with the timer disabled. CHK-WKUP-WIDE grades the crossing class only.
|`sep_documented_absence_cg`
|The cold-reset clear of `cp_secure_tm_state` in the cold boots that start legs C, D0 and E
|These boots are set-up reset sequences. CHK-STM-CLEAR grades the clear of leg A.
|`sep_cpu_ctrl_reg_cg`, `sep_aperture_reserved_access_cg`, `sep_scratch_reg_access_cg`
|The read value of the reserved bits of `SEP_VERSION_ID`, `SEP_NMI_VEC`, `SW_RESET_N` and `SCRATCH[n]` (`cp_version_id`, `cp_nmi_vec`, `cp_reserved_field_write`, `cp_bank_reg_access`)
|The register descriptions give these bits access "-" and reset "-" and state no read value. The owning tests log them (`OBS-CC-RSVD`, `OBS-SWRST-RSVD`).
|===

[[sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-sampler]]
===== Sampler contract for these groups

The sampler and the bench provide the following. None of them changes the DUT
logic. A read-only sampler input outside the closed set of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
is an added tap. A drive of a `sep` port or an error inject outside that set
is an added hook. A drive, force or deposit of a net inside `sep` for a purpose
other than error injection, outside that set, is PROPOSED.

[cols="1,3,2",options="header"]
|===
|Item |Contract |Class
|Time unit |`sep_fcov.sv` carries `` `timescale 1ps/1fs ``. The 1 ps comparisons of `cp_rst_release_sync`, `cp_aon_release_sync` and `cp_scan_net_effect` need it |approved
|Ungated reset-edge sampling |The reset-edge coverpoints use blocks with no `in_reset` term, because the Phase 1 gate holds every Phase 1 group |approved
|AXI shape inputs |The sampler takes AxLEN, AxBURST, AxSIZE, the W strobe and the `m_axi` W channel as inputs, and a write-completion wire. They come from the approved `s_axi_*` and `m_axi_*` TB inputs. `cp_sram_burst`, `cp_requesting_master`, `cp_rom_write_response` and `cp_reserved_field_write` need them |approved
|Sampler ports of approved nets |One sampler input for each net of <<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-paths>> of the class "approved". `tb/tb_top.sv` connects each input read-only |approved
|Sampler ports of added taps |One input for each net of <<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-paths>> of the class "added tap". `tb/tb_top.sv` connects each input read-only, and the owning test adds the net to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list |added tap
|TAP state tracker |A shift-register model of the TAP from the `jtag_tms_i` history, `jtag_tck_i`, `jtag_tdi_i` and `jtag_tdo_o`. It reads no RTL net. `cp_source_edge` (Test-Logic-Reset), `cp_tap_instruction` and `cp_rstvec_tdr` need it |approved model that reads the TAP pin drive, an added hook
|Approved bench drives |`rst_ni`, `ext_boot_seq_done_i`, `mpc_reset_run_req`, `wdt_rst_ni_i`, `dbg_rstb_i`, `test_en_strap_i`, the clocks `clk_i`, `clk_wdt_i` and `clk_ref_i` with a chosen period and start phase, the IC_RESET TDR inputs `jtag_ic_reset_*`, `rst_vec_i`, `tcm_load_i`, ROM image initialisation, and the plusargs `+skip_fuse_sense` and `+sep_smc_fuse_sense_hold` |approved
|Added bench drives and injects |Top-port drives: `test_en_i` and `scan_rst_ni`; `dmi_core_enable_i` and `dmi_uncore_enable_i` and a JTAG DTM driver; the TAP pins `jtag_tck_i`, `jtag_tms_i`, `jtag_tdi_i` and `jtag_trst_ni` as TB inputs. Error injects: the TCM bit-flip hook at `u_dut.u_sep_ip_integration.u_sep_tcm_wrapper`; the ROM macro bit-flip hook. The two bit-flip hooks are stimulus only, and nothing samples them |added hook
|===

[[sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset-paths]]
===== Sampled signal paths

Class:

* *approved*: the net is listed in `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` or exists in
  `tb/tb_top.sv`. The sampler adds a read-only input where it has none.
* *added tap*: a read-only reference to a DUT net or port that
  `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` does not list and that the testbench does not tap. The
  owning test adds it to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. It drives
  nothing, and a bin that samples it scores.
* *added hook*: a top-port drive (a drive of a port of the `sep` module) or an
  error inject that `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` does not list and that the testbench
  does not have. The owning test adds it to the HDL Top port table or to the
  inject exceptions of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, and a bin that needs it scores.
* *PROPOSED*: a drive, force or deposit of a net inside the `sep` module for a
  purpose other than error injection, outside the closed set of
  `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` and not in the testbench. A bin that needs it does not
  score.
* *Permanent hole*: no net, port or register exists, so no coverpoint can fail
  on it.
