[[sep-fcov-p2-efuse-lifecycle-notdeclared]]
===== Bins not graded

A declared cell that no stimulus can fill, or whose value the specification does
not define, is a dead bin. The groups above do not declare these cells.

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Cell or scenario |Reason
|Active level of `sep_fuse_dft_disable_o` and `smc_fuse_dft_disable_o` (the port value enters `x_sep_port` and `x_smc_port` only relative to the all-open cell, and no `cp_port_level` coverpoint is declared) |The specification does not state the polarity of the two ports, so no bin names a level
|Response past the interface-control extent (`0x1093_041C` to `0x1093_04FF`) in `x_win_op_resp` |`sep_unmapped_access_policy_test` grades that refusal, and `sep_efuse_window_decode_reset_test` logs the response as a control
|Write cells of `EFUSE_READ_REQ_TIMEOUT` and `EFUSE_PROGRAM_REQ_TIMEOUT` in `x_ctrl_reg_op`, and the `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` read in `x_token_blk_op` |`sep_unmapped_access_policy_test` grades the timeout write and readback and the reset value of `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT`; `sep_efuse_window_decode_reset_test` logs them as controls, and the sticky bits belong to `sep_token_fault_cg`
|Response of the reserved window `0x1093_0600` to `0x1093_FFFF` in `x_win_op_resp` |No cited source states the CPU-side response of that span: `memory_map.adoc` states the inbound AXI port view, and `otp_fuse_controller.adoc` sends the span to the shim bank control registers
|Guard flag bits of `ext_debug_bus_o` (shadow read-locked, program-locked, read-locked, secure-test blocked) |The specification names the bus and no bit of it, so no bin uses a flag. The `secure_tm`-blocked flag coverpoint `cp_blocked_flag` is not declared
|TRoot as a source in `sep_efuse_secure_tm_cg` |The specification does not define TRoot, and the bench has no port or register for it
|Frontdoor write of the four protection shadows under each strap (no `x_write_block` coverpoint is declared) |`sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test` grades the frontdoor write block, and `sep_efuse_secure_tm_matrix_rand_test` runs those writes only as controls (CHK-STM-CTL)
|RMA_CHIPLET cell of `x_state_demote_forced` |The RMA_CHIPLET profile row is all ones with and without SEC_DIS, so a forced match cannot differ from the row; `sep_lcc_feat_ctrl_state_matrix_rand_test` logs that cell as a control (`ctl=1`)
|Secure test mode JTAG write in PROD and RMA_SIP |The lifecycle gate refuses every JTAG shadow write, so a refusal and a control cannot differ
|Secure test mode JTAG write of the four protection shadows in TEST_DEV, under each strap (no `x_jtag_write_block` coverpoint is declared) |A strap-1 cell counts only after the strap-0 JTAG write to the same target landed, and `sep_efuse_secure_tm_matrix_rand_test` runs its strap-1 epoch before its strap-0 epoch, so no strap-1 cell can fill at its sample. The strap-0 write is the control of CHK-STM-JTAG and fills no graded cell. CHK-STM-JTAG grades the strap-1 block
|(SEC_DIS channel, all-zero shape) in `x_ch_shape` |The bound SEC_DIS token is zero, so that token matches
|(SEC_DIS channel, common-mode flip) in `x_ch_common_flip` |A flip of a SEC_DIS mismatch is a SEC_DIS match, and `sep_efuse_token_fault_matrix_test` never holds a SEC_DIS match while an injection is active
|(SEC_DIS, Mismatch) in `x_ch_retry` |`sep_efuse_token_fault_matrix_test` holds only a SEC_DIS mismatch, and its retry presents the token of the opposite verdict, so the SEC_DIS retry gives a match
|(RMA_SIP channel, common-mode flip) in `x_ch_common_flip` |`sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test` drives the unanimous legal flip of the RMA_SIP comparator in both directions and grades the result, and `sep_efuse_token_fault_matrix_test` runs the flip on RMA_CHIPLET only
|Result code under a latch freeze in `x_ch_latch_freeze` |The specification names no test-enable signal and states no effect on a match in progress
|Corruption distance of the integrity-error byte |The bench injection forces both rails of the byte to 0, so only one corrupt value exists
|JTAG policy in TEST_DEV, PROD_END, RMA_CHIPLET, INVALID and before sense, and `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` over JTAG, in `sep_efuse_jtag_policy_cg` |The specification states the policy for PROD, RMA_SIP and the integrity error only
|(PROD, token word read) and (PROD, token word write) in `x_state_class_op` |The open test `sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test` grades the PROD token word write and readback, and `sep_efuse_jtag_access_rand_test` logs them without a grade
|SEC_DIS held over a cold reset with `feat_ctrl_i` all ones before sense, the recovery sense after a Mismatch and a cold reset, and the OKAY shadow-map read with `sec_dis_i` 1 and sense not done (no `cp_cold_reset_retained`, `cp_recovery` or `cp_map_read` coverpoint is declared) |`sep_sec_dis_override_test` grades these cells and Phase 1 `sep_sec_dis_boot_cg.cp_map_resp` records the map read; `sep_sec_dis_timing_rand_test` only logs them as controls
|Code of a denied JTAG response (SLVERR or DECERR) in `cp_resp` |The specification states only "an error response", and no owning test drives an address that could give another code
|INVALID state in `x_sigint_state_secdis_out` |Feature control is zero with and without the integrity logic
|`LOCKS` shadow write with every lock bit set |No new bit exists to write
|Frontdoor write to `LC_STATE`: gate bits, terminal states, lock exemption, refusal, no-burn cells |The register description gives `LC_STATE` access R and the field table gives it set-only writes. The two sources differ (VPLAN Blocked list), so no cell names a landing or a refusal
|Token window span `0x1093_0574` to `0x1093_05FF` |The response differs between two documents
|`LC_STATE` upper nibble and shadow reset value |The specification and the RDL differ
|Sense response status on words 0 to 9 (`LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE`, `LC_STATE`, `SBOOT_DIS`, `TRANSIENT_RMA_EN`, `SIP_DIS`, `SYS_DIS`) and word 255 in `cp_sense_status_word` |The specification does not state what a status on these words does to lock, lifecycle and disable state, or whether the strobe of the last word finishes sensing
|Program request outcome when the program timeout fires in `x_prog_lifecycle` |The register description states no status, done or busy value for a program timeout
|Digest word and byte packing |The specification does not state how the digest value splits into shadow words and bytes, so no bin names a packing
|The 32 combinations of {SIP_DBG, CHIPLET_DBG, SEP_DBG, SEP_FUSE_DBG, SMC_FUSE_DBG} at the fuse DFT ports in TEST_DEV with DEMOTE 0/0 (no `cp_combo5` coverpoint is declared) |A leg E leaf of `sep_lcc_feat_ctrl_state_matrix_rand_test` walks six of the 32 combinations, so the 32 need about 50 regressions of 4 seeds to close. Every leg E leaf grades the gate formula on its drawn combinations (CHK-DTP-PORT), and the port crosses `x_sep_port` and `x_smc_port` stay graded as seed-dependent coverpoints
|(bit 2, other channel matched only) in `x_lc_prog_gate` |`sep_efuse_transient_rma_rand_test` runs this program only as a control (`role=control`), and `sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test` grades that refusal
|Copy-0 collapsed-pair cells of the three channels and the copy-0 dissent cell of RMA_SIP in `x_ch_kind_copy` |`sep_efuse_token_fault_matrix_test` does not draw them, and the open tests that it extends (`sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test`, `sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test`) grade them
|RMA_SIP cells of `x_ch_latch_freeze` |`sep_efuse_token_fault_matrix_test` runs the RMA_SIP freeze cell as a control, and the open test `sep_efuse_digest_latch_fault_test` grades it
|Zero value of `jtag_id_i` in `cp_jtag_id_class` |With the ID at 0, two of its three 32-bit forms (`0x0` and `0x1`) can equal staged words, so CHK-JTAGID cannot grade the absence of the ID; `sep_efuse_field_layout_rand_test` drives a non-zero ID in every leaf
|prog_wlocked in `x_case_cmd` |`sep_efuse_interface_status_rand_test` grades only the flag of the lock-blocked program, and `sep_efuse_lock_slot_matrix_rand_test` grades the absent program command of a write-locked program (CHK-LOCK-CELL)
|prog_data0 in `x_case_cmd` |The register description states that a program with data 0 is ignored and not whether the guard drops its command; `sep_efuse_interface_status_rand_test` grades the unchanged bank image (CHK-IF-CMD)
|Multi-chiplet reachability, key-derivation tuple, bypass inventory |See <<sep-fcov-p2-dropped,Dropped groups>>
|===

[[sep-fcov-p2-key-manager]]
==== Key Manager groups

The groups below belong to the Phase 2 scope of <<sep-fcov-planned>>. Each group is a VCS
covergroup in the sampler module `sep_fcov` (`hw/sys/sep/dv/cov/sv/sep_fcov.sv`, `ifndef VERILATOR`).
Verilator compiles no covergroup. No cover property is used.

The group names and the owning tests are the contract. The owning tests are the Key Manager tests of
`SEP_VPLAN.adoc`.

===== Rules for every point

* A point is a coverpoint or a cross. The tool of every point is VCS.
* A point samples DUT nets or the DUT bus. It never reads log text or a scoreboard count.
* A bin follows the specification or the RDL. A fixed value comes from the text cited with the group.
* An `illegal_bins` entry is a value that the specification forbids. An `ignore_bins` entry is a cell that the specification makes impossible.
* A hit of a bin shows that the scenario happened. The test checker shows that the DUT behaved correctly. The two are separate.
* A bin that no owning test drives, that the design cannot reach, or whose value the specification does not state, has no bin in a point. It is listed in <<sep-fcov-km-unreachable>> with its reason.
* The SEP host clock and the Key Manager clock are one net, so every point has one clock.
* The class of a point is `executable`, `executable in part` or `parked` (<<sep-fcov-parked>>). A bin whose stimulus needs the PROPOSED drive `HK-STRM-PAT` is blocked: the point reads `executable in part. Blocked: <bins> need <item>`, or `parked: <item>` when it has no executable bin. The added hooks (the error injects and the top-port drive `HK-LAT`) and the preloads (`HK-IMG-PARAM`, `HOST-UNREC`, `HOST-BOOTWIPE`) block no bin. <<sep-vplan-km-proposed>> in the VPLAN lists the items. A parked point and a blocked bin are not in an executable total.
* A sampled net that is not an approved item is an added tap: a read-only reference that the owning test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. It drives nothing and blocks no bin. <<sep-fcov-km-signals>> lists the class of every sampled net.
* A point whose class ends in `seed-dependent` records cells that its owner draws from the seed of the leaf, so one regression can leave a cell empty. The point is graded on a merge of regressions, as <<sep-fcov-rand>> states for the `_rand_cg` groups, and no single regression is claimed to fill it. The bins text of the point names the cells that run on every seed. A point without the mark is filled by cells that its owners run on every seed.

[[sep-fcov-km-signals]]
===== Sampled signals

Names in the `Sampled signals` column of every table below are the names in this section and in <<sep-fcov-p2-unresolved-key-manager,Unresolved signal paths>>.
The Key Manager root of every `km_*` hierarchical name is `SEP_CORE.u_sep_crypto.u_key_manager_s3c_scan`.
`SEP_CORE` is `u_dut.u_sep` in `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv`.

====== Signals of the testbench table

The table lists the nets that the testbench has or that a testbench probe covers. These entries are added taps, wholly or in part: `km_rom_rsp`, `efuse_req_err`, the `rdata` and `rparity` fields of `km_sram_mem_if` and the `addr` field of `km_rom_req`. They are not in the unresolved list, they belong to the VPLAN item `KM-COV`, and they block no bin.

[cols="2,5,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Name |Signal |Source |Class
|`host_mbx_bus` |Host LSU AXI monitor: `SEP_READ_DATA`, `SEP_WRITE_DATA`, `SEP_WRITE_SEPARATOR`, `SEP_STATUS`, `SEP_IRQ_STATUS`, `SEP_CTRL`, `SW_RESET_N`, `KM_WIPE_CTRL` |`hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` |approved
|`pic_km_mbx_irq` |PIC vector bit 14, Key Manager mailbox interrupt |`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep.sv` |approved (`sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`)
|`pic_km_unrec_err` |PIC vector bit 30, `km_unrecoverable_err` (the SEP output `km_unrecoverable_err_o`) |`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep.sv` |approved (`sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`)
|`pic_km_rec_err` |PIC vector bit 31, `km_recoverable_err` (PIC source 32 in `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`) |`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep.sv` |approved (`sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`)
|`km_entropy_stream` |`km_entropy_tvalid_o`, `km_entropy_tdata_o`, `km_entropy_tready_o` |`hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` |approved
|`km_isolation_probe` |`hmac_km_isolated_probe_o`, `kmac_km_isolated_probe_o`, `abr_km_isolated_probe_o` |`hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` |approved
|`km_mem_req_count` |`km_rom_req_count_o`, `km_sram_req_count_o`, `km_sram_write_count_o` |`hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` |approved
|`secure_tm` |`secure_tm_o`, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` |`hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` |approved
|`km_sram_probe` |`km_sram_probe_o`, words 0 to 97 only. It is not the 8192-word array |`hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` |approved
|`km_sram_mem_if` |`u_dut.km_sram_mem_req` and `u_dut.km_sram_mem_rsp` (`req`, `we`, `addr`, `wdata`, `gnt`, `rvalid`, `rdata`, `rparity`) |`hw/top/sep_wrapper.sv`, `hw/ip/key_manager/rtl/km_intf_pkg.sv` |approved for `req`, `we`, `addr`, `wdata`, `gnt` and `rvalid`, which `tb_top.sv` reads; added tap for `rdata` and `rparity`
|`km_rom_req` |`u_dut.km_rom_mem_req.req`. The ROM request has `req` and `addr` only, with no write field |`hw/ip/key_manager/rtl/km_intf_pkg.sv` |approved for `req`, which `tb_top.sv` reads; added tap for `addr`
|`km_rom_rsp` |`u_dut.km_rom_mem_rsp` (`gnt`, `rvalid`, `rdata`, `parity`) |`hw/top/sep_wrapper.sv` |added tap
|`efuse_req_err` |eFuse controller `efuse_req_err` |`hw/ip/efuse/rtl/efuse_interface_controller.sv` |added tap
|`test_en_strap` |Secure test strap `test_en_strap` to `secure_tm_req_i` |`hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` |approved (`test_en_strap_i` input)
|`hk_kpv_arm` |The logical KPV word index and the bit that the error inject `HK-KPV` arms, read as stimulus |the `HK-KPV` inject of `sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test` |added hook (error inject); it blocks no bin
|===

====== Signals in the unresolved list

Every name in <<sep-fcov-p2-unresolved-key-manager,Unresolved signal paths>> is an added tap, and it blocks no bin. The names map to the observation taps of the VPLAN (<<sep-vplan-km-proposed>>) as follows. `km_kpv_ctrl` and `km_kpv_words` are `KM-KPV`. `engine_key_valid` and `abr_shared_key` are `KM-VALID`. `km_irq_status`, `km_irq_enable`, `km_irq_aggregate`, `km_cold_boot_done`, `km_recoverable_err`, `km_scrambler_cfg`, `km_sram_exec_mode`, `km_otp_read_lock` and `km_otp_change_status` are `KM-CSR`. `km_sram_array` is `KM-SRAM`. `engine_key_bus` and `km_efuse_master` are `KM-BUS`. `km_cpu_mem_bus` is `KM-FETCH`. `km_sram_latency` is `KM-LAT`. `km_reset_nets` is `KM-RST`. The registers and fields that a mapped name has beyond the check use of its item are listed in the item definition of <<sep-vplan-km-proposed>> and serve the coverage points only. The other names are `KM-COV`: sampler-side nets of the Key Manager that no test check uses.

====== Decoders in the sampler (no new net)

[cols="2,6",options="header"]
|===
|Name |Function
|`rsp_decode` |Unarmed decoder of every outbound frame on `SEP_READ_DATA` with its own word index. It records response ID, payload length, sequence, return code, return argument and fault code. It does not need a command to arm it, so it sees `RESP_KM_READY`, `RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY` and unsolicited fault frames. See `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, "Response Message Reference".
|`cmd_decode` |Decoder of the command frame on `SEP_WRITE_DATA`: sent sequence number, command ID, payload length, wire word count and separator position.
|`seq_model` |Model of the expected sequence number, built from the responses. It advances on a rejection after the header passes. It resets on a flush, a receive overflow and a warm reset. See `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, "Command Message Reference".
|`crc_check` |CRC-8/ROHC and CRC-32C functions over the words that the host sent. They classify a frame as header-CRC-bad or payload-CRC-bad. See `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, "CRC Function Library".
|`plen_table` |Table of the fixed payload length of each command: IDs 0x00 to 0x04, 0x10 and 0x12 take 0 words. IDs 0x11, 0x23, 0x25, 0x27 and 0x28 take 1 word. IDs 0x22 and 0x24 take 2 words. ID 0x26 takes KEY_SIZE+3 words.
|`handle_table` |Table of the echoed size and the handle per handle value, built from the `RESP_CMD` fields.
|===


[[sep-fcov-km-phase1-hits]]
===== Phase 1 groups that Key Manager tests also hit

A Key Manager test fills an existing Phase 1 bin as a side effect. No Phase 1 denominator changes.

[cols="3,2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Group |Bin hit |Key Manager tests
|`sep_km_generate_cg` |`cp_generate` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` (positive twin), `sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test`
|`sep_km_command_sideload_cg` |`cp_dest` |`sep_km_key_transfer_rand_test`, `sep_km_abr_shared_key_flow_rand_test`
|`sep_km_host_cmd_cg` |`cp_cmd` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test`, `sep_km_boot_reset_rand_test`, `sep_km_handover_rand_test`
|`sep_km_wipe_cg` |`cp_wipe` |`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test`, `sep_km_kpv_rand_test`
|`sep_km_sw_reset_cg` |`cp_rel` |`sep_km_boot_reset_rand_test`, `sep_km_handover_rand_test`, `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test`
|`sep_mailbox_pic_cg` |`cp_km_raise`, `cp_km_clear` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test`
|===

The Phase 1 frame tracker follows the host frame stream. A test that streams handover image words restarts the tracker with a `SW_RESET_N` write that holds the Key Manager in reset.
The image report words of `sep_km_handover_rand_test` do not carry 0x00 in bits [15:8] of a first word. Otherwise the tracker reads the word as a `RESP_CMD` header.

[[sep-fcov-km-frame-validation]]
===== `sep_km_frame_validation_cg`

*Tool*: VCS. *Class*: executable (<<sep-fcov-parked>>).

*Specification*: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, "Command Message Reference", "0x00 - RESP_CMD", "Message Buffers", "Incoming Message Handler".

[cols="2,6,2,3,2",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins, crosses, illegal and ignore bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Class
|`cp_fv_first_fail` |The first check to fail, one bin per ordered check: header CRC (-2), sequence (-3), command ID (-4), length (-5), payload CRC (-6), admission gate (-1 with RECOVERABLE_ERR set), handler reached. 7 bins. Cross with the return code. An `illegal_bins` entry is any return code that does not match the first failing check. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `cmd_decode`, `seq_model`, `km_recoverable_err` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_fv_pair_order` |Two faults in one frame. Ordered pairs (earlier, later) of {header CRC, sequence, command ID, frame length, payload CRC}: 10 bins. Pairs with the dispatch length check, which follows the payload CRC check: (header CRC, dispatch length), (sequence, dispatch length), (payload CRC, dispatch length): 3 bins. 13 bins in all. A bin is hit when the frame has both faults and the reply code is the earlier one. `illegal_bins`: the reply code is the later one. The pairs (command ID, dispatch length) and (frame length, dispatch length) have no valid frame and are not bins. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `cmd_decode`, `seq_model`, `crc_check` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_fv_seq` |Sent sequence against the expected one: equal, plus one (skip), minus one (replay), 0xFF to 0x00 rollover, other mismatch. The mismatch bins cross with return code -3. |`host_mbx_bus`, `cmd_decode`, `seq_model` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_km_seq_wrap` |Command counter at 255 then 0, response counter at 255 then 0. 2 bins. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `cmd_decode` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_fv_counter_reset` |Cause of the flush: SEP flush, receive overflow, warm reset. Each crossed with {first response sequence 0, first accepted command sequence 0}. 6 bins. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `cmd_decode`, `km_reset_nets` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_fv_invalid_id_gap` |Command ID in a gap: 0x05 to 0x0F, 0x13 to 0x21, 0x29 to 0xFF. Crossed with return code -4. 3 bins. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `cmd_decode` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_fv_plen` |PAYLOAD_LEN class against the length the command requires (`plen_table`): 0, 1, exact, one too few, one too many, 255. Crossed with {accepted by the length check, return code -5}. `ignore_bins`: cells with no command of that length. |`host_mbx_bus`, `cmd_decode`, `plen_table` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_fv_arg_kind` |Return argument of the rejection: -5 argument equals the received length for a self-consistent frame whose PAYLOAD_LEN differs from the length that the command requires (the argument for a frame whose PAYLOAD_LEN contradicts the wire length is not stated and is not a bin), -3 argument equals the expected sequence, -2 and -6 argument differs from the received CRC when the received CRC is wrong. 4 bins. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `cmd_decode`, `seq_model`, `crc_check` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_fv_rx_buffer` |A frame of 257 words with the separator on the last word is accepted. 257 words without a separator raise rx_buff_oflow. 2 bins. The bin follows the firmware text: a full buffer of 257 words without a separator. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `cmd_decode` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_fv_reserved_bits` |Reserved bit set, by field: CMD_KEY_GENERATE REQ_SIZE[31:7], CMD_KEY_GENERATE DEST_VALID[31:8], CMD_KEY_LOAD KEY_SIZE[31:7], CMD_KEY_LOAD DEST_VALID[31:8], CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER DEST_VALID[31:8]. Each crossed with return code -7 and the stated argument word index. 5 bins. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `cmd_decode` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable

|Coverpoint |Bins, crosses, illegal and ignore bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Class
|`cp_gate` |Command class {5 admitted, 10 gated} by RECOVERABLE_ERR {0, 1}. 4 cells. `ignore_bins`: CMD_SRAM_VER (it fails in both states). |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `cmd_decode`, `km_recoverable_err` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_gate_cmd` |Each of the 9 gated command IDs (0x10, 0x11, 0x12, 0x22, 0x23, 0x24, 0x25, 0x26, 0x28) with the gate set, in a frame that passes header CRC, sequence, command ID, frame length and payload CRC (classified by `crc_check`, `seq_model` and `cmd_decode`). The frame validation runs before the gate (`firmware.adoc`, "Error Recovery"), so a frame that fails one of those checks is not sampled here; `cp_fv_first_fail` owns the order. `illegal_bins`: a return code other than -1 for such a frame with the gate set. 9 bins. CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER (0x27) is not a bin: with no shared key pending it returns -1 in both gate states, so it is a control of the owner and no check can show the gate. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `cmd_decode`, `seq_model`, `crc_check`, `km_recoverable_err` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_key_extent` |Key length in words: 1, 16, 17, 128 accepted. Read from the echoed size of CMD_KEY_LOAD and CMD_KEY_GENERATE. 4 bins. Length 0 and 129 are not host reachable. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `km_kpv_ctrl` |`sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_capacity` |Failure with no argument and: no free slot (64 live 16-word keys), free slots but handle 255 already issued, fewer consecutive free slots than the span needs (one free slot and a 2-slot request, or free slots that are not adjacent). 3 bins. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `handle_table`, `km_kpv_ctrl` |`sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_load_arg_order` |The five CMD_KEY_LOAD checks, each as the first failure: PAYLOAD_LEN below 3 (argument 0), KEY_SIZE reserved bits (0), PAYLOAD_LEN not KEY_SIZE+3 (0), DEST_VALID reserved bits (1), DEST_VALID zero (1). Crossed with return code -7. 5 bins. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `cmd_decode` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_transfer_policy` |Transfer mask against the stored policy: subset (allowed), mixed (some in, some out). The mixed refusal crosses with "no destination key_valid rose". 2 bins. The fully-outside mask is graded by `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`, and the owning test runs it as a control, so it is not a bin. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `cmd_decode`, `engine_key_valid` |`sep_km_key_transfer_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_transfer_integrity` |The key CRC fails on an `HK-KPV` read-data fault before the policy check: fault frame (-1) then RESP_CMD failure, RECOVERABLE_ERR set, no engine key_valid rise. Crossed with the word class of the logical index that `HK-KPV` arms, against the slot run on `km_kpv_ctrl` {first used, interior, last used, residue after the key end}. The residue class must give success. 4 bins. The residue bin runs on every seed. The owner flips one used-word class in each leaf, so the first-used, interior and last-used bins fill on a merge of regressions. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `km_recoverable_err`, `km_kpv_ctrl`, `hk_kpv_arm`, `engine_key_valid` |`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test` |executable; seed-dependent
|`cp_image_bounds` |FW_WORDS: zero (rejected with argument 0 and no image request), at or above 8192 (rejected, values 8192 and 0xFFFF on every seed and one drawn value), an accepted size. 3 bins. The accepted ceiling is not stated and is not binned. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `cmd_decode` |`sep_km_handover_rand_test` |executable
|===

[cols="2,6,2,3,2",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins, crosses, illegal and ignore bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Class
|`cp_kpv_slot_index` |Slot 0 to 63 on a completed key-word or CTRL access. 64 bins. Boundary slots 0 and 63 required. |`km_cpu_axi` |`sep_km_kpv_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_kpv_word_index` |Word 0 to 15 of a key access. 16 bins. Words 0 and 15 required. |`km_cpu_axi` |`sep_km_kpv_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_kpv_post_reset` |After a reset all 64 CTRL read 0. Cold and warm. 2 bins. |`km_kpv_ctrl`, `km_reset_nets` |`sep_km_kpv_rand_test`, `sep_km_boot_reset_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_kpv_ctrl_write` |Per field (lock_write, lock_use, seal, erase): write 1 sets, write 0 leaves, three identical writes are idempotent. 4 by 3 bins. |`km_cpu_axi`, `km_kpv_ctrl` |`sep_km_kpv_rand_test`, `sep_km_handover_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_kpv_lock_write` |Set by write 1; key write while clear lands; CTRL stays writable while set; cleared by a warm reset; cleared by a cold reset; held set while seal is set. 6 bins. |`km_kpv_ctrl`, `km_kpv_words`, `km_cpu_axi`, `km_reset_nets` |`sep_km_kpv_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_kpv_lock_use` |Set by write 1; read while clear returns data; set by hardware when an erase of a sealed slot completes; cleared by warm reset; independent of lock_write. 5 bins. |`km_kpv_ctrl`, `km_cpu_axi`, `km_reset_nets` |`sep_km_kpv_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_kpv_erase` |Erase bit reads 1 while in flight and self-clears. From an unsealed slot CTRL clears and the slot takes new data. From a sealed slot the slot is retired (CTRL 0xB). Erase is never blocked by lock_write, lock_use or seal. 4 bins. |`km_kpv_ctrl`, `km_kpv_words`, `km_cpu_axi` |`sep_km_kpv_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_kpv_seal` |One CTRL write of seal gives seal and lock_write 1. Key read on a sealed slot succeeds. Write 0 to seal has no effect. Seal cleared by a warm reset. Seal and erase in back-to-back writes retires the slot when the erase is still in flight at the seal write; this bin samples only a try in which the CTRL read right after the seal write shows the erase bit at 1, and a `finished` try does not score. 5 bins. |`km_kpv_ctrl`, `km_cpu_axi`, `km_reset_nets` |`sep_km_kpv_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_kpv_scramble_mode` |KPV_SCRAMBLER_CTRL.enable 0 and 1. With enable 1 two writes of different data to one cell change exactly one stored word, and the inverse-cipher difference of the two stored words equals the data difference. Enable and lock kept by a warm reset. Cleared by a cold reset and by a wipe. 5 bins. |`km_kpv_scrambler`, `km_kpv_words`, `km_cpu_axi` |`sep_km_kpv_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_kpv_extent` |Key length 1, 15, 16, 17, 128 words gives a CTRL run of 1, 1, 1, 2, 8 slots. Final-slot residue 0 and non-zero. 7 bins. |`km_kpv_ctrl` |`sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_kpv_trailing` |After an erase, a partial write keeps the unwritten words. With lock_use set the same words read 0. 2 bins. |`km_kpv_words`, `km_cpu_axi` |`sep_km_kpv_rand_test` |executable
|===

Each crossed with {alone, with a lower-ranked source set}; (rom_access, with a lower-ranked source set) has no lower source and is an `ignore_bins`. 20 bins. (wipe_state, alone) is not a bin: the real-wipe point reports code -1, which `sep_km_wipe_reset_test` grades, so it is a control of the owner. (drbg_err, with a lower-ranked source set) runs on every seed. The owner draws 3 of the 8 cells in each leaf, so the point fills on a merge of regressions. |`km_rom_rsp`, `km_sram_mem_if`, `km_err_flags`, `km_irq_status` |`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test` |executable; seed-dependent
|`cp_if_rom_write` |ROM write after the lockout raises rom_write_err and not rom_access_violation. 1 bin. The write before the lockout is graded by `sep_km_rom_write_err_test`. |`km_cpu_mem_bus`, `km_err_flags`, `km_irq_status` |`sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test` |executable
|===

[[sep-fcov-km-exec-protection]]

*Tool*: VCS. *Class*: executable (<<sep-fcov-parked>>).

*Specification*: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, "Key Manager Control and Status Registers (KMCSR)"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_csr.adoc`, "IRQ_STATUS", "SRAM_EXEC_MODE".

|`cp_ex_mode_x_target` |SRAM_EXEC_MODE.enable {0, 1} by fetch target {ROM, write-locked SRAM, unlocked SRAM, peripheral window, address 0x10000000}. 10 cells. Outcome: permitted, EXEC_VIOLATION, or ROM_ACCESS_VIOLATION. `ignore_bins`: enable 0 with ROM_ACCESS_VIOLATION. The ROM and SRAM cells run on every seed. The owner draws the enable of the peripheral-window fetch and of the fetch from 0x10000000 in each leaf, so those four cells fill on a merge of regressions. |`km_cpu_mem_bus`, `km_sram_exec_mode`, `km_sram_lock`, `km_err_flags` |`sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test` |executable; seed-dependent

|`cp_ex_lockout_x_access` |Lockout state {enable 0, enable 1 not engaged, engaged} by access {ROM fetch, ROM data read, ROM write}. 7 cells. Case C0 gives the enable-0 data read. |`cp_ex_lockout_order` |IRQ_STATUS.rom_access_violation rises in an earlier cycle than the CPU trap. 1 bin. |`km_irq_status`, `km_cpu_trap` |`sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_ex_lockout_irreversible` |After the lockout engages, a write of 0 to the enable leaves it 1. Only a reset clears it. 2 bins. |`km_cpu_axi`, `km_sram_exec_mode`, `km_reset_nets` |`sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_ex_handover_entry` |After the jump the first instruction fetch is at 0x8000. The enable is the last write before the jump. 2 bins. |`km_cpu_mem_bus`, `km_sram_exec_mode`, `km_cpu_axi` |`sep_km_handover_rand_test` |executable

[[sep-fcov-km-drbg-sampler]]
===== `sep_km_drbg_sampler_cg`

*Tool*: VCS. *Class*: executable in part (<<sep-fcov-parked>>). Bins whose stimulus needs the PROPOSED drive `HK-STRM-PAT`: the delayed-arrival bins of `cp_smp_tvalid_arrival` and the `TSTRB` values other than 0xF of `cp_smp_strb`.

*Specification*: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, "DRBG Sampler", "Reads, prefetch and register access", "Errors and reset"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_drbg_sampler.adoc`, "DRBG Sampler", "CFG", "STATUS", "PREFETCH_DATA".

[cols="2,6,2,3,2",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins, crosses, illegal and ignore bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Class
|`cp_smp_read_service` |A prefetched word returns at once. With no prefetched word the sampler raises TREADY and waits. 2 bins. The timing of the response of a waiting read is graded by `sep_km_drbg_sampler_timeout_reset_test` (the exact cycle count), is logged by the owner and is not a bin. |`km_drbg_sampler`, `km_entropy_stream`, `km_cpu_axi` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_smp_tvalid_arrival` |TVALID already high at the read start. TVALID delayed by 1, 2, or 3 or more cycles. 4 bins. |`km_entropy_stream`, `km_drbg_sampler` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable in part. Blocked: the delayed-arrival bins need the PROPOSED drive `HK-STRM-PAT`
|`cp_smp_strb` |TSTRB of a beat: each non-zero value 0x1 to 0xF. 15 bins. `ignore_bins`: 0x0 (not stated). |`km_entropy_stream`, `km_drbg_sampler` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable in part. Blocked: every TSTRB value other than 0xF needs the PROPOSED drive `HK-STRM-PAT` (the 0x1 bin fills from the partial beat of CHK-SMP-TO-ASM, which needs it)
|`cp_smp_freshness` |Two successive DATA reads each take a new stream word: a handshake on `km_entropy_stream` falls between them. 1 bin. |`km_cpu_axi`, `km_entropy_stream`, `km_drbg_sampler` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_smp_prefetch_mode` |PREFETCH 0 and 1. A word held after a read. PREFETCHED set. Disabling prefetch with a word held clears it. A stalled prefetch does not time out. 5 bins. |`km_drbg_sampler` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_smp_prefetch_mirror` |PREFETCH_DATA equals the word the next DATA read returns. Repeated reads return one value. Cleared when prefetch is disabled. 3 bins. |`km_drbg_sampler`, `km_cpu_axi` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_smp_count_good` |COUNT_GOOD 0, 1, 0xFFFF, one more read at saturation without a wrap. Prefetched words are not counted. A non-zero write clears. A write of 0 keeps. 7 bins. The owner runs the saturation loop only in a leaf whose seed draws it (probability 1/3), so the 0xFFFF bin, the read at saturation and the clear from saturation fill on a merge of regressions; the other bins run on every seed. |`km_drbg_sampler` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable; seed-dependent
|`cp_smp_count_bad` |COUNT_BAD 0, 1, 0xFF, one more failure at saturation without a wrap. A non-zero write clears. A write of 0 keeps. 6 bins. |`km_drbg_sampler` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_smp_reset_defaults` |After a cold reset and after a warm reset: CFG.timeout 256 and CFG.prefetch 0. 2 bins. The counters and the STATUS bits after a reset are graded by `sep_km_drbg_sampler_timeout_reset_test`, are controls of the owners and are not part of the bin. |`km_drbg_sampler`, `km_reset_nets` |`sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test`, `sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_smp_ready` |STATUS.DRBG_READY reads 0 with a stalled stream and 1 with a valid stream. 2 bins. |`km_drbg_sampler`, `km_entropy_stream` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable
|===

|===
|Coverpoint |Bins, crosses, illegal and ignore bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Class
|`cp_df_timeout_value` |CFG.timeout of a stalled read: 1 and 65535. 2 bins. The values 0 (disabled) and 256 are graded by `sep_km_drbg_sampler_timeout_reset_test`, are controls of the owner and are not bins. |`km_drbg_sampler`, `km_entropy_stream` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_df_entry_state` |Timeout from ST_REQUEST. Timeout from ST_BYTE_ASSEMBLY after a partial beat. 2 bins. |`km_drbg_sampler` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable in part. Blocked: the ST_BYTE_ASSEMBLY bin needs the PROPOSED drive `HK-STRM-PAT` (CHK-SMP-TO-ASM)
|`cp_df_timeout_outcome` |The read returns SLVERR with data 0. TIMEOUT_ERR set. COUNT_BAD plus 1. IRQ_STATUS.drbg_err set. 4 bins. |`km_drbg_sampler`, `km_irq_status`, `km_cpu_axi` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_df_stream_violation` |TVALID drops while TREADY is low: with no read active, with an active read whose first active cycle has TREADY low. Each sets STREAM_ERR and raises IRQ_STATUS.drbg_err. The first bin also needs COUNT_BAD + 1. The second bin also needs SLVERR with data 0 and COUNT_BAD + 1 or more (the exact delta and TIMEOUT_ERR are not stated for that read and are not part of the bin). 2 bins. |`km_entropy_stream`, `km_drbg_sampler`, `km_irq_status` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_df_offset_race` |The withdrawal on the first active cycle of a read with TIMEOUT 1 (withdrawal and expiry in one cycle) and with TIMEOUT 2 (withdrawal before the expiry). Each sets STREAM_ERR. 2 bins. A TVALID pulse inside the active window with TREADY high is a handshake and is a control, not a bin. |`km_entropy_stream`, `km_drbg_sampler` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_df_status_clear` |TIMEOUT_ERR and STREAM_ERR clear by write 1 only, one at a time. 4 bins. |`km_drbg_sampler`, `km_cpu_axi` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_df_trng_reset` |The TRNG reset while the KM is held in software reset: the first DATA read after it completes with SLVERR clear and STREAM_ERR 0. 1 bin. |`km_reset_nets`, `km_drbg_sampler`, `host_mbx_bus` |`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` |executable

|`cp_fr_unrec_code` |One bin per unrecoverable code that a Phase 3 test raises and grades: -2, -3, -4, -5, -6, -7, -8, -13, -14, -17, -18. 11 bins. The codes -8 and -14 run on every seed. The codes -2 to -7, -13, -17 and -18 come from the fatal points that the owner draws in each leaf, so they fill on a merge of regressions. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode` |`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test`, `sep_km_handover_rand_test` |executable; seed-dependent

Code -7 differs from -8 in the same set. 3 bins. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode` |`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test`, `sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test`, `sep_km_handover_rand_test` (the 0xFF frame, CHK-FWCRC) |executable
|`cp_fr_flow_order` |Order of events: sideload key_valid cleared, mailbox flushed, report visible, SRAM write burst, km_unrecoverable_err_o high. 4 ordered bins. |`engine_key_valid`, `abr_shared_key`, `km_mbx_fifo`, `host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `km_mem_req_count`, `pic_km_unrec_err` |`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test` |executable (the sideload-clear and SRAM-burst bins fill in the HOST-UNREC entry of the owner)
|`cp_fr_wipe_predicate` |Predicate on (HOST-UNREC leaf): both shreds happen. Predicate off (HOST leaf, the bench build): the sideload keys stay, and the report and the halt still happen. 2 bins. The absence of the SRAM shred with the predicate off has no stated bound and is not binned. |`engine_key_valid`, `km_mem_req_count`, `pic_km_unrec_err`, `host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode` |`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test` |executable (the predicate-on bin fills in the HOST-UNREC entry of the owner, the predicate-off bin in the HOST entry)
|`cp_fr_rec_code` |Recoverable codes -1, -2, -5. 3 bins. The codes -3 and -4 are in <<sep-fcov-km-unreachable>>. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode` |`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test`, `sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_fr_resp_pairing` |The fault frame and then a failure RESP_CMD (key_slot_crc). The fault frame alone (rx_buff_oflow, flushed_by_sep). 2 bins. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode` |`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test`, `sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_fr_recovery_handshake` |Cause {rx_buff_oflow, flushed_by_sep} by step in order: the fault frame with no paired RESP_CMD; the first response sequence 0; a gated command refused with failure; CMD_RECOV_ACK success with recov_fault 0 afterwards; the gated command accepted. 9 bins. (flushed_by_sep, CMD_RECOV_ACK success with recov_fault 0 afterwards) is graded by `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`, is a control of the owner and is not a bin. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `km_recoverable_err`, `pic_km_rec_err` |`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_fr_isr_disposition` |Handler by disposition. Halting: wipe_state, rom_parity, sram_parity, rom_write, sram_write_lock, axi_decerr, axi_slverr, drbg_err, otp_sigint, exec, rom_access (11). Informational: the ABR shared key (1). IRQ_STATUS stays set after a halt. 12 bins. The otp_change handler is dormant in the ROM and is in <<sep-fcov-km-unreachable>>. |`km_irq_status`, `pic_km_unrec_err`, `km_cpu_trap`, `host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode` |`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test`, `sep_km_abr_shared_key_flow_rand_test` |executable; seed-dependent: the wipe_state, drbg_err and informational bins run on every seed, and the other halting bins come from the fatal points that the owner draws in each leaf
|`cp_fr_err_out` |km_unrecoverable_err_o low in normal operation. High on a trap. Stays high until a reset. Cleared by a warm reset. Cleared by a cold reset. No command answered while high. 6 bins. |`pic_km_unrec_err`, `host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `km_reset_nets` |`sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test`, `sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test`, `sep_km_handover_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_km_trap_cause` |Trap entry: EBREAK with the interrupt masked, illegal instruction, misaligned access, EBREAK inside a handler, illegal instruction inside a handler, an undefined CRC accelerator encoding. 6 bins. The cause is classified from the fetched word (`km_cpu_mem_bus`) and `irq_active` (`km_cpu_irq_active`). |`km_cpu_trap`, `km_cpu_mem_bus`, `km_cpu_irq_active` |`sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test` |executable

|`cp_ho_fwsize` |The stored image size seen through CMD_SRAM_EXEC: refused after a cold boot, refused after a load whose stream stopped after the confirmation. 2 bins. The success after a warm reset with a stored image is graded by `sep_km_handover_test`, is a control of the owners and is not a bin. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `km_reset_nets` |`sep_km_boot_reset_rand_test`, `sep_km_handover_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_ho_image_state` |One RESP_CMD before the stream. No second RESP_CMD. A trailer mismatch gives the fault. 3 bins. |`host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `pic_km_unrec_err` |`sep_km_handover_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_ho_state_at_jump` |At the jump: KMCSR IRQ_ENABLE 0, KM_IRQ_ENABLE 0, IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR 0x10, IRQ_ENTRY_LOCK 0. 4 bins. |`km_irq_enable`, `km_mbx_regs`, `km_irq_entry`, `km_sram_exec_mode` |`sep_km_handover_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_ho_mailbox_flush` |Both FIFOs empty after the flush. Inbound residue is erased. The confirmation is read before the flush. The confirmation is still unread so the ROM waits. 4 bins. |`km_mbx_fifo`, `host_mbx_bus` |`sep_km_handover_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_ho_order` |The enable of SRAM_EXEC_MODE is the last write before the jump. The outbound FIFO is drained before the flush. 2 bins. |`km_cpu_axi`, `km_sram_exec_mode`, `km_mbx_fifo` |`sep_km_handover_rand_test` |executable
|===

[[sep-fcov-km-vault-wipe]]
===== `sep_km_vault_wipe_cg`

*Description*: The wipe effect, the wipe edge protocol and the handover shred. `sep_km_wipe_cg` has one bin.

*Tool*: VCS. *Class*: executable (<<sep-fcov-parked>>).

*Specification*: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, "Wipe State"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_kpv.adoc`, "KPV_SCRAMBLER_KEY", "KPV_SCRAMBLER_CTRL"; `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, "KM_WIPE_CTRL"; `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, "Error Recovery", "Interrupt Handlers", "Interrupt Priority".

[cols="2,6,2,3,2",options="header"]
|===
|Coverpoint |Bins, crosses, illegal and ignore bins |Sampled signals |Owning tests |Class
|`cp_vw_pre_contents` |Vault at the wipe: empty, keys in unsealed slots, keys in sealed slots, retired slots, KPV scrambler locked. 5 bins. |`km_kpv_ctrl`, `km_kpv_scrambler` |`sep_km_kpv_rand_test`, `sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test`, `sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_vw_effect` |The cycle after the wipe edge: all 1024 words 0, all 64 CTRL 0 with seal, KPV scrambler key and enable and lock 0. 3 bins. A wipe by the SEP bit and a wipe by a CPU trap. |`km_kpv_words`, `km_kpv_ctrl`, `km_kpv_scrambler`, `km_wipe` |`sep_km_kpv_rand_test`, `sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test`, `sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_vw_edge` |0 to 1 edge with the bit left high. 1 to 0 to 1 gives a second wipe. A write of 1 while high gives no second wipe. Level held high for many cycles. High for at least one KM clock. 5 bins. |`km_wipe`, `host_mbx_bus` |`sep_km_kpv_rand_test`, `sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_vw_isr_flow` |In the ROM flow: sideload banks purged before the report. SRAM shred after the report. IRQ_STATUS.wipe_state left set. 3 bins. The report code -1 is graded by `sep_km_wipe_reset_test`, is a control of the owner and is not a bin. |`engine_key_valid`, `host_mbx_bus`, `rsp_decode`, `km_mem_req_count`, `km_irq_status` |`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test` |executable (the sideload-purge and SRAM-shred bins fill in the HOST-UNREC entry of the owner)
|`cp_vw_handover_shred` |At the handover: 8 outbound banks with a key loaded before (8 cells); a bank that held no key (AES and HMAC only loaded in the second pass: 1 cell); the shared key with a delivery pending and with none pending (2 cells). 11 cells. |`engine_key_valid`, `abr_shared_key`, `engine_key_bus` |`sep_km_handover_rand_test` |executable
|`cp_vw_scope` |An SEP wipe does not halt the CPU. SRAM words stay. A mailbox word stays. An engine key_valid stays. 4 bins. |`pic_km_unrec_err`, `km_sram_array`, `km_mbx_fifo`, `engine_key_valid` |`sep_km_kpv_rand_test` |executable
|===

[[sep-fcov-km-unreachable]]
===== Bins not graded

These cells are not bins of any point. No stimulus of an owning test drives them, or the design cannot reach them, or the specification does not state their value. A group that has such a cell keeps its other bins.

* `IMPOSSIBLE`: the design cannot reach the cell, or no net and no specification observation exists.
* `UNSTATED`: the specification does not state the value, so no check can fail on it (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>).
* `OWNED-OPEN`: an open test grades the cell.
* `NO-STIMULUS`: the cell is defined and reachable, and no Phase 3 test drives it.

[cols="3,4,5,2",options="header"]
|===
|Group and point |Bin |Reason |Disposition
|`sep_km_kpv_slot_cg` `cp_kpv_erase` |The stored block after an erase differs from the block before it |The specification does not state the value of the erase fill. |UNSTATED
|`sep_km_kpv_slot_cg` `cp_kpv_scramble_mode` |With enable 1 the stored word differs from the written word; with enable 1 the physical index differs from the linear index |A one-round cipher can map a word to itself, and the address permutation perm10 is not tabulated. |UNSTATED
|`sep_km_sideload_delivery_cg` `cp_sd_write_order` |A share-0 word after a share-1 word; not all share-0 words before all share-1 words |A uniformly random shuffle admits every order, so no order property of one transfer is stated. |UNSTATED
|`sep_km_fault_report_cg` `cp_fr_unrec_code` |-9 illegal_insn, -10 bus_error, -11 ebreak |The production ROM holds none of these faults. The host cannot raise them. |IMPOSSIBLE
|`sep_km_fault_report_cg` `cp_fr_unrec_code` |-12 spurious_irq |Which CPU IRQ bits are unrecognised is not stated. |UNSTATED
|`sep_km_fault_report_cg` `cp_fr_unrec_code` |-15 fw_stack_ovf |The host cannot stream more words than the ROM reads. |IMPOSSIBLE
|`sep_km_fault_report_cg` `cp_fr_unrec_code` |-16 shred_range |Callers use compile-time bounded counts. |IMPOSSIBLE
|`sep_km_fault_report_cg` `cp_fr_rec_code` |-3 mbox_overflow, -4 mbox_underflow |The ROM never writes a full FIFO or reads an empty one. The cell needs an injected KM mailbox error, and no Phase 3 test injects one. |NO-STIMULUS
|`sep_km_fault_report_cg` `cp_fr_isr_disposition` |otp_change as an informational handler |The boot sequence does not enable the source, so the handler is dormant in the ROM. |IMPOSSIBLE
|`sep_km_fault_report_cg` |`shred_range` exception flow, emission-path bins (buffered, blocked) |Firmware-internal state, no net, no specification register. |IMPOSSIBLE
|`sep_km_cmd_reject_cg` |Load Key rollback at each of the store steps: a slot-write error destroys the registration and keeps the earlier writes |The specification states the result. It needs an injected slot-write failure, and no Phase 3 test injects one. |NO-STIMULUS
|`sep_km_cmd_reject_cg` |Transfer stop at the first driver failure with the earlier engine writes kept |The specification states the result. It needs an injected driver failure, and no Phase 3 test injects one. |NO-STIMULUS
|`sep_km_cmd_reject_cg` |Handle validity of CMD_KEY_REVOKE (null, never issued, revoked) and of any command for a null or never-issued handle |The return code is not stated. |UNSTATED
|`sep_km_cmd_reject_cg` `cp_key_extent` |len 0, len 129, "span past slot 63 rejected" |The command layer rejects KEY_SIZE above 127 first. The registry layer is not host reachable. |IMPOSSIBLE
|`sep_km_key_registry_cg` `cp_reg_eligibility` |Slots that are read-locked, write-locked outside a live key, sealed or retired |No ROM flow and no host command sets seal or lock_use before an allocation, and a warm reset clears the control bits. |IMPOSSIBLE
|`sep_km_key_registry_cg` |The DRBG-selected start slot of the search |The start slot is internal to the ROM and has no net. |IMPOSSIBLE
|`sep_km_kpv_locked_access_cg` `cp_kpv_retired` |skipped by allocation |A warm reset clears a retired slot, so the poke path and the ROM allocator never meet. |IMPOSSIBLE
|`sep_km_secret_nonexposure_cg` |Host surface by vault target matrix (16 cells) |The content of the 4 kB aperture is an open specification question. |UNSTATED
|`sep_km_integrity_fault_cg` `cp_if_parity` |corruption class (data bit versus parity bit) |A mismatch looks the same at the port. |IMPOSSIBLE
|`sep_km_integrity_fault_cg` `cp_if_otp_dual_rail` |Both words all zero, both words all ones |The hook flips one bit on one rail, so no draw makes both words uniform. |NO-STIMULUS
|`sep_km_otp_readout_cg` `cp_otp_change` |OTP_CHANGE_STATUS cleared by a warm reset |No step of the owning test leaves a change bit set before a KM warm reset: every change bit is cleared by W1C before the reset. |NO-STIMULUS
|`sep_km_abr_shared_key_cg` `cp_sk_notify` |delivery inside the sample-and-clear window, delivery just before wait-for-interrupt |Timing races with no deterministic stimulus. |NO-STIMULUS
|`sep_km_reset_domain_cg` |reset synchronizer structure, warm hold counter reset |No net that the specification states. |IMPOSSIBLE
|`sep_km_handover_cg` |the 13 handover step bins, the general register file at the jump |Firmware-internal, no net. The order point `cp_ho_order` and the first-fetch point `cp_ex_handover_entry` sample the observable events. The test checker grades x5 and the other registers. |IMPOSSIBLE
|`sep_km_memory_boot_cg` |the 19 boot step bins |Firmware-internal, no net. The point `cp_mem_boot_order` samples the observable order. |IMPOSSIBLE
|`sep_km_drbg_fault_cg` `cp_df_stream_violation` |TVALID drop during a prefetch |The entry cycle of a prefetch is not controlled, and the sampler keeps TREADY high while it requests. |NO-STIMULUS
|`sep_km_drbg_fault_cg` `cp_df_offset_race` |The withdrawal at offsets -2, -1, 0, +1 and +2 cycles around the expiry of a TIMEOUT 8 read |In the active states the sampler keeps TREADY high, so a TVALID drop inside the window is a handshake and not a withdrawal, and after the expiry the read has ended. The specification states no result for those cells. |UNSTATED
|`sep_km_drbg_fault_cg` `cp_df_stream_violation` |TVALID drop during byte assembly |The sampler keeps TREADY high in byte assembly, so a drop there is a completed handshake and not a withdrawal. |IMPOSSIBLE
|`sep_km_drbg_fault_cg` `cp_df_trng_reset` |A TRNG reset while the KM runs: idle, StRequest, StByteAssembly 1, 2, 3 bytes |The SEP host must keep the KM idle before a TRNG reset, and the hardware does not enforce it (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, "Resetting the TRNG"). A reset during a sample can give mixed bytes or a stream error, and the specification does not state which. |UNSTATED
|`sep_km_sideload_delivery_cg` `cp_sd_mask_shape` |ENGINE_SHRED with mask 0 |The specification states no outcome for it. |UNSTATED
|`sep_km_drbg_fault_cg` `cp_df_timeout_value` |With CFG.timeout 0 a stalled read blocks until the stream returns |The open test `sep_km_drbg_sampler_timeout_reset_test` drives the stalled read with CFG.TIMEOUT 0. The Phase 3 test drives T = 0 on the fed stream only. |OWNED-OPEN
|`sep_km_mailbox_fault_cg` `cp_mbf_cond`, `cp_mbf_resp_cfg`, `cp_mbf_sticky_clear` |SEP inbound overflow and SEP outbound underflow: the conditions, the SEP_CTRL response bits and their sticky bits |The open test `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test` drives these conditions and their response and sticky bits. |OWNED-OPEN
|`sep_km_mailbox_transport_cg` `cp_mbt_irq_src_x_en` |SEP overflow, underflow and flushed-by-other interrupt sources |The open test `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test` drives the other sources. |OWNED-OPEN
|`sep_km_frame_validation_cg` |CRC-8/ROHC vectors, response reserved fields |No stimulus of an owning test drives them. |NO-STIMULUS
|`sep_km_frame_validation_cg` `cp_fv_rx_buffer` |A partial frame that is later completed |No step of an owning test completes a partial frame after the buffer fills. |NO-STIMULUS
|`sep_km_mailbox_fault_cg` `cp_mbf_flush` |KM flush mid-frame, SEP flush mid-frame, SEP flush during a blocked response send |No owning test flushes in the middle of a frame, and the outcome of a SEP flush during a blocked response send is not stated. |UNSTATED
|`sep_km_mailbox_transport_cg` `cp_mbt_separator` |Arm twice without a write |The specification states no outcome for a second arm. |UNSTATED
|`sep_km_memory_boot_cg` `cp_km_sram_region`, `sep_km_integrity_fault_cg` `cp_if_sram_lock_violation` |Region 0 (the region bit and its two store cells) |Region 0 holds the POKE image data, so the owning test never locks or writes it. |NO-STIMULUS
|===

[[sep-fcov-p2-dropped]]
==== Groups with no owning test

A Phase 2 group is in <<sep-fcov-planned>> only when at least one Phase 3
test reaches a bin that the group defines. These groups have no owning test,
so they are not Phase 2 groups.

[cols="4,3,10",options="header"]
|===
|Group |Area |Reason
|`sep_crypto_bus_error_cg`
|<<sep-fcov-p2-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|No test names it. The AxLEN rule and the `SEP_ABR_EN` gating have no stimulus. The bridge-fault test of the fabric faults names `sep_fault_source_report_cg` and `sep_fault_source_ack_cg`. The fabric response matrix names `sep_crypto_bus_access_cg`. The ABR-absent build is a Verilator-only elaboration
|`sep_esrc_debug_exposure_cg`
|<<sep-fcov-p2-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The group is the `DEBUG_CTRL` selector and `signal_monitor_o`. The net has no SEP-level path. The open lock test grades the two `DEBUG_CTRL` fields. The observe FIFOs belong to `sep_esrc_conditioning_cg`, where the coverpoints `x_obs_capture`, `cp_obs_lane_sel`, `cp_obs_flush`, `cp_obs_overflow` and `cp_obs_lock` sit
|`sep_fabric_periph_err_status_cg`
|<<sep-fcov-p2-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|No Fabric and remap test hits it. The peripheral bridge fault kinds and the `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS` latch are sampled by `sep_fault_source_report_cg` and `sep_fault_source_ack_cg`, not by this group. The same-cycle fault and clear bin is sampled in the mailbox area through the observation tap `sep_cpu_ctrl_err_pulse_probe_o`, and no test of this bucket owns the bin.
|`sep_filter_entry_cfg_cg`
|<<sep-fcov-p2-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Its only coverpoint, `cp_group_id_no_effect`, has no check that can fail. The specification states that group ID 0 matches whatever the field holds, and every transaction of this bench carries group ID 0. The bank walk of the other entry fields belongs to the open walk tests.
|`sep_strap_config_cg`
|<<sep-fcov-p2-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|No test reaches a bin that the group defines. The group samples `sep_straps_i` and `SEP_CPU_CTRL.SEP_STRAPS`. The port list of `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` has no `sep_straps_i`, and `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc` has no `SEP_STRAPS` register. The strap latch is outside SEP. The one remaining bin that matches the topic, stability of `secure_tm_req_i` after a sample, is `cp_stm_strap_stability` in `sep_documented_absence_cg`
|`sep_feat_ctrl_export_cg`
|<<sep-fcov-p2-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|No Phase 3 test hits its content. The multi-chiplet reachability rules are out of scope for a one-chiplet bench. The key-derivation tuple has no test in this set. The bypass inventory has no owning test. The one part that the Phase 3 tests hit, the demotion export codeword, is a coverpoint of `sep_lc_demotion_cg` (`x_export_codeword`). The gate-bound check of `sep_lcc_feat_ctrl_state_matrix_rand_test` is graded by `x_row_group_class`
|===

The table has no group of these areas: <<sep-fcov-mbox-irq-pic-dropped,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>, <<sep-fcov-spi-dma-dropped,SPI and DMA>> and <<sep-fcov-p2-key-manager,Key Manager>>.

[[sep-fcov-p2-unresolved]]
==== Unresolved signal paths

A path in this section does not resolve in the testbench under the name that
the specification or the sampler uses, or has no sampler input. Each area states
the disposition of each path.

[[sep-fcov-p2-unresolved-crypto-entropy]]
===== Crypto and entropy

Every other sampled path of this area resolves in the tree. The paths below have no input at the
sampler. Each has one disposition. `Wire` uses a probe that exists in `tb_top` and is approved. `Added hook` is a top-port drive that the owning test adds to the port table of the HDL Top section of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, and it blocks no bin. `Added tap` is a read-only reference that the owning test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list, and it blocks no bin.

[cols="2,1,3",options="header"]
|===
|Taps |Disposition |Action
|`intr_vec` (bits not wired), `edn_req`, `pool_stream`, `pool_edn`, `edn_fips`, the `tdata` of `km_stream` and of `crypto_stream` (streams 0 and 1), `trng_rst`, `trng_iso`, `ip_rst` (AES, KMAC, OTBN), `esrc_decor`, `esrc_compress`, `esrc_noise` (lanes 1 to 11) |Wire |The `tb_top` probe exists. Wire it to a new sampler input. No DUT change
|`ext_stream`, `ext_irq` |Added hook |The integration wrapper ties `ext_trng_axis_req_o` and `ext_trng_irq_o` to zero. A driver at the `sep` boundary supplies the stimulus, and a read-only monitor observes the response. The owning test adds the driver to the port table of the HDL Top section of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`. No DUT change
|`eng_idle`, `alert_tx`, `drbg_int`, `ext_sel`, `pool_state`, the HMAC, AES, KMAC, OTBN, CSRNG and EDN internal nets, and the ESRC taps `esrc_reg_out`, `esrc_reg_in`, `esrc_sm`, `esrc_fail`, `esrc_fifo`, `esrc_biw_obs`, `esrc_noise_obs` and `esrc_detune` |Added tap |The signal is internal. A read-only hierarchical reference in `tb_top` taps it, and the owning test adds the tap to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. No force
|`pool_err`, `otbn_lc_escalate`, `aes_lc_escalate`, `esrc_signal_monitor`, `esrc_oscillator` |Hole |No stimulus can reach the bin. The coverpoints that name them keep the bins out
|===

Not coverable, and so not in any group: the Key Manager stream (stream 0)
with a consumer outside the external Key Manager leg of `sep_drbg_sink_mix_rand_test` (that leg runs Key Manager firmware and fills `cp_km_ext_stream`); the pool
`fill_stall` and `pool_error` value 1 (the stall threshold has no stated value
and the pool error port stays 0); `FIPS_LOCK` with `HEALTH_TEST_WINDOW_SIZE`
below 1024 (the RDL states a requirement and no response); the EDN auto mode
and numeric `CMD_STS` codes (the spec states no value); SHA3 with strength 128;
the FIPS provenance cells with tag 0 (the tag is 1 on every path); the ACVP row
inside a reason class of the ABR sigVer reject trial and the ACVP rows of the ABR
decapsulation reject trials (stimulus labels, not sampled values).

[[sep-fcov-p2-unresolved-fabric-remap]]
===== Fabric and remap

The paths below, named by the spec or the sampler, do not resolve under that name, or have no sampler input. Disposition "Added tap": a read-only reference that the owning test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list; it blocks no bin. The CPU DBG driver is an added hook: a top-port drive of the JTAG and DMI ports that `sep_debug_master_target_matrix_test` adds to the port table of the HDL Top section of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`.

[cols="2,4,3",options="header"]
|===
|Name used |Finding |Disposition
|`axi_extension_axi_req_o` |The port table names it. `sep_wrapper` has no such port. The wrapper net is `u_dut.axi_extension_axi_req`, response `u_dut.axi_extension_axi_resp`. |Added tap (PR-EXT): read-only reference to the wrapper net
|`sep_sram_req_o` |A port of `sep` only. `sep_wrapper` has the net `sep_sram_req`. |Added tap (PR-SRAM): read-only reference to the wrapper net
|`sep_boot_rom_req_o` |A port of `sep` only. `sep_wrapper` has the net `sep_boot_rom_req`. |Added tap (PR-ROM): read-only reference to the wrapper net
|`sep_cpu_tcm_req_o` |A port of `sep` and `sep_cpu`. `sep_wrapper` has the net `sep_cpu_tcm_req`. |Added tap (PR-TCM): read-only reference to the wrapper net
|CPU DBG initiator request tap and driver |No JTAG or DMI system-bus driver exists in the testbench: the DMI enables are tied low. |Added hook (top-port drive), with an added tap: the JTAG and DMI driver of `sep_debug_master_target_matrix_test`. The CPU DBG cells have no bin in this section
|`aperture` |The wrapper ports `sep_global_base_addr_o` and `sep_region_size_o` stay open in `tb_top`. The values are `u_dut.u_sep.u_sep_system_peripherals.{sep_global_base_addr,sep_region_size}`. |Added tap: read-only reference, or the two ports wired to the sampler
|`smu_aperture` |The two values `u_dut.u_sep.u_sep_system_peripherals.{smu_global_base_addr,smu_region_size}` have no wrapper port. |Added tap: read-only hierarchical reference
|`alias_out` |The input and output requests (address and AxCACHE) of `u_local_master_remap_wrap` in `sep_system_peripherals` have no port and no tap. |Added tap (PR-ALIAS): read-only reference
|`in_filter` |`inbound_filter_ctrl[i].{FILTER_CONFIG,START_ADDR,END_ADDR}`, i = 0..15, have no sampler input. A genvar-indexed hierarchical reference is not used: the reference is one explicit assign per index. |Added tap: read-only reference
|`out_filter` |`outbound_filter_ctrl[i].{FILTER_CONFIG,START_ADDR,END_ADDR}`, i = 0..31, have no sampler input. The same rule as `in_filter` holds. |Added tap: read-only reference
|`sep_debug` |`u_dut.u_sep.feat_ctrl.sep_debug`. A testbench output exists and is not wired to the sampler. |Added tap: wire the existing output to the sampler
|`dma_req_raw` |`u_dut.u_sep.u_sep_dma_wrap.dma_axi_req_raw`, the net before the DMA local alias remap. It has no port and no sampler input. |Added tap: read-only reference
|===

The initiator identity on a shared port has no SEP port: the ID most significant bits are internal. The initiator rule derives it from `lsu_req`, `dma_req` and `xbar_ext_req`, and no port is added.

[[sep-fcov-mbox-irq-pic-paths]]
===== Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

Every other sampled path of this area resolves in the tree. These do not, or
resolve in part. Every read-only reference into `PIC`, `CLAIM`, `TLU`, `MB[m]` and `FILT` is an added tap: an observation tap the owning test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list.

[cols="2,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Path |Why it does not resolve |Disposition
|Vector bits above 42 (`sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o[63:43]`) |The probe is 43 bits wide |Sample `extintsrc_req_gw[255:44]` of `PIC` and `meip` words 2 to 7 instead. Read-only reference; no testbench change
|`nmi_int` at the SEP boundary |The pin is not a boundary port; the wrapper ties it inside the SEP |Sample the synchronized bark (`BARK`) and `take_nmi` of `TLU` instead. Read-only reference
|Full ICCM fetch address (`dbg_iccm_addr_o`) |The tap is the row address of bank 0, [13:0] only. It does not show a fetch that uses another bank, or the full address |Sample the first retired PC after an NMI entry (`TRC`) instead. Read-only reference
|Crypto alert receiver pulses (`SEP_CORE.u_sep_crypto.crypto_alert_pulse[10:0]`) |An RTL net with no testbench probe |Added tap: the testbench output `crypto_alert_pulse_probe_o` (`logic [10:0]`), with a read-only hierarchical reference in `tb_top`. The alert rows of `sep_pic_alert_cg` sample it
|===

Permanent holes, with no path to fix: the driven values of `timer_int_i` and
`soft_int_i` (the testbench ties a constant).

Added hooks, which block no bin: the drive of `sep_ext_interrupts_i` (`EXT`,
tied to 0 in `tb_top`; which bit reaches which source is a specification
question, so only a count is graded), and the response control of the outbound
responder on `smn_outbound_axi_resp_i` (`RESP`), which the DMA host-path bins
and the outbound stall bins use.

[[sep-fcov-p2-unresolved-spi-dma]]
===== SPI and DMA

A path in this table does not resolve as the test text states it. The coverpoints
do not depend on these paths, except where the disposition says so.

[cols="2,3,2,3",options="header"]
|===
|Path as stated |Evidence |Disposition |Reason and effect
|Response lanes `spi_sd_i[3:0]` as a TB input (the responder drives all four lines) |`sep_tb_signal_list.svh` has one bit `spi_miso_i`. `tb_top.sv` drives `{2'b00, spi_miso_i, 1'b0}`: only `sd[1]` carries data |Permanent hole for the Rx bit content |The specification does not state which line carries the Rx bit. The tests grade the Rx word count at every speed and no Rx content. No coverage bin depends on it
|Beat fields (`AxLEN`, `AxSIZE`, ID, `AxADDR`, data) of the DMA at `sep_sram_req_o` |`sep.sv` and `sep_pkg.sv`: the struct has `req`, `addr`, `wdata`, `strb`, `atop`, `wenable`. It has no length, size or master identity, and the CPU shares this port |Added tap (`dma_axi_req_probe_o`). The sampler and the cocotb monitor read `u_dut.u_sep.dma_axi_req` |CHK-DMA-BEAT and CHK-DMA-ADDR of `sep_dma_transfer_matrix_rand_test` read the DMA master AXI request on the tap
|DMA `STATUS.DONE`, `CHUNK_DONE`, `ERROR`, `ABORTED`, `SHA2_DIGEST_VALID` as TB flat probes |`sep_tb_signal_list.svh` and `tb_top.sv` give `dma_busy_probe_o` only |Added tap (`dma_status_probe_o`) |CHK-DMA-CHUNKRISE and CHK-DMA-DONE-ORDER of `sep_dma_transfer_matrix_rand_test` read the tap to grade the `CHUNK_DONE` rise count and the order of `DONE` against the last B response
|Handshake inputs 1 to 10 as a stimulus |`sep.sv` ties `lsio_trigger[$bits(lsio_trigger)-1:1]` to 0. `dma.adoc`, OCAH Modifications, states that only input 0 is connected |Permanent hole |No stimulus raises them
|Clear evidence for the SPI CDC FIFOs and the shift register |`spi_controller.adoc`, `CONTROL.SW_RST`, names them. No register or specification-named signal shows them |Permanent hole |The clean command after the release shows the net effect and stays in the checker
|`spi_irq_i` as an externally driven input |`sep.sv` and `sep_io_pkg.sv`: the interrupt is the `irq` member of the output struct and feeds the PIC inside SEP |Permanent hole |No such input exists
|A zero-strobe write that reaches the SPI core (the path that raises `ERROR_STATUS.ACCESSINVAL`) |`sep_ot_spi_wrap.sv` completes such a write locally with OKAY. `spi.adoc`, Features, states the same |Permanent hole |The test checker reads `ERROR_STATUS.ACCESSINVAL` and `TXQD`. No coverpoint observes the TL-UL request

The paths below resolve and the specification is silent on their meaning.

|Item |State |Effect

[[sep-fcov-p2-unresolved-key-manager]]
===== Key Manager vault

Each row is a net that the testbench does not sample. The net exists in the RTL. The disposition states how the sampler gets it.

* `ADD-PORT`: the sampler needs a tap in `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` and in the `sep_fcov` port list. The net is outside the Key Manager, or a measurement is needed.
* `ACCEPT-PROBE`: the sampler reads the net by hierarchical reference from a bind module or a testbench helper. No testbench port is added. A `bind key_manager` precedent exists in `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv`.
* `Added tap`: a read-only reference that the owning test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. It drives nothing and blocks no bin.

Every signal of the table is an added tap (<<sep-fcov-parked>>). No signal is a PERMANENT-HOLE. A coverpoint that samples one of them is not blocked by it.
Where a bin needs a net that no specification observation backs, the bin is in <<sep-fcov-km-unreachable>>.
